**A23 — First screens: the 2026-10-04 redesign sweep (§2 L71 "Layout primitives"; §5.3 L199–L212;
§14 A21 clause 1 L738–L754, clause 2 L755–L765, clause 5 L820–L832, clause 6 (c) L866–L869, clause 7
L874–L884; A22 clause 1 L961–L979 and open item (iii) L1033–L1039; AC-12, AC-14, AC-15, AC-24,
AC-26, AC-27; spec 008 §2 L55–L59, §5.3 L180–L184, §5.4 L199, §14 A9, A11, A13 and A15; spec 007
§14 A11; spec 003 §14 A14 and A16; the audit `docs/design/audits/2026-10-04-site-sweep.md` (PR 185);
the round-2 design PR (to come); founder rulings 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05).**
Original: A21 clause 1 binds the v2 artboards as they stood on 2026-10-03. Those drew every hub,
index and listing as an H1 and a 5–9 line paragraph with the right half empty and no flower on the
first screen at 390 (audit §1 finding 1; §2, each listing entry's "vs artboard: matches"). §2 L71's
`Container` sets the content column 72 px inside the header (audit finding 4), and each page places
its own breadcrumb (finding 8).
Ruling: the founder asked for the sweep in chat on 2026-10-04: "i dont like the design.. of the
birthday, occasions, sympathy, bouqets ppages etc etc roses plants and destinations too... they look
bland at first glance or from top... only when we scroll down they look somewhat good... you aren't
being creative bro... also some things still have shoit alignment or old deisgns... fix that too..
also now do a full sweep of redesign pls." Shown the before and after images of PR 185 and told that
the new copy is the audit's Appendix A (A1–A11, L1–L6, B1, B2), the founder answered in chat on
2026-10-04: **"go, approve copy and also i asked to redesign all the pages not just a few. right?"**
"go" approves the redesign direction of PR 185. "approve copy" approves the Appendix A **English**
strings exactly as written (clause 6); the German and Polish line L2 is not approved. The last
sentence means more pages are coming in a second design round; this amendment covers only what the
sweep redrew.
Further rulings, 2026-10-05 (founder, in chat; decisions log, same date):
- **"browser language only".** The language popup's highlight uses the browser language alone.
  Spec 003 §14 A16 clause 2 is resolved to its drop variant: no IP or country hint, no `/api/geo`,
  no RoPA row.
- **"approve the wording just no em dash".** The neutral copy batch of clause 12 is approved.
- **Design round 2 picks** (as relayed by the orchestrator): the desktop home is option A, a
  full-bleed photograph with a white card; the phone home is option 1, a cover photograph with a
  sticky "Send flowers" bar; the product page is the approved two-column desktop plus the phone
  "sheet" (option 2) with a sticky price-and-button bar. The founder's bar for every page:
  "uncluttered, state of the art, grid-aligned, mobile designed on its own". Round 2 replaces
  several round-1 devices (stamps, postmarks, envelopes, dated stamps on hubs): clause 10.
- **Motion.** The founder asked for a loading screen and flower micro-animations. The design comes
  in `docs/design/system/motion.dc.html`; clause 11 binds its limits.

Corrected (founder rulings, written as binding by the spec-writer, 2026-10-04, and amended on
2026-10-05 for the rulings above):

1. **Design source.** Each page's **artboard in `docs/design/wireframes` (round 2, PR to come)**
   binds its look, at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844, together with the system group the round-2 PR adds
   to `system/components.dc.html`. Each artboard's annotation block binds the structure; the fold
   line marks the first screen. PR 185's round-1 artboards keep binding only what round 2 does not
   redraw, and none of their round-1 devices binds a build (clause 10). A21 clause 1 is otherwise
   unchanged: the artboards set the look; behaviour, data and states stay the owning spec's, and
   where an artboard draws what a spec forbids, the spec wins and the difference is recorded in
   `docs/design/README.md`.

   | Page type | Artboard | Behaviour owned by |
   |---|---|---|
   | Occasion hub | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/occasion-hub-*`) | spec 008 §2 row 13 |
   | Category hub | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/category-hub-*`) | spec 008 §2 row 10 |
   | Occasions index | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/occasions-index-*`) | spec 008 §2 row 14 |
   | Destinations hub | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/all-destinations-*`) | spec 007 §2 |
   | Country shop root | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/country-shop-*`) | spec 008 §2 row 6 |
   | Country category | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/country-category-*`) | spec 008 §2 row 7 |
   | Country occasion | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/country-occasion-*`) | spec 008 §2 row 8 |
   | Country guide, first screen | the page's round-2 artboard (`wireframes/corridor-country-*`) | spec 007 §14 A11 |
   | Language popup | `wireframes/locale-popup-{desktop,mobile}.dc.html` (PR 185, unless round 2 redraws it): a centred card at 1440, a **top sheet** at 390 | spec 003 §14 A16, with A14's Shape as A16 amends it |

   The file names are the round-1 ones; the round-2 PR fixes the final names, and its names win.

   **Round-2 picks (founder, 2026-10-05).** The desktop home is option A (a full-bleed photograph
   with a white card); the phone home is option 1 (a cover photograph with a sticky "Send flowers"
   bar); the product page is the approved two-column desktop plus the phone "sheet" (option 2) with
   a sticky price-and-button bar. The bar for every page is "uncluttered, state of the art,
   grid-aligned, mobile designed on its own". The home and product builds are **not** in this
   amendment's tasks: they are planned after the round-2 design PR merges (Non-goal here; the
   product page's v1 leftovers stay a carry-forward in TASK-179's brief).

   **Order.** No TASK-186 to TASK-191 build starts before the round-2 design PR merges (clause 10).
   **Scope.** Checkout, confirmation, track order, for florists and blog are not redrawn by this
   amendment, and no task builds them from it.

2. **Container and gutters (audit finding 4).** The content column is the header's frame on every
   page: 1,328 px wide (`--container-page`, `--page-max`) inside 56 px gutters (`--gutter`) from
   `md` up, and 20 px gutters (`--gutter-s`) below `md`. So the inline-start edge of `<main>`'s
   content equals the header logo's at every width: x 56 at 1440 and x 20 at 390. `Container`
   (`src/modules/ui/primitives/layout.tsx` L96–L127) takes the header's frame
   (`SiteHeader.tsx` L76) and stays the one place a page width is decided. No page sets its own
   inline padding. This supersedes the 1,328 px *outer* width and the "16 px on mobile" padding in
   `Container` today, which leave the content 1,184 px wide at x 128 and 4 px off at 390. The home
   already conforms (audit §2 "Home", alignment: none).

3. **Breadcrumb offset (audit finding 8).** On every page with a breadcrumb, the breadcrumb's top
   edge is **20 px** below the sticky header's bottom edge, inside the hero band, as every redrawn
   artboard draws (audit §3 row 8). Today it is 10 px on the listings and 50 px on the product page
   and the guide. The offset lives in one shared place (`Breadcrumbs` or the page-head frame that
   holds it), and every per-page margin above a breadcrumb is removed.

4. **The rulings the drawings need (audit §4 R1–R8), binding.**
   - **R1, a register per listing page (audit §4 R1; findings 1, 6).** The closed set of grounds is
     `warm` (butter), `quiet` (cream, no tilt, no tape, italic in `--color-ink-2`), `dated` (blush),
     `cream`, `leaf`, `sky` and `ink`. Each occasion and each category has exactly one, chosen by a
     **registry field**, never by a component. The registry is one zod-parsed config file,
     `src/config/catalogue/listing-presentation.ts`, keyed by occasion and category key (covered by
     `pnpm check:no-db`). It holds `register`, the hub's `leadSku` (R2) and the category's count
     noun (spec 008 §14 A15). It is config rather than a copy-row field because none of the three
     is per locale, and spec 002's category and occasion columns must not drift. This is the bounded
     config deviation of spec 008 §12, and spec 012's admin takes it over with no call-site change.
     Defaults: `warm` for celebrations, `quiet` for sympathy and funeral, `dated` for every seasonal
     occasion. The categories the artboards draw: roses `ink`, hand-tied bouquets `cream`, plants
     `leaf`. The country occasion page uses its occasion's register (always `dated`, because a
     country occasion page exists only for an observed, dated occasion). The occasions index is
     drawn on `sky`. How each register looks (its ground and type) is the round-2 artboard's
     (clause 10); the closed set, the registry and "a `quiet` band has no rotated and no taped
     element" bind.
   - **R2, a lead product per page (audit §4 R2).** On the country-scoped pages the lead is
     `items[0]` at size; spec 008 §14 A15 rules it. On the occasion and category hubs the lead is
     the registry's `leadSku`, falling back to the hub's first item. The `leadSku` must belong to
     that hub's own product set. The artboards fix six: Birthday FO-BQ-023, Sympathy FO-BQ-016,
     Mother's Day FO-BQ-007, roses FO-BQ-002, hand-tied bouquets FO-BQ-009, plants FO-PT-002. For
     every other hub the orchestrator sets the lead for Phase 0 (the spec 008 §14 A12 precedent) and
     lists it in the PR. The hub grid is unchanged: `collator` order, every item, the lead in its own
     place. The hero figure is not a card and not an `ItemList` element.
   - **R3, a dated occasion hub shows its dates in the first screen (audit §4 R3).** A `dated`
     occasion hub shows its per-country dates above the fold, drawn as its round-2 artboard draws
     them (the round-1 stamps do not bind; clause 10). They hold the same `occasionDate(rule, year)`
     values as the date table, in the table's order, through `formatDate`. An entry is a link only
     where its country occasion page exists, and plain text otherwise (A20). The table stays below
     the fold as the accessible, rankable form, and the first-screen entries are a named list that
     repeats it. A country whose date is unknown prints `occasionHub.dateUnknown` and no number.
   - **R4, the category hub's destination picker moves into the first screen (audit §4 R4).** It is
     drawn as the round-2 artboard draws it (the round-1 stamps do not bind). Each destination is
     one link, named by `categoryHub.destinationLink`; its visible text is the country and the
     product count `categoryHub.destinationCount`. A destination below the six-product floor is
     text with `categoryHub.destinationPending` and is not a link. Spec 008 §13 Q4 (countries first)
     is unchanged: the picker now sits inside the first screen.
   - **R5, the occasions index gains a "next six" block (audit §4 R5).** This new block sits above
     the groups: the six soonest dated occasions in the index's date country (Poland, design-round
     Q4), in chronological order, from `nextOccasions` through `listingView()`. These are the same
     dates the table prints. With fewer than six in the next twelve months, the block shows what
     there is; with none, it renders nothing. Each entry links its hub where the hub exists. Its
     label reuses `shop.root.occasionsEyebrow`. The look is the round-2 artboard's (the round-1
     sheet and postmark do not bind). The groups keep `collator(locale)` order (design-round Q3).
   - **R6, the destinations hub's first screen (audit §4 R6).** This is presentation of the same
     list spec 007 renders, with the same entries, order and links (spec 007 AC-7, AC-17, AC-20
     unchanged), drawn as the round-2 artboard draws it (the round-1 envelope and postmark do not
     bind). While any destination is a guide, the first screen prints
     `destinations.state.guideNotDelivering` as real text. A live destination carries
     `destinations.state.deliveringNow`. When no destination is a guide, no guide-state line
     renders.
   - **R7, `listingView()` carries the destination's state (audit §4 R7).** `ListingViewSchema`'s
     `country` gains `state: 'guide' | 'live'` from spec 007's `corridorState(iso2)`, the predicate
     the corridor page already uses. The country pages' state text reads that field and nothing
     else. Spec 008 §14 A9 stands: no listing module reads `corridorView()` or a `CorridorView`.
   - **R8, the country category's toolbar moves under the grid (audit §4 R8).** Spec 008 §14 A15
     rules it. The count line and the ranking disclosure stay above the grid and stay **visible**,
     in the compact form the artboard draws. Spec 008 §8 L240's ranking-transparency duty needs the
     disclosure on screen, so "visually hidden" is not used for it.

5. **The first screen, per page type.** What each round-2 artboard's first screen shows sits
   inside the first viewport at both sizes, and in the laptop band (clause 9). The **content order**
   holds on every page: the H1 first, then the primary action, with a product photograph or flowers
   visible in the first viewport wherever the page has a photograph to show.
   - **Country pages, page 1** (shop root, country category, country occasion): the H1, the
     destination's state text (R7), and the lead's photograph with its price tag, which is the
     primary action. The tag is the card's own all-in price (`formatMoney`, `catalog.price.allIn`,
     and the equivalents line where A21 clause 6 (c) shows one). On the country occasion page the
     first viewport also holds the occasion's date: one `formatDate` value, and where the artboard
     sets its day, month, weekday and year apart, they are the parts of that one value, produced by
     a parts formatter added to `src/modules/i18n/format.ts`, so no component calls `Intl`.
   - **The shop's notice** (`shop.root.demoNotice`, the round-1 "P.S.") is **not** a first-screen
     item. It renders where the round-2 artboard places it, and no text in the first viewport
     contains a phrase AC-37 forbids.
   - **Page 2 and later** render no hero: the toolbar and the grid start the page. Page 1 is the
     lead card plus 11 grid cards (spec 008 §14 A15 (c)).
   - **Hubs.** On the occasion and category hubs, the first screen holds the lead photograph, the
     no-money sentence `shop.hub.noMoney` and the way to a price (R3, R4, or the country links). The
     occasions index shows the R5 block, and the destinations hub the R6 guide-state line. Hubs
     still show no money (spec 008 AC-7).

6. **Copy: the approved English strings (Appendix A; founder, 2026-10-04: "go, approve copy and
   also i asked to redesign all the pages not just a few. right?").** These are approved text,
   verbatim. Asterisks in Appendix A mark the artboards' italic; that is presentation, not text.
   Each key ships `reviewed: false` in the PR that adds it. **The founder attests the exact
   catalogue value with their own script** (`record-approval.py`, decisions log "Recording the
   founder's copy approval"); no agent sets `reviewed: true`. `de`/`pl` values are machine drafts,
   `reviewed: false`.

   | Id | Key or record | Approved English text |
   |---|---|---|
   | A1 | Birthday hub deck | Bright flowers for the day itself, made by our florist in the town where they live. |
   | A2 | `categoryHub.h1` | {entity} to send abroad ("Roses to send abroad", "Hand-tied bouquets to send abroad", "Plants to send abroad") |
   | A3 | `occasionHub.productsHeading` | {entity} flowers ("Birthday flowers", "Sympathy flowers", "Mother's Day flowers") |
   | A4 | Hub "about" block, eyebrow and heading | About birthdays · What people send (occasion); About roses · Which rose says what (category) |
   | A5 | Category hub decks | Roses: "Red for love, white for a loss, pink and orange for everything in between." · Bouquets: "Tied by hand the morning they go, and handed over wrapped, ready for water." · Plants: "Something that is still there next season." |
   | A7 | `copy_category.bouquet.descriptionMd` (`en`) | "Prices on this page include delivery and local tax, and the size you choose is the size that is made." becomes "The size you choose is the size that is made." |
   | A8 | Occasions index eyebrows | "Every occasion, on every calendar" (over the H1) · "Dated elsewhere" (over the undated group) |
   | A9 | `shop.root.tileCount` | {count} {noun} we can make for {country}, the noun per category ("8 plants", "14 arrangements", "40 bouquets") |
   | A10 | Country category, the label of the toolbar under the grid | Change the order |
   | A11 | Guide calendar, Rule column | Same date every year · Moves with Easter · A set weekday of the month |
   | L1 | Language popup title (replaces `banner.headline`) | Choose your preferred language |
   | L3 | Popup tag on the URL's language | Current |
   | L4 | Popup tag on the browser's match | Matches your browser (desktop) · Your browser (390) |
   | L5 | Popup close button, accessible name | Close |
   | L6 | Popup foot | You can change it any time at the top of every page. |
   | B1 | `catalog.addon.card.name` / `.description` | Printed card · "Your message, printed on a card and tucked into the bouquet." |
   | B2 | Hub intros' last sentence (`copy_occasion.*` and `copy_category.*` `descriptionMd`, `en` and `en-gb`) | Our florist in the recipient's town makes every one by hand and hands it over in person. |

   - **B1** replaces "Handwritten card" and "written out by hand", which are still live (audit
     finding 5). A21 clause 5 already bans the claim; B1 gives the description its approved text.
   - **B2** replaces "Every order will be made by hand and delivered in person by our florist in the
     recipient's city." in the hub intros only. It closes A22 open item (iii) for the hub intros, as
     the decisions-log row "The product florist sentence moves to the present tense" closed it for
     `catalog.floristSentence`. Product descriptions are outside B2.
   - **Not approved:** L2 (the German and Polish sub-lines under L1) waits for the `de`/`pl` native
     reviewers. A3 and A4 strings for entities the batch did not name, and every deck other than A1
     and A5, follow open item (iii) below.
   - **Decks without approved text.** A hub whose deck has no approved string (Sympathy and Mother's
     Day as drawn) prints one **verbatim sentence of its own reviewed intro**, chosen by a sentence
     index in the registry row. A sentence index is not new copy. A hub with neither renders no deck.

7. **Carried over, unchanged in force.**
   - **One photograph.** At most one hero photograph above the fold, the page's only image with
     `fetchpriority="high"` and its only preload, from the same manifest lookup as its `srcset`
     (spec 008 §5.4 L199, spec 006 AC-19). Spec 008 §14 A11's zero applies when the lead has no
     approved photograph. The occasions index, the destinations hub and the guide preload no image:
     their LCP element is the H1.
   - **Nothing moves by itself.** No carousel, no auto-advance, no client island beyond those A21
     already allows and the language popup. Clause 11's motion is the one addition, inside its
     limits.
   - **No counts and no false state.** No count of florists, orders, reviews or deliveries; product
     counts are catalogue facts and stay. No "Delivering now" or `destinations.state.deliveringNow`
     for a destination whose state is `guide`, and no freshness number of days.
   - **Tense.** Florists in the present tense (A22 clause 1).
   - **Performance and accessibility.** LCP under 2,000 ms and CLS under 0.05 (AC-24, A18). WCAG
     2.2 AA: tap targets of at least 24 × 24 CSS px (44 px where §5.3 asks it), focus visible, no
     colour-only meaning. Every decorative device the round-2 artboards draw is server-rendered
     inline SVG or CSS; decoration is `aria-hidden`, and any text on it is real text. AC-26's zero
     serious or critical axe violations, with no exception list, holds.
   - **The system.** Tokens only. The one new token here is clause 9's `--hero-photo-max`; any token
     the round-2 PR adds lands in `system/tokens.css` and `src/app/globals.css` together. Logical
     properties only. The airmail edge appears in at most A21 clause 2's three places. Caveat stays
     on the product page.

8. **Rollout: six build tasks, in this order, none started before the round-2 design PR merges
   (clause 10), plus one copy task that is not gated on it.**
   - **TASK-186.** Clauses 2, 3 and 9. It lands first.
   - **TASK-187.** The three country listing first screens, R1's registry, R2 on country pages, R7,
     R8, and spec 008 §14 A15 (pagination, the tile noun). It ships with TASK-146 as one feature PR.
   - **TASK-188.** The four hub first screens: R2 on hubs, R3–R6, and clause 6's hub copy. It comes
     after TASK-187, because both edit `listingView()` and `ListingViewSchema`.
   - **TASK-189.** The guide (spec 007 §14 A11), after TASK-179.
   - **TASK-190.** The two mismatched photographs (audit finding 15; spec 006 seed imagery).
   - **TASK-191.** The not-found document to `errors-*`, after TASK-179.
   - **TASK-193.** Clause 12's neutral copy batch and AC-37's phrase scan. It changes copy and one
     test, draws nothing, and may land before TASK-186.

   The language popup's look and copy (L1, L3–L6) belong to TASK-119, which builds spec 003 §14 A16
   (merged in PR 186): there is no chooser page, `/` answers one 308 to `/en`, and the closable
   popup is the only chooser, a centred card on desktop and a top sheet on mobile. Its highlight
   uses the browser language only (A16 clause 2, resolved by the founder on 2026-10-05: "browser
   language only"). TASK-119 owns AC-38 for L1 and L3–L6 and AC-39 here, beside spec 003's AC-28.
   The product page's v1 leftovers and B1 are a carry-forward in TASK-179's brief. Motion
   (clause 11) gets its task after the round-2 design PR; none exists yet.

9. **Sizing for laptops and phones (founder, 2026-10-04, in chat: "also when u redesign or plan for
   redesign... make sure the sizing of page is good for both computer and phone.. cuz current pages
   on demo site feel big on my macbook").**
   - **Why the pages feel big.** The fluid steps grow with width only and reach their maxima at
     1,450 px or below, so a 13–14" laptop gets the type the 1440 × 900 artboards draw while its
     usable height is smaller. Computed from the shipped `clamp()`s (`src/app/globals.css`
     L242–L249, `system/tokens.css` L193–L200):

     | Token | 1280 × 800 now | 1512 × 945 now |
     |---|---|---|
     | `--text-hero-fluid` `clamp(50px, 6.2vw, 90px)` | 79.4 px | 90 px |
     | `--text-display-fluid` `clamp(42px, 6vw, 84px)` | 76.8 px | 84 px |
     | `--text-title-fluid` `clamp(44px, 5vw, 68px)` | 64 px | 68 px |
     | `--text-3xl-fluid` `clamp(32px, 4.2vw, 54px)` | 53.8 px | 54 px |
     | `--text-2xl-fluid` `clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 44px)` | 43.5 px | 44 px |
     | `--section-fluid` `clamp(64px, 9vw, 128px)` | 115.2 px | 128 px |

     These are computed, not measured. The live H1 box, the hero photograph's height and the
     y-position of the first price or primary action at 1280 × 800 and 1512 × 945 are **measured by
     TASK-186 as its first step**, on production before the change and on the preview after. Both
     tables go into the brief and the PR.
   - **The laptop band.** Every page is checked at 1280 × 800 and 1512 × 945 CSS px as well as at
     1440 × 900. The fluid steps gain a height term and lower maxima; their minima, which are the
     phone sizes, do not change.

     | Token | New value | 1280 × 800 | 1440 × 900 | 1512 × 945 | 390 × 844 |
     |---|---|---|---|---|---|
     | `--text-hero-fluid` | `clamp(50px, min(5.2vw, 8.6vh), 76px)` | 66.6 | 74.9 | 76 | 50 |
     | `--text-display-fluid` | `clamp(42px, min(5vw, 8vh), 70px)` | 64 | 70 | 70 | 42 |
     | `--text-title-fluid` | `clamp(44px, min(4.2vw, 6.8vh), 58px)` | 53.8 | 58 | 58 | 44 |
     | `--text-3xl-fluid` | `clamp(32px, min(3.6vw, 5.8vh), 46px)` | 46 | 46 | 46 | 32 |
     | `--text-2xl-fluid` | `clamp(30px, 3vw, 38px)` | 38 | 38 | 38 | 30 |
     | `--section-fluid` | `clamp(64px, min(7vw, 11vh), 104px)` | 88 | 99 | 104 | 64 |
     | `--hero-photo-max` (new) | `min(520px, 60svh)` | 480 | 520 | 520 | 506 |

     The `min(…vw, …vh)` term keeps a short laptop window from getting a width-driven headline.
     The px minimum means browser zoom still enlarges text, so §5.3 L214's 200 % resize holds.
     `--text-lede-fluid`, the body steps, `--gutter` and `--gutter-s` do not change.
   - **The vertical rhythm.** Hero bands and page sections take their block padding from
     `--section-fluid` instead of a fixed `--space-3xl` (128 px) or `--space-2xl` (72 px). The lead
     or hero photograph's block size is capped at `--hero-photo-max`, and its aspect ratio is kept
     by narrowing its inline size, never by cropping a different crop.
   - **The container stays 1,328 px** (clause 2). At 1280 the column is already 1,168 px
     (1,280 − 2 × 56). Narrowing it would move the header frame and break AC-31's alignment, and the
     oversized feel comes from type and vertical rhythm, not width.
   - **What the first screen shows.** At 1280 × 800 and 1512 × 945, on the home, the seven listing
     page types and the guide, the first viewport holds the H1, the deck or lede, the hero or lead
     photograph (where the artboard draws one) and the primary action: the first price on a country
     page, the way to a price on a hub, and on the home its "Send flowers" action (the sentence's
     submit today; the white card's action or the sticky bar once the round-2 home is built).
   - **The phone.** At 390 × 844 the minima above hold, so nothing shrinks there. Nothing is
     cramped and nothing is too small:
     - no horizontal overflow;
     - no rendered text under 13 px (`--text-xs`);
     - inline-size gutters of 20 px;
     - every link and button in `<main>` at least 24 × 24 px (WCAG 2.2 2.5.8), and the primary
       actions at least 44 px tall (§5.3).
   - **The artboards.** `docs/design/system/tokens.css` takes the same values in the same PR, name
     for name and value for value (T-1; CLAUDE.md: the system files stay in step with the code).
     The round-2 artboards are drawn at these values. Any artboard still drawn at the old maxima
     gets a dated "Where the sheet and the code currently differ" row in `docs/design/README.md`
     citing this clause.

10. **Round 2 replaces the round-1 devices; no build before it merges (founder, 2026-10-05).**
    - **No TASK-186 to TASK-191 build starts before the round-2 design PR merges.** The orchestrator
      dispatches none of them earlier, and an implementer who finds the round-2 artboards absent
      from `main` stops and escalates.
    - **What no longer binds.** Round 2 replaces the stamps, postmarks, envelopes and dated stamps
      on hubs that PR 185 drew. Wherever this amendment, spec 008 §14 A15, spec 007 §14 A11 or a
      TASK-186 to TASK-191 brief named such a device, the binding is now **the page's artboard in
      `docs/design/wireframes` (round 2, PR to come)** plus the measurable rules below. No task
      builds a round-1 device the round-2 artboard does not draw.
    - **What still binds, whatever round 2 draws:**
      - the laptop band and the phone checks (clause 9; AC-41 to AC-43);
      - the first-screen content order: the H1 first, then the primary action, with a product
        photograph or flowers visible in the first viewport (clause 5; AC-34, AC-35);
      - pagination: page 1 is the lead card plus 11 grid cards, page 2 and later have no hero, and
        every product is reachable (spec 008 §14 A15; AC-29, AC-31 there);
      - honesty: clause 7's "No counts and no false state", clause 12's copy and AC-37's scan;
      - tokens only (clause 7 "The system"; AC-42).
    - **Where round 2 draws what a clause forbids,** the clause wins and the difference is recorded
      in `docs/design/README.md` (clause 1). A clause that names a round-1 device and was missed
      here is read as this bullet says.

11. **Motion: the loading screen and the flower micro-animations (founder, 2026-10-05).** The
    design comes in `docs/design/system/motion.dc.html`. Whatever it draws, these bind:
    - **Loaders appear only on client transitions**, never in front of server-rendered content.
      A first page load, a reload and a JavaScript-off load show the server's HTML with no loader
      over it.
    - **No animation delays the LCP element.** The LCP element is painted at its final position and
      opacity in the first frame it renders; no entrance animation, fade-in or delay applies to it.
    - **CSS and SVG only.** No animation library and no new runtime dependency. Any JavaScript is
      limited to toggling a class on a client transition, inside the existing islands' budgets.
    - **Transform and opacity only.** No animation changes layout (no `width`, `height`, `top`,
      `margin` or similar), so CLS stays 0 across every animation.
    - **A `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` version for every motion:** no movement, at most an
      opacity change, and no loop.
    - **Order.** No task exists for motion yet; it is planned after the round-2 design PR, and its
      task owns AC-44.

12. **The neutral copy batch (founder, 2026-10-05: "approve the wording just no em dash").** These
    `en` strings replace the "still choosing florists" wording. They are approved text, verbatim,
    and carry no em dash. Each ships `reviewed: false`; the founder attests the exact catalogue
    value with their own `record-approval.py` run (clause 6), and no agent sets `reviewed: true`.
    The `de` and `pl` values are machine drafts of the new text, with no em dash, `reviewed: false`.

    | Id | Key | Approved English text |
    |---|---|---|
    | N1 | `shop.root.demoNotice` | Ordering opens soon. Every price here is the price you will pay, with VAT and delivery included. |
    | N2 | `product.demo.body` | When ordering opens, this is the price you will pay. Nothing is added at a later step. |
    | N3 | `delivery.picker.unavailable` | Delivery dates for {country} open when ordering does. |
    | N4 | `corridor.facts.delivering.none` | Not yet. This page will say so the day we start delivering in {country}. |
    | N5 | `corridor.coverage.bodyNone` | We are not delivering in {country} yet, so this page lists no towns. |
    | N6 | `categoryHub.destinationPending` | No page yet. This becomes a link the day we start delivering here. |
    | N7 | `catalog.availability.noPartner` | We do not deliver to this destination yet. |

    - **Removed:** `nav.utility.datesPending` and `nav.utility.datesPendingShort` ("Delivery dates
      open when we confirm our first florist"), with their render site in the utility strip and
      their meta entries in every locale. TASK-186 does not own them, so TASK-193 does.
    - N1 and N2 drop the `{country}` argument. The call sites stop passing it, and the `de` and
      `pl` drafts carry the same arguments as `en`, so `i18n:check` stays green.
    - **Honesty is unchanged.** Each page that showed the old string still says that nothing can be
      ordered yet (N1, N2, the product page's `product.demo.heading`), and the price shown is still
      the price charged.

Acceptance criteria (each test is watched red by mutating its subject):
- **AC-31 — the frame (TASK-186).** *Amended by §14 A24 clause 4 (full-bleed media skipped).*
  On the home, the seven listing page types, the guide, the
  product page and the not-found document, at 1440, 1024, 768 and 390 px wide, the inline-start edge
  of the first block in `<main>` equals the header logo's inline-start edge within 0.5 px (x 56 at
  1440, x 20 at 390). At 1440 the listing content column is 1,328 px wide.
- **AC-32 — the breadcrumb (TASK-186; the phone half TASK-195).** *Amended by §14 A24 clause 4
  (e) below `md`.* On every page type with a breadcrumb, at 1440 × 900 and
  390 × 844, the breadcrumb's top edge minus the sticky header's bottom edge is 20 px, within 1 px.
- **AC-33 — the registry (TASK-187).** `listing-presentation.ts` parses at module load, with exactly
  one row per occasion and per category that has a hub. `register` must be in the closed set of R1
  and `countNoun` in spec 008 §14 A15's closed set. A `leadSku` outside its hub's product set fails
  the load, naming the key. The rendered band's register (`data-register`) equals the row's: a
  fixture that flips Birthday to `quiet` changes the band with no change to any component. A
  `quiet` band renders no rotated and no taped element.
- **AC-34 — the country first screens (TASK-187).** *Rebound by §14 A24 clause 2: read A24's
  text, which replaces this one.* On page 1 of `/en-gb/poland/flowers`,
  `/en-gb/poland/flowers/roses` and `/en-gb/poland/occasions/mothers-day`, at both sizes:
  - these lie inside the first viewport: the H1; the destination's state text, equal to
    `listingView().country.state`'s message; and the lead photograph with its price tag;
  - the H1 precedes the price tag in DOM order;
  - on the country occasion page, the occasion's date text equals `formatDate`'s value for that
    occasion and country (or, where it is set in parts, the parts of that one value);
  - the tag's price equals the lead card's `formatMoney` output;
  - no text inside the first viewport contains any phrase AC-37 forbids, and `shop.root.demoNotice`
    is not required there;
  - page 1 holds the lead plus 11 grid cards (spec 008 AC-31), and page 2 renders no hero.
- **AC-35 — the hub first screens (TASK-188).** *Amended by §14 A24 clause 2 (c) below `md`.*
  At both sizes on `/en-gb/occasions/birthday`,
  `/en-gb/occasions/sympathy`, `/en-gb/occasions/mothers-day`, `/en-gb/flowers/roses`,
  `/en-gb/occasions` and `/en-gb/send-flowers-to`, the first viewport holds:
  - on the occasion and category hubs, the lead figure (the registry's `leadSku`), `shop.hub.noMoney`
    and at least one destination link, with the H1 first in DOM order;
  - on Mother's Day, the R3 per-country dates, equal to the table's and in its order;
  - on roses, the R4 destination links;
  - on the occasions index, the R5 block in chronological order;
  - on the destinations hub, the text of `destinations.state.guideNotDelivering` (R6).

  No hub renders money (spec 008 AC-7).
- **AC-36 — one photograph, the budgets and axe (TASK-188).** *Amended by §14 A24 clause 5
  (the priority image per width).*
  - On each of the seven listing page types, exactly one `<img>` has `fetchpriority="high"`, with
    exactly one matching preload. It is the lead's photograph on page 1 of a country page and on a
    hub, and the first card's photograph on page 2.
  - The occasions index and the destinations hub have none.
  - No element carries `aria-roledescription="carousel"` and none auto-advances.
  - Lighthouse mobile: one URL per page type, LCP under 2,000 ms and CLS under 0.05.
  - axe: zero serious or critical violations on the seven types in four locales.
- **AC-37 — honesty on the first screens (state and counts: TASK-188; phrases: TASK-193).**
  - A DOM scan of the seven listing types and the guide in four locales finds none of these:
    `destinations.state.deliveringNow`'s text for a `guide` destination; a florist, order, review
    or delivery count; a freshness number of days. "Handwritten" also stays absent (T-17).
  - **The phrases.** No `en` or `en-gb` copy contains, case-insensitively, "still choosing",
    "first florist", "no florist" or "choosing florists". This holds for every value in
    `messages/en.json` and `messages/en-gb.json`, every `en`/`en-gb` seed copy record, every
    `content/corridors/en*/` file, and the rendered `en` and `en-gb` DOM of the home, the seven
    listing types, the guide and a product page. The only exceptions are the entries of one named
    list in the test (open item (vii)): each names one key or one file and FAQ entry, the list may
    only shrink, and a match outside it fails.
- **AC-38 — the approved strings (per id: A9, A10 TASK-187; A1–A5, A7, A8, B2 TASK-188; A11
  TASK-189; L1, L3–L6 TASK-119; B1 TASK-179; N1–N7 TASK-193).** Each `en` value or seed record in
  clause 6 and clause 12 equals its approved text byte for byte. Each key added or changed for
  clause 6 or 12 is `reviewed: false` in the PR that changes it. L2 is never `reviewed: true`
  without a native reviewer's record. No N1–N7 value in `en`, `de` or `pl` contains an em dash
  (U+2014), and `nav.utility.datesPending` and `datesPendingShort` exist in no catalogue.
- **AC-39 — the popup's look (TASK-119; spec 003 §14 A16 clause 2, with spec 003 AC-28 (g) as
  A16 restates it).**
  - At 1440 × 900 the open popup is a centred native `<dialog>` card.
  - At 390 × 844 it is a top sheet: its top edge is at y 0 and it is at most 260 px tall.
  - It is never on screen together with the consent sheet.
  - Its four choices are real `<a>`s with `lang` and `hreflang`.
  - Its strings are L1 and L3–L6 (AC-38); the L4 mark follows `navigator.languages` alone (spec 003
    AC-28 (b)).
- **AC-40 — the not-found document (TASK-191).** *Rebound by §14 A24 clause 6.*
  `/en/does-not-exist` and `/pl/nie-ma` answer 404
  with `lang` set, and render the `errors-*` notice document: the logo header and no navigation; the
  letter with the code as a paragraph eyebrow, one `<h1>`, one sentence, and two links (Home,
  Destinations) that each answer 200. Every decorative mark (the round-1 postmark, or what round 2
  draws in its place) is `aria-hidden`. AC-12's contracts are unchanged.
- **AC-41 — the laptop first screen (TASK-186).** *Amended by §14 A24 clause 5 (the home hero's
  own bound).* At 1280 × 800 and 1512 × 945 on `/en-gb`,
  `/en-gb/poland/flowers`, `/en-gb/poland/flowers/roses`, `/en-gb/poland/occasions/mothers-day`,
  `/en-gb/occasions/birthday`, `/en-gb/flowers/roses`, `/en-gb/occasions`, `/en-gb/send-flowers-to`
  and `/en-gb/send-flowers-to/poland`:
  - the H1 and the page's primary action (clause 9) lie fully inside the viewport;
  - the H1's computed `font-size` equals clause 9's value for its step at that viewport, within
    0.5 px (for example `--text-hero-fluid`: 66.6 px at 1280 × 800 and 76 px at 1512 × 945);
  - no hero or lead photograph is taller than `--hero-photo-max` resolved (480 px at 1280 × 800).

  Before TASK-186 lands, the shipped tokens fail the font-size clause (79.4 px for the hero step at
  1280 × 800).
- **AC-42 — the tokens are pinned (TASK-186).** `src/app/globals.css` and
  `docs/design/system/tokens.css` declare exactly clause 9's values for the seven tokens of its table,
  and the two files agree.
- **AC-43 — the phone (TASK-186).** At 390 × 844 on the AC-41 page set:
  - `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` equals the viewport width;
  - no visible text node renders below 13 px;
  - every `<a>` and `<button>` in `<main>` is at least 24 × 24 px, and each primary action is at
    least 44 px tall;
  - each fluid step resolves to its unchanged minimum;
  - the H1 lies inside the first viewport.
- **AC-44 — motion stays inside its limits (TASK-194; clause 11).** *Amended by §14 A24
  clause 9.* On the AC-41 page set and the product page, at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844:
  - a first load, a reload and a JavaScript-off load render no loader element over `<main>`; a
    loader renders only during a client-side navigation;
  - the LCP element and its ancestors carry no `animation`, no `transition` and no
    `animation-delay`, and the LCP element's computed `opacity` is 1 and its `transform` is `none`
    at its first paint;
  - every `@keyframes` rule and every `transition-property` in the shipped CSS names only
    `transform` and/or `opacity`, and CLS is 0 across each animation;
  - no animation library is in `package.json` or the client bundles;
  - with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` emulated, no element's transform changes over time and
    no animation iterates more than once.

Open for the founder (defaults bind until answered):
- **(i) Registers for undrawn categories.** The artboards draw three of the categories. Default:
  every other category is `cream` until the founder picks another from the closed set.
- **(ii) Leads for undrawn hubs.** Default: the orchestrator sets them (R2), and the founder sees
  the list in the PR.
- **(iii) A3, A4 and decks beyond the batch.**
  - A3: the pattern applies to every occasion except those where it reads badly (at least "I am
    sorry" and "Love and romance"). Those keep the shipped reviewed heading until a per-occasion
    string is approved.
  - A4: "What people send" heads the about block on every occasion hub. The eyebrow ships only for
    Birthday ("About birthdays"), and the category pair only for roses. On other hubs the block
    renders the intro with no eyebrow and no heading (spec 008 §5.2: missing data renders nothing).
  - TASK-188 lists the strings still needed for the founder's next batch.
- **(iv) The 404 letter's airmail edge.** `errors-*` draws `--airmail-edge` on the letter, a fourth
  place against A21 clause 2's three (and T-1's "exactly three"). Default: the spec wins; the
  letter has no airmail edge, and the difference is recorded in `docs/design/README.md`.
- **(v) B2 outside English.** Default: the `de`/`pl` hub intros stay byte-identical, unreviewed
  machine drafts, until their native review.
- **(vi) The bouquet hub's meta description.** `copy_category.bouquet.seoDescription` ends
  "Delivery and local tax included." on a page with no price: the same untruth A7 removes from the
  intro, but it is not in Appendix A. Default: unchanged until the founder approves new wording,
  which TASK-188 proposes.
- **(vii) AC-37's phrases outside the approved batch.** Clause 12 covers seven keys. Two kinds of
  shipped, reviewed `en` copy still match a forbidden phrase:
  - `corridor.facts.orderBy.none` ("… no cutoff, because no florist has agreed to one"), on the
    guide;
  - the guides' "can you deliver today" FAQ answers that say "We are still choosing the shops…":
    today `content/corridors/en/pl-guide.md`, `en-gb/pl-guide.md`, `en/es-guide.md`,
    `en-gb/es-guide.md` and `en-gb/fr-guide.md` (spec 007's reviewed corpus, kept byte-identical by
    A22 open item (ii)).

  Default: these, and only these, are T-47's named exception list, and TASK-193 proposes
  replacement wording for each (no em dash) in its PR for the founder's next batch. Each entry
  leaves the list in the PR that ships its approved replacement.
- **(viii) The round-2 quotes.** The round-2 picks and the motion request are recorded as the
  orchestrator relayed them. If the founder's chat words differ, the decisions-log rows take the
  verbatim text and this amendment follows them.

**Status: approved by the orchestrator on the founder's delegation, 2026-10-05; founder to
confirm on return.** The delegation, in the founder's words (chat, 2026-10-05): "in my absence
you are the design reviewer/critquer/approver. I trust you will not fail me. build everything".
The founder's own picks, which this amendment writes down and does not reinterpret (decisions
log, 2026-10-05; audit "The founder's rulings in this round"): "A homepage is good", "product
page is good", "home 1 and product 2", and, on the motion sheet, "i like the animations... they
are good approve them". Everything else here (the measurable rules, the phone pattern, the
rebinding of AC-34 to AC-36 and AC-40, the copy left pending) is the orchestrator's approval
under that delegation. It is not the founder's own words, and the founder may reverse any of it.

Original: A23 binds each page to its round-2 artboard "(PR to come)", but its clause 5, R3, R4,
R6, AC-34 and AC-36 still name round-1 forms: the destination's state in a postmark, the lead
photograph with its price tag on every country page, dates and destinations as stamps. A4 puts
the languages and the currency in the utility strip "on every breakpoint". A20 clause 2 omits the
phone menu because it opened nothing. AC-40 names a postmark on the 404. Clause 9 caps every hero
photograph at `--hero-photo-max`. Spec 008 §14 A2 puts the "Example arrangement" label on every
card. Nothing in the spec describes how the phone behaves as a design of its own.

Measured (PR 189's artboards, rendered by the designer with Playwright at 1440 × 900, 1280 × 800,
1470 × 830, 1512 × 860, 1512 × 945, 390 × 844 and 360 × 640; the audit's collision probe reports
92 renders with 0 findings):
- **Listings, desktop.** No postmark, perforated stamp, tape or tilt. The state is one quiet text
  line, destinations are pills, dates are plain cards, and a lede over two lines moves under the
  first screen. The shop root and the country category go H1, state line, count and disclosure,
  then a grid of 12 priced cards with no separate lead figure. The country occasion keeps a lead
  photograph with its price beside the occasion's date. The hubs keep their lead figure.
- **Listings, phone.** A calm shop list: a `‹ Parent` back link, the H1, one muted line, one chip
  row, and a two-column grid from the first product. Each card is the image, the name and the
  price, with "all in" (`catalog.price.inclusive`) beside the price, so spec 008's card contract
  (AC-6) still holds. Then the honesty note and the ranking disclosure once under the grid, and a
  compact pager. No lead figure on any phone listing, hubs included. Clause 1 lists what these
  artboards omit and the spec still requires.
- **Chrome.** Desktop unchanged (a 37 px utility strip and an 82 px header, 119 px). Phone: logo
  and Menu only (65 px including its rule). The links, Send flowers, the languages, the currency
  and the help line are inside the menu (a `<details>` element), and there is no utility strip.
- **Home.** Desktop A: the flat-lay fills the first screen under the chrome,
  `block-size: clamp(560px, 100svh − 119px, 826px)`, with one white card on columns 1–5 holding
  the H1, one line, the sentence and Continue (Continue ends at y 678 at 1280 × 800). Phone 1:
  the photograph is the screen (`min(703px, 100svh − 141px)`), the H1 on an ink fade at its
  foot, and a 76 px docked bar holding Send flowers.
- **Product.** Desktop: the photograph at 4∶5 on columns 2–7 with no height cap (712, 815 and
  855 px tall at 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 and 1512 × 945), so it runs 31 px past the 1280 × 800 fold
  by design. The buy column on 9–12 (name, price, sizes, the week's dates, the action) is fully in
  view at every laptop size (the action ends at y 740 at 1280 × 800). Phone: the photograph at
  `min(470px, 100svh − 300px)`, a paper sheet rising over its foot with name and price, segmented
  sizes and a date scroller, and a docked bar. There is no "Here is what will arrive" recap.
- **404.** No stamp, no postmark, no airmail edge. One faint dashed route that draws in once, and
  the document fills the first screen.

Corrected (the orchestrator on the founder's delegation, 2026-10-05):

1. **The design source is PR 189.** The "round-2 design PR" of A23 is PR 189 (branch
   `docs/design-round2-2026-10-05`). Its file names are final: in `docs/design/wireframes/`,
   `home-*`, `product-*`, `chrome-*`, `country-shop-*`, `country-category-*`, `country-occasion-*`,
   `occasion-hub-*`, `category-hub-*`, `occasions-index-*`, `all-destinations-*`,
   `corridor-country-*`, `errors-*`, `how-it-works-*`, `guarantee-and-delivery-*`,
   `help-and-contact-*`, `about-*`, `legal-template-*`, `cookies-*` and `locale-popup-*` (each in
   `-desktop` and `-mobile`), plus `system/motion.dc.html` and the round-2 group in
   `system/components.dc.html`. `locale-chooser-*` are retired (spec 003 §14 A16). The checkout,
   confirmation, track-order, for-florists and blog artboards are layout direction only, and no
   task builds them from this amendment (A23 clause 1 "Scope" stands).
   - **Where artboards disagree, the audit's "founder's rulings" table wins.** One known case: the
     `chrome-mobile` annotation's "Notice bar" row still describes a 44 px phone row holding the
     languages and the currency. The audit's Chrome row and the home, product and listing phone
     artboards remove that row (clause 3). The designer corrects the annotation in PR 189, or
     TASK-195 records the difference in `docs/design/README.md`.
   - **Where an artboard draws what a clause here forbids, the clause wins** (A21 clause 1), and
     the task that builds the page records the difference in `docs/design/README.md`. The known
     cases are named in clauses 2 (e), 4 (c), 4 (d) and 4 (f).
   - **Bindings the round-2 artboards omit.** Each item below is **required although the round-2
     artboard omits it**. The implementer adds it in the artboard's own style (type, colour,
     spacing of the nearest drawn element), records a dated row in `docs/design/README.md`, and
     the designer updates the artboard later. Each is held by the AC named.
     - **The state line on the three country pages, below `md`.** `country-shop-mobile`,
       `country-category-mobile` and `country-occasion-mobile` draw the back link, the H1 and then
       the notice or date line, with no state line. The state line (R7's value, one quiet text
       line as on desktop) sits directly under the H1, inside the first viewport. Held by the
       rebound AC-34. Built by TASK-187.
     - **The back link on the occasions index and the destinations hub, below `md`.**
       `occasions-index-mobile` and `all-destinations-mobile` draw none. Both pages have a
       breadcrumb, so both show the `‹ Parent` back link (to the home), placed as on
       `country-shop-mobile`. Held by AC-50 (and spec 007 §14 A12 (b)). Built by TASK-188.
     - **The product page's visible trail, below `md`.** `product-mobile` draws no trail. The
       full trail is displayed **below the buy section**, before the "More about this bouquet"
       blocks, styled as the desktop trail at the phone's text size. It is displayed because
       `BreadcrumbList` must equal a visible trail (spec 009's breadcrumb AC, this spec's AC-50),
       and the product page has no back link, so the trail is the only visible breadcrumb at
       390. Held by AC-50. Built by TASK-197.
     - **The `product.demo.body` line (N2) on the product page, below `md`.** `product-mobile`
       drops it; desktop keeps it under the status pill. It renders under the status pill in the
       sheet, so the phone still says why nothing can be bought and that the price shown is the
       price charged (spec 009 §14 A12, "spec wins where mobile drops required content"; AC-38
       N2). Held by AC-46's product half. Built by TASK-197.
     - The phone "Example arrangement" label is the fifth case, and it is a move, not an
       omission: clause 2 (e).

2. **The listing first screens (rebinds A23 clause 5's country-page bullet and the forms in R3,
   R4 and R6; AC-34 rebound; AC-35 and AC-36 amended; new AC-45).** No listing page and no guide
   draws a postmark, perforated stamp, envelope, tape, tucked card or dated stamp. The destination's
   state is one text line (R7's value). Destinations (R4, R6) are pills, dates (R3, R5) are plain
   cards, and a lede longer than two lines moves directly under the first screen. What binds is
   measurable:
   - **(a) Shop root and country category, page 1.** At 1440 × 900, 1280 × 800 and 390 × 844, the
     first viewport holds the H1, the state line and the **whole first row of priced cards**: every
     card of the grid's first row, from the top of its photograph to the bottom of its price. These
     two types draw **no separate lead figure**. The lead of spec 008 §14 A15 (c) is `items[0]`,
     drawn as the grid's first card, and page 1 is still 12 products, `items[0]` to `items[11]`.
     **The card contract holds at every width** (spec 008 AC-6). Below `md` a card is the image,
     the name and the price, with `catalog.price.inclusive` ("all in") beside every price. It is
     never a bare number.
   - **(b) Country occasion, page 1.** From `md` up, `items[0]` is drawn as the artboard's lead
     figure, with its price beside the occasion's date. Below `md` it is the grid's first card.
     Either way it is **one element in the DOM**: no product URL appears twice in `<main>` (spec
     008 AC-31), and no second copy of the product sits hidden with `display: none`. At the three sizes the first viewport
     holds the H1, the date (one `formatDate` value, or its parts per A23 clause 5), the state line
     and `items[0]`'s price. At 390 × 844 it also holds the rest of the grid's first row.
   - **(c) Hubs (AC-35 amended).** From `md` up, AC-35 is unchanged. Below `md` there is no lead
     figure, as the phone artboards draw it. At 390 × 844 the first viewport holds the H1,
     `shop.hub.noMoney`, the chip row's first destination link and the grid's first row. The lead
     figure's image is **not downloaded** below `md` (clause 5).
   - **(d) Calm, not bland (AC-45).** On the seven listing types and the guide, at the three sizes,
     the first viewport of `<main>` has no empty vertical gap over 120 px and no ornament.
   - **(e) R8 and the honesty label on the phone.** Below `md` the count line stays above the
     grid, and the ranking disclosure follows the grid, visible (non-zero box, not clipped, not
     visually hidden). From `md` up R8 is unchanged. The "Example arrangement" label is placed by
     spec 008 §14 A16. The phone artboards draw the label under the grid. The spec wins: below
     `md` it sits **directly above the grid**, inside the first viewport. The building task
     records the difference in `docs/design/README.md` (open item (iii)).
   - **(f) Checked before the build.** The designer renders the three country artboards at
     1280 × 800 in PR 189. If a first-row price falls below 800, the artboard is tightened, not
     this rule.

3. **The phone chrome (supersedes A4 L436's "on every breakpoint" below `md`, and A20 clause 2's
   omission of the menu; new AC-47).** Below `md` the header is the logo and **Menu**, 65 ± 1 px
   including its rule, sticky as the A4 addendum makes it. **There is no utility strip below
   `md`**: the strip, with its languages, currency and help line, is from `md` up only, exactly as
   A4 and A7 describe it. The Menu is a native disclosure (`<details>`/`<summary>`) that opens and
   closes with JavaScript off. It holds:
   - the header's published links (spec 008 §14 A14);
   - Send flowers;
   - spec 003's `LocaleSwitcher`, its markup unchanged (spec 003 AC-3);
   - the currency chip (AC-8 unchanged: text, a localised `aria-label`, no cookie);
   - the help line.

   With JavaScript on, `Esc` closes it and focus returns to the summary. Every target in it is at
   least 44 px tall. The Menu now opens something real, so A20 clause 1 holds and clause 2's
   omission of "the mobile menu icon" is lifted. The basket stays omitted (A20: no page, no
   control). AC-7's rule (the reserved height is identical before and after hydration) holds at
   every width. AC-14's crawl covers the menu's links.

   **Why the strip's two notices can leave the phone.** With the strip gone, `nav.utility.pricesInclude`
   ("Prices include delivery and VAT") and `nav.utility.guarantee` ("Fresh-flower promise") are not
   in the phone chrome. That is safe for two reasons:
   - every price on the phone carries its inclusive label beside it (the listing cards'
     `catalog.price.inclusive`, clause 2 (a), and the product page's "Includes VAT and delivery"),
     so the all-in statement sits next to each number it qualifies;
   - the promise stays on the product page (spec 009 §14 A11), in the footer once spec 041
     publishes the guarantee page, and on that page itself.

   If either condition stops holding, the line returns to the Menu.

4. **The phone patterns are system rules (new AC-48 to AC-50).** They are built once in
   `src/modules/ui` and drawn in round-2 `system/components.dc.html`. "The phone" means below `md`,
   the same split as clause 2 of A23.
   - **(a) The sticky primary-action bar (AC-48).** Fixed to the viewport's foot.
     - It is opaque (computed background alpha 1), and its block-end padding includes
       `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`.
     - The page's last block is padded by the bar's block size plus the safe area, so at the
       bottom of the page no content box sits under the bar.
     - **It repeats; it never replaces.** Every action or fact in the bar also exists in the page's
       flow, or the bar is an in-page link to it. With JavaScript off, nothing is lost if the bar
       does not show.
     - A price in the bar comes from the same projection as the visible selected price, and it
       equals it (price shown = price charged; schema price = visible price).
     - The consent sheet and the language popup never cover the bar's action: their boxes do not
       intersect the action's box while both are shown.
     - Showing or hiding the bar moves no layout (CLS 0).
   - **(b) Segmented control (AC-49).** Used for sizes. A `<fieldset>` with a `<legend>` and native
     radio inputs, or links where the owning spec makes each option its own URL (spec 009's
     `?tier=`, TASK-128). The selected option is exposed (`checked` or `aria-current`). Each segment
     is at least 44 px tall. It works with JavaScript off, and it is never colour alone.
   - **(c) Horizontal scroller (AC-49).** Used for the date row, the chip rows and the phone
     gallery. It actually scrolls (`overflow-x: auto`, with scroll snap allowed). The artboards'
     `overflow: hidden` is drawing shorthand and does not bind: every item is reachable by touch
     and by keyboard. A scroller with no focusable child is itself focusable and labelled. The
     page never overflows sideways (AC-43). Nothing auto-advances, and no element carries
     `aria-roledescription="carousel"` (AC-36). The phone gallery's dots are drawn only as real
     in-page links to each image, each with a target of at least 24 × 24 px; otherwise no dots are
     drawn (A20: nothing looks clickable and does nothing).
   - **(d) The "sheet" product layout (AC-46).** The photograph on top, and a paper sheet rising
     24–28 px over its foot, holding the name and price side by side, the segmented sizes and the
     date scroller. It is static layout and needs no JavaScript. **No grip handle** is drawn: a
     grip invites a drag that does nothing (A20 clause 1). The building task records the
     difference in `docs/design/README.md` (open item (iv)).
   - **(e) The back link (AC-32 amended, AC-50).** Below `md`, every page that has a breadcrumb
     shows one `‹ Parent` back link in its place: a link to the trail's last ancestor, named by
     that ancestor's breadcrumb label. Its chevron is an icon that flips under `mirror-in-rtl`,
     not a literal character in copy. The product page shows no back link and displays its full
     trail below the buy section instead, a binding its phone artboard omits (clause 1). At any
     one width **exactly one** of the trail and
     the back link is displayed; the other is `display: none`, out of the accessibility tree.
     **SEO is unchanged.** The full trail stays in the server HTML at every width, displayed from
     `md` up, and `BreadcrumbList` equals that trail item for item (spec 007 AC-15, spec 008 AC-17
     and spec 041 AC-19 read "the visible breadcrumb" as this trail). AC-32's 20 px holds from
     `md` up. Below `md` the back link's box top is 14 ± 1 px below the header's bottom edge (the
     phone artboards' `padding-block-start`), and its box is at least 44 px tall.
   - **(f) AC-31 skips full-bleed media.** An element whose inline size equals the viewport's (the
     home photograph, the phone cover, the phone product photograph, a bleeding chip row) is not
     "the first block". The probe measures the first block after it: on home A that is the white
     card, at x 56 at 1440 and x 20 at 390.

5. **The photograph bounds: the exemptions from clause 9 (amends A23 clause 9 "The vertical
   rhythm" and AC-41's third bullet; AC-36 amended; new AC-46).** `--hero-photo-max` keeps binding
   the country occasion's lead figure, the hub lead figures and every other hero or lead
   photograph. Two images are exempt, and each gets its own bound:
   - **The home hero.**
     - Desktop (option A): full-bleed under the chrome. Its block-start edge is the chrome's
       block-end edge, its block size is at least 560 px, and its block-end edge is at or above
       the viewport's bottom edge at 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 and 1512 × 945. In other words it is
       at most 100 svh minus the chrome and fills the first screen.
     - It alone may crop with the window (`object-fit: cover`). Its `object-position` keeps the
       flowers inside the visible crop.
     - Phone (option 1): the header, the cover and the docked bar together equal the viewport at
       390 × 844 (65 + 703 + 76), and the cover is at least 420 px tall at 360 × 640.
   - **The product gallery.**
     - Desktop: the main image is a full 4∶5 on columns 2–7. Its block size is 1.25 × its inline
       size (± 1 px), it is never taller than the viewport at 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 and
       1512 × 945, and its top edge lies inside the first viewport. The buy column's H1, price,
       size control and primary action (Phase 0: the status pill) lie fully inside the viewport at
       those three sizes.
     - This replaces the brief's suggested bound ("the viewport height minus the header at
       1280 × 800") because the approved artboard fails it by design: 712 px against 681.
     - Phone: the photograph is `min(470px, 100svh − 300px)`, and at least 300 px. At 390 × 844
       the H1, the price and the size control lie inside the viewport above the bar's top edge.
   - **The priority image, per width (AC-36 amended).** At each of 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844,
     exactly one **displayed** `<img>` carries `fetchpriority="high"`, and exactly one
     `<link rel="preload">` whose `media` matches that width names it. It is the image painted in
     that width's first viewport:
     - on a country page, `items[0]`'s photograph at both widths;
     - on a hub, the lead figure's photograph from `md` up, and the first grid card's below `md`;
     - on page 2 and later, the first card's photograph.

     An image that is not displayed at a width is not downloaded at that width.
   - **Budgets are not raised.** Image transfer ≤ 204 800 B, LCP under 2 000 ms and CLS under 0.05
     (AC-24) hold on every page these exemptions touch.

6. **The 404 (AC-40 rebound).** The not-found document draws no stamp, no postmark and no airmail
   edge. Its one decoration is the faint dashed route: an `aria-hidden` inline SVG behind the
   letter, static as TASK-191 builds it, and drawn in once by motion 13 (TASK-194). The document
   fills the first viewport (its minimum block size is the viewport minus the header). A23 open
   item (iv) is closed: round 2 removed the edge, so the spec and the artboard agree.

7. **The airmail edge (A21 clause 2; T-1).** Round 2 draws it on the home's white card and on the
   footer. The product page has no recap any more. T-1's "referenced by exactly three components"
   now reads "**at most three, and each is one of A21 clause 2's named places**". It goes red with
   a component outside those places.

8. **Gutters.** Below `md` the gutter stays 20 px (A23 clause 2, AC-43), and round 2 draws 20.
   **16 px is not adopted**, although a design brief asked for it.

9. **Motion (amends A23 clause 11, AC-44 and AC-6; owned by TASK-194).** All thirteen motions of
   `system/motion.dc.html` are approved (founder, 2026-10-05). Clause 11 stands, with these
   changes:
   - **(a) Tokens.** `--duration-draw: 600ms` and `--duration-bloom: 1200ms` land in
     `src/app/globals.css` and `docs/design/system/tokens.css` together.
   - **(b) Stroke drawing is allowed.** Besides `transform` and `opacity`, an inline SVG path may
     animate `stroke-dashoffset` (with a fixed `stroke-dasharray`). It is paint-only and moves no
     layout. Motions 1, 5 and 13 use it.
   - **(c) Reduced motion (AC-6 now reads this way).** Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`:
     - no transform, position or stroke changes over time;
     - no animation iterates;
     - the only change over time allowed is an opacity transition of 120 ms or less (motion 10's
       reduced form);
     - every other motion's reduced form is static, as its cell on the sheet says.
   - **(d) JavaScript.** One small client module serves motions 1, 3, 4 and 12. It only toggles
     classes and the `aria-busy` and `disabled` states. Its bytes are measured inside AC-25's
     budget (A1's 131 072 B). The budget is not raised: if the module does not fit, the task
     escalates with the numbers.
   - **(e) Built now:** motions 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 12 and 13, the ones with a Phase 0
     trigger. **Motions 2** (skeletons: no client fetch exists), **8** (petals: no basket) **and
     9** (the confirmation stamp: no confirmation page) have no Phase 0 trigger. Their CSS ships
     with the task that creates their trigger (specs 009 and 010), under the same rules. Nothing
     unreachable ships now.
   - **(f) No loader on a first load.** No route-level loading fallback may reach the HTML of a
     first load, including a parameterised listing response (`?page=2`) that renders per request.
   - **(g) Motion 12** applies its hidden start state only after its class is on `<html>`, and
     never to a section that intersects the first viewport at load.

10. **Copy introduced by round 2: founder copy pending.** None of these strings is approved. Each
    ships `reviewed: false` (or, in a spec 041 content file, in a file marked `reviewed: false`)
    in the task named below. The founder attests it with their own `record-approval.py` run, and
    no agent sets `reviewed: true`. No string here has an em dash. The `de`/`pl` values are
    machine drafts with no em dash.

    | Id | English text (proposed) | Where | Ships in |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | P1 | Read more | the product description's disclosure | TASK-197 |
    | P2 | Rose bouquet · Poland (the pattern "{category} · {country}") | the product phone sheet's eyebrow | TASK-197 |
    | P3 | Next page · Page {page} of {total} | the phone listing pager | TASK-187 |
    | P4 | Seven countries in Europe, each with a guide we wrote ourselves. | the destinations hub's phone line (desktop keeps `destinationsHub.intro`) | TASK-188 |
    | P5 | No price here. The price depends on the destination. | `shop.card.noPrice` (replaces the em-dash string) | TASK-188 |
    | P6 | Message us any time, 24/7. We reply within a few hours. | `company.support.hours` (replaces the em-dash string; spec 041 §14 A1) | TASK-182 |
    | P7 | WhatsApp {phone} | the phone dock on help, guarantee and delivery | TASK-182 |
    | P8 | We will open them one at a time. · Until a country opens, it has a guide and no dates. · and will open them one at a time, starting with Poland. · Not yet. We will open one country at a time. | the trust-page replacement lines (spec 041 §14 A1 lists each one with the sentence it replaces; the about page's second proposed sentence, "We will open each country in turn.", is dropped because it repeats the first) | TASK-182 |
    | P9 | In force | the cookies page's corner stamp | TASK-183 |
    | P10 | None yet. | `corridor.facts.orderBy.none`, the replacement A23 open item (vii) asks for | TASK-189 (TASK-193 proposes it in its PR; whichever merges second rebases; the exception-list entry leaves only when the founder approves) |
    | P11 | Step 1 of 3 · Recipient | the checkout phone stepper | no task (spec 010 is not written) |
    | P12 | Default | the popup's English mark (spec 003 §14 A16) | TASK-119 (listed here only) |

    - **The printed card.** B1 approved "printed on **a** card". The shipped string and the
      artboards say "printed on **our** card". AC-38 asserts B1's approved text, so B1 wins until
      the founder picks the other one (open item (ii)).
    - **For florists.** The two-line lede and "Agreed in advance" wait for spec 011 and are not
      shipped by any task here.

11. **Rollout: four new tasks, all blocked until PR 189 merges.**
    - **TASK-194 (motion).** Clause 9 here, A23 clause 11, AC-44 and T-48. It runs after
      TASK-196, TASK-197, TASK-195, TASK-191 and TASK-119, because the motions touch their pages.
    - **TASK-195 (the phone chrome and the phone primitives).** Clauses 3 and 4: the minimal
      header, the Menu sheet, the sticky bar, the segmented control, the horizontal scroller and
      the back link. It owns AC-47 to AC-50 and the phone half of AC-32. It comes after TASK-186
      (the frame) and TASK-176 (the v2 chrome), and before every task that uses its primitives:
      TASK-187, TASK-188, TASK-189, TASK-191, TASK-196, TASK-197 and TASK-182 to TASK-184.
    - **TASK-196 (home v3).** Desktop A and phone cover 1, with the AC-46 home half. It comes
      after TASK-186, TASK-195 and TASK-177 (PR 174, the same files).
    - **TASK-197 (product v3).** The two-column desktop and the phone sheet, with the AC-46
      product half. It comes after TASK-186, TASK-195 and TASK-179 (PR 170). It shares files with
      TASK-128, so the two never run at the same time. Its spec 009 ruling is spec 009 §14 A12
      (PR 195; open item (v)), and it dispatches once PR 195 merges.
    - **The trust and legal pages need no new task.** TASK-182 to TASK-184 build them straight to
      the round-2 artboards (spec 041 §14 A1), after TASK-186 and TASK-195.
    - **TASK-190 is released from PR 189.** It withdraws or replaces two catalogue photographs and
      draws nothing, so A23 clause 10's "no build before the round-2 design PR" no longer applies
      to it.

    **Cross-reference: spec 009 §14 A12 (PR 195)** owns the product page v3's behaviour:
    - the block order, with the card message and the add-ons after the primary action;
    - "The price" section, which takes the equivalents line off the price line;
    - the description clamped to three lines with "Read more", and the full text in the server
      HTML with JavaScript off.

    This amendment owns the product page's layout bounds (AC-46), the phone patterns (clause 4)
    and the bindings its artboard omits (clause 1). Where the two touch, A12 governs behaviour and
    content, and A24 governs geometry.

**Unchanged, and still binding:**
- the laptop band;
- the H1 first in DOM order on every page;
- pagination (12 products a page, no hero on page 2 and later, every product reachable);
- honesty (A23 clause 7, clause 12, AC-37);
- tokens only, logical properties only, no literal strings;
- server rendering with zero new islands, except the motion module of clause 9 (d);
- every budget.

**SEO.** No URL, canonical, robots rule, hreflang, sitemap entry or JSON-LD changes.
`BreadcrumbList` keeps the full trail (clause 4 (e)). `ItemList` still equals the rendered cards
in order, one element per product (clause 2 (b)). The pager keeps every page's `<a href>` in the
HTML at every width. Below `md` it displays the compact form, with a previous link from page 2
on (spec 008 §14 A16). Every indexable word stays in the server HTML. The menu, the trail and the
per-card labels are hidden only by CSS at a width, never removed from the HTML.

**i18n.** Clause 10's strings are message keys with machine `de`/`pl` drafts, `reviewed: false`,
and the unreviewed share stays under spec 003's 5 % gate. The back-link chevron flips in RTL.
Segmented controls and scrollers use logical properties, and the date scroller starts at the
inline-start edge.

**Compliance.**
- Price shown = price charged in the bar (clause 4 (a)) and on every first-row card (clause 2).
- The AI-image label stays visible on the first screen of every grid (spec 008 §14 A16; spec 006
  AC-17).
- The ranking disclosure stays on the page and visible (clause 2 (e); spec 008 §8).
- WCAG 2.2 AA: 44 px targets in the phone chrome, scrollers reachable by keyboard, the H1 on the
  phone cover at least 3∶1 and its supporting line at least 4.5∶1 (AC-46).
- No new personal data and no RoPA change.

Acceptance criteria (each test is watched red by mutating its subject):
- **Rebound AC-34 — the country first screens (TASK-187).** On page 1 of `/en-gb/poland/flowers`,
  `/en-gb/poland/flowers/roses` and `/en-gb/poland/occasions/mothers-day`, at 1440 × 900,
  1280 × 800 and 390 × 844:
  - these lie fully inside the first viewport:
    - the H1;
    - the state line, equal to `listingView().country.state`'s message;
    - on the shop root and the country category, every card of the grid's first row, from the
      top of its photograph to the bottom of its price;
    - on the country occasion page, the date and `items[0]`'s price, and at 390 × 844 the rest of
      the grid's first row too;
  - the H1 precedes the first price in DOM order;
  - the shop root and the country category render no lead figure, and their first grid card is
    `items[0]`;
  - each price string equals that card's `formatMoney` output, and at every width each card's
    price has `catalog.price.inclusive` beside it in the same card (spec 008 AC-6);
  - at 390 × 844 the state line sits under the H1, although the phone artboards omit it
    (clause 1);
  - the date text equals `formatDate`'s value (or the parts of that one value);
  - no first-viewport text contains a phrase AC-37 forbids;
  - page 1 holds 12 products, `items[0]` to `items[11]`, each product's URL once in `<main>`;
  - page 2 renders no hero.
- **AC-45 — calm, not bland (state and gaps: TASK-187 for the three country types, TASK-188 for
  the four hubs, TASK-189 for the guide).** On the seven listing types and the guide, at
  1440 × 900, 1280 × 800 and 390 × 844:
  - **no empty vertical gap over 120 px.** Take the boxes of every rendered text node, image and
    form control in `<main>` that lie between the header's bottom edge and the viewport's bottom
    edge. No band of that span taller than 120 px is free of all of them, including the bands at
    its top and bottom.
  - **no ornament.** Inside that span, no element has a computed rotation other than 0, and no
    `aria-hidden` element is at least 48 px in both inline and block size (icons inside controls
    are smaller, and the photographs are not `aria-hidden`).
- **AC-46 — the home and product first screens (home half TASK-196, product half TASK-197).**
  - **Home.**
    - At 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 and 1512 × 945 on `/en-gb`: the photograph's block-start edge
      equals the chrome's block-end edge (± 1 px); its block-end edge is at or above the
      viewport's bottom edge; its block size is at least 560 px; the white card, with the H1, the
      sentence and Continue, lies fully inside the viewport.
    - At 390 × 844: the header, the cover and the bar together are 844 ± 1 px; the H1 and its
      line lie inside the cover; Send flowers lies in the bar.
    - Computed over pure white under the fade (the worst case), the H1 meets at least 3∶1 and
      the supporting line at least 4.5∶1. This is a unit test on the fade token and the two text
      tokens.
    - At 360 × 640 the cover is at least 420 px tall.
  - **Product.**
    - At 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 and 1512 × 945 on `/en-gb/poland/product/amber-hour`: the main
      image's block size is 1.25 × its inline size (± 1 px) and at most the viewport's; its top
      edge is inside the viewport; the H1, the price, the size control and the primary action lie
      fully inside the viewport.
    - At 390 × 844: the photograph is `min(470, 844 − 300)` px; the H1, the price and the size
      control lie inside the viewport above the bar; the sheet draws no grip element; the
      sheet renders `product.demo.body` (N2) under the status pill, although the artboard omits
      it (clause 1).
- **AC-47 — the phone chrome (TASK-195).** At 390 × 844 and 360 × 640 on the AC-41 page set, the
  product page and a trust page:
  - the header is 65 ± 1 px and holds exactly two controls, the logo link and the Menu summary;
  - no utility-strip element is displayed;
  - with JavaScript disabled, activating Menu reveals the published header links, Send flowers,
    the four locale links (spec 003's `LocaleSwitcher` markup), the currency chip (AC-8's text and
    label) and the help line, each target at least 44 px tall;
  - with JavaScript enabled, `Esc` closes the menu and focus returns to the summary;
  - at 1440 × 900 the utility strip and the full header render as A4 and A7 describe them;
  - the header's box is identical before and after hydration at both widths (AC-7);
  - spec 003 AC-3 and this spec's AC-8 still pass.
- **AC-48 — the sticky action bar (TASK-195 for the primitive; asserted on the pages of TASK-196,
  TASK-197, TASK-182 and TASK-183).** Wherever a bar renders at 390 × 844:
  - its computed background alpha is 1;
  - its block-end padding includes `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` (asserted from the stylesheet,
    and by emulating a 34 px inset);
  - scrolled to the bottom, no text node's box intersects the bar's box;
  - each link or action in the bar has a twin in the page's flow, or is an in-page link to one,
    and with JavaScript disabled that twin is reachable;
  - any price in the bar equals the visible selected price;
  - with the consent sheet or the language popup open, neither box intersects the bar's action;
  - CLS is 0 when the bar shows or hides.
- **AC-49 — segmented controls and horizontal scrollers (TASK-195; used by TASK-187, TASK-188 and
  TASK-197).**
  - A segmented control is a `<fieldset>` with a `<legend>` and native radios (or links where
    the owning spec makes each option a URL). It exposes the selection by `checked` or
    `aria-current`, and each segment is at least 44 px tall.
  - A horizontal scroller has computed `overflow-x` of `auto` or `scroll`. Its last item can be
    scrolled into view by touch and by keyboard. If it has no focusable child it carries
    `tabindex="0"` and an accessible name.
  - `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` equals the viewport width.
  - Gallery dots, where drawn, are `<a href="#…">` links with targets of at least 24 × 24 px.
  - axe reports no `scrollable-region-focusable` violation.
- **AC-50 — the back link and the trail (TASK-195).** On every page type with a breadcrumb:
  - at 390 × 844 exactly one of the trail and the back link is displayed. On every type but the
    product page it is the back link: its `href` and its text equal the trail's last ancestor,
    and its chevron is mirrored under `dir="rtl"` (`/ar-XB`). On the product page the trail is
    displayed below the buy section;
  - at 1440 × 900 the trail is displayed and the back link is not;
  - at both widths the trail is in the server HTML, and `BreadcrumbList` equals it item for item
    in order and label (spec 007 AC-15, spec 008 AC-17, spec 041 AC-19);
  - below `md` the back link's top edge is 14 ± 1 px below the header's bottom edge, and its box
    is at least 44 px tall (AC-32's phone half).
