implemented by TASK-186 to TASK-191, TASK-182 to TASK-184, TASK-119 and TASK-194 to TASK-197.

**The founder's rulings of 2026-10-14.** The founder's replies in chat, verbatim: "R ok, D1–D5, E7
ok, map 20, partner ok, italic ok", then "D6 ok, C1–C5 ok". Each approves the orchestrator's pick
named in the amendment below. The replies reached the spec writer through the orchestrator's work
order (`.claude/state/wo-spec-rulings-1014b.md`); the founder confirms these texts when they
approve the docs PR that carries them. Context: the site test of 2026-10-14
(`.claude/state/qa-2026-10-14/SUMMARY.md`, and QA-3's design report).

**A-founder-2026-10-14c — The card message limit is 200 characters everywhere (D5 A; A21 clause 5
L820–L832; spec 009 §14 A12; spec 010 L50, §5.2 `CardAndBuyerStepSchema`, §13 Q9; new AC-51,
T-56).**
Original: A21 clause 5 sets no limit on the product page's card `<textarea>`. The built page
counts to 400 (`CARD_MESSAGE_MAX` in `ProductPage.tsx`); spec 010 allows 200 graphemes at checkout
(`src/config/checkout.ts`). The site test found the two disagreeing.
Corrected: one limit, **200 characters counted as graphemes** (an emoji or "ł" is one), the
definition spec 010 §13 Q9 already uses. The product page's counter and its limit read the same
constant as checkout, so the two cannot drift; the PDP keeps no limit of its own. The counter shows
the grapheme count against 200, and the island does not accept a 201st grapheme. Spec 010 agrees
and does not change. A21 clause 5's other rules stand: the `<textarea>` has no `name`, the text is
never stored, submitted or put in a URL, and the island's 2,048 B budget holds.
- **AC-51** The PDP's card limit and checkout's card limit are the same exported constant, equal
  to 200. On a PDP, typing 200 graphemes (including "ł" and a multi-code-point emoji) is accepted
  and the counter reads 200 of 200; a 201st grapheme is not added. No PDP source contains 400 as a
  card limit.

| ID | Layer | Given / When / Then | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-56 | unit + e2e | Unit: the PDP's limit and `src/config/checkout.ts`'s are one constant equal to 200. E2E at 390: 199 ASCII characters plus one family emoji reads 200 and is accepted; one more grapheme is refused. Red with the PDP constant set back to 400 | AC-51 |

**A-founder-2026-10-14d — The italic poppy phrase is marked in the message text (italic ok; A21
clause 3 L766–L793; A23 clause 6 L1217–L1223; QA-3 D-01; new AC-52, T-57).**
Original: A23 clause 6 says "Asterisks in Appendix A mark the artboards' italic; that is
presentation, not text", and nothing carried the italic into the build. QA-3 D-01: the artboards
draw one phrase per heading in Fraunces italic 300, poppy (sunflower on the phone cover), and the
built home and product pages print every heading in roman.
Corrected:
1. **The marker.** A heading whose artboard draws the italic phrase carries an `<em>` tag pair
   around exactly that phrase in its message value, rendered with next-intl's `t.rich` into an
   `<em>`. The words are unchanged, character for character. Today's list, from QA-3 D-01: on the
   home, "far away.", "choices", "sending for", "occasion?", "None of them is a courier.",
   "promise", "ask first" and "send flowers"; on the product page, "our order". The implementing
   PR names each key and phrase. At most one `<em>` pair per heading, and no `<em>` outside a
   heading's value.
2. **The look.** `h1 em` and `h2 em` render in the Fraunces italic face of clause 3 at 300, in the
   poppy token (sunflower on the phone cover), from tokens only. If clause 3's fallback has cut the
   italic file, the phrase renders in the roman at the lightest weight shipped, as clause 3 says.
3. **The approval survives.** Inserting the `<em>` pair is a marker-only change and **keeps the
   founder's copy approval**: the founder approved the words, and the asterisks in Appendix A
   already marked the phrase. A value that was `reviewed: true` stays reviewed with the same
   `reviewedBy` and `reviewedAt`; only its `sourceHash` is recomputed. The exception is exactly this:
   the value with its `<em>` and `</em>` removed equals the base value byte for byte. Any other
   change in the same value is an ordinary edit and needs a new approval. The founder's
   `record-approval` exact-match comparison reads the value with the two tags removed.
4. **Other locales.** Each `de` and `pl` value of these keys carries one `<em>` pair around the
   phrase's translation (spec 003 §14 A15 clause 6 drafting duty), and `i18n:check` treats the tag
   set like the argument set: a locale whose value lacks the pair, or adds another, fails.
- **AC-52** On `/en-gb` and an `en-gb` product page at 1440 and 390, each heading of clause 1's
  list contains exactly one `<em>` whose text equals its phrase, with the italic face (or clause
  3's fallback) and the poppy token computed; no other heading contains an `<em>`. For every key
  changed, the base value equals the new value with `<em>` and `</em>` removed, and its
  `reviewed`, `reviewedBy` and `reviewedAt` are unchanged.

| ID | Layer | Given / When / Then | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-57 | unit + e2e | Unit: for each listed key, tag-stripped value equals the base value and the review meta is byte-identical; `de`/`pl` values carry one pair; `i18n:check` fails a fixture `de` value without the pair. E2E: the `em` text and computed style per heading at both widths. Red when one word inside a marked value changes (the tag-stripped comparison fails) and when `t.rich` is replaced by `t` (the tags print as text) | AC-52 |

artboards** to drop the price, "all in" and the equivalents line, and records it in
`docs/design/README.md`. Whether the "Example arrangement" note shows on the home cards is spec
006 AC-17's rule and is unchanged.
- **AC-53** On every locale home at 1440 and 390, no "Popular choices" card contains a currency

**A-founder-2026-10-15a — The home `<title>` has no em dash (spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-15a;

**Corrected.**

|---|---|---|---|

Resolved by the founder (2026-10-15, chat, verbatim "specs ok (237 and 240), dashes yes, prices: your picks"): the proposed wording is the target; it still ships `reviewed: false` until the founder's record run (spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-15a, resolution 2).

**A-founder-2026-10-15b — Non-critical client chunks wait for the LCP paint (§9 AC-24; §14 A1,
A12, A18; A23 clause 11; TASK-235 escalation E1, option (a); new AC-55, T-60).**
The founder's reply, 2026-10-15, in chat, verbatim: "go with option a". It answers escalation E1
of `docs/tasks/TASK-235.md` (branch `task/TASK-235-lighthouse-warmup`). The reply reached the spec
writer through the orchestrator; the founder confirms this text when they approve the docs PR that
carries it.

**Original.** AC-24 requires LCP < 2,000 ms on every audited URL, measured on the Brotli origin
(A12), warmed (A18), and A18 forbids weakening the assertion. Nothing in this spec says when a
page's async client chunks may load relative to its first paint.

**Measured (TASK-235 `## Result`, C1 to C3).**
- The home and listing pages sit 50 to 250 ms under the budget in every one of 30 CI runs, and
  `/en-gb` failed all three runs of CI run 38592098548 (2,042, 2,023 and 2,062 ms). The cause is
  not a cold path, so TASK-235's warm-up cannot move it.
- On the photo homes the LCP element is the hero `img` (AC-46), and its simulated LCP is the
  simulated FCP plus about 450 ms.
- Lighthouse's simulation counts an async chunk that finishes before the first paint as an LCP
  dependency. With a faster origin, the chunks finished first and LCP went to 2,474 to 2,920 ms
  in six of nine runs. So the page's margin depends on a race between its scripts and its paint.

**Corrected.**
1. **The rule.** On every page, a client chunk that is not needed to paint the LCP element is
   neither requested nor finished before the LCP element has painted. It loads after that paint,
   on idle, or on the first interaction with its control; the implementer chooses the mechanism
   and records it in the brief. "Needed" means the framework runtime and whatever the server
   HTML's first screen cannot paint without. Today the LCP element needs no application island.
2. **Which chunks.** The task declares the deferred set in one place in `src/modules/ui`, and a
   unit test reads it. The set includes at least: the motions (A23 clause 11, TASK-194), the
   first-visit language popup (spec 003 §14 A14 as A16 amends it), the header and phone Menu
   enhancements (A24), and every other island that is not on the first screen's paint path.
   - **The consent banner is not deferred by this amendment.** If its chunk is found in the race,
     the task escalates instead of delaying consent UI. No tag fires before consent either way
     (AC-20).
3. **What must not change.**
   - The measurement basis: A12's Brotli origin, A18's warm runs, the URL set, `numberOfRuns`,
     the throttling and the aggregation. TASK-235's warm-up stands, and TASK-103's later move to
     the median does not change this rule.
   - The first paint looks the same: the visual baselines taken at first paint do not change.
   - No indexable content moves client-side. Every text and link a crawler reads is in the server
     HTML.
   - The 131,072 B Brotli client-JS budget (A1) holds, and CLS stays < 0.05: a deferred popup or
     motion lays nothing out in the flow and shifts nothing.
   - Every control that worked without JavaScript still does (a link, a form, a `<details>`).
     With JavaScript on, each control works once its chunk has loaded. A tap before that is
     honoured, either by the native element or by loading the chunk and acting on the tap.
4. **Who follows it.** TASK-194 (motions, PR 221) and the language popup's task build to this
   rule before they merge, and every later island does too. A merged island that breaks it is a
   new task row, not a reopened one.
5. **SEO, i18n, compliance.** LCP is a ranking input, and this protects it without changing the
   assertion. No message key changes. No data flow changes. Consent is unchanged (clause 2).

- **AC-55** On the built site, for `/en`, `/en-gb`, `/de`, `/pl` and every other URL in
  `tests/fixtures/seo/lighthouse-urls.json`, under Lighthouse's mobile emulation and throttling:
  - no chunk in the declared deferred set has a resource-timing `startTime` before the page's
    largest-contentful-paint entry's render time, and the set includes the motions, the language
    popup and the Menu enhancements;
  - the `lighthouse` job's asserted LCP is < 2,000 ms on every audited URL, on the shipping PR's
    head and in three `ci:full` runs whose per-URL numbers are recorded in the task's `## Result`;
  - the script budget (A1), CLS < 0.05 and the first-paint visual baselines are unchanged, and the
    JavaScript-off e2e cases of the home, the Menu and the language links pass unchanged.

| ID | Layer | Given / When / Then | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-60 | e2e (Playwright, built site, CI) + unit + Lighthouse CI | Unit: the deferred set lists the motions, the popup and the Menu. E2e: on each audited URL, a `PerformanceObserver` for `largest-contentful-paint` and `performance.getEntriesByType("resource")` show every deferred chunk starting after the LCP render time; a tap on Menu and on the language control before idle still opens it. The `lighthouse` job's LCP table from three `ci:full` runs. Red when the language popup's module is imported eagerly again (its chunk starts before the LCP) | AC-55 |

Open for the founder: none. The mechanism is the implementer's, inside clause 1.

**A-founder-2026-10-18a: The product page's script budget is 133,120 B (§14 A1, A12, A13; A21
clause 3 "Unchanged"; A-founder-2026-10-15b clause 3; AC-24, AC-25; spec 009 §14
A-founder-2026-10-18a; new AC-56, T-61).**
Status: approved (founder, 2026-10-18). The founder's reply, 2026-10-18 ~05:50Z, in chat,
verbatim: "approve..". It approves the orchestrator's pick 1, in the orchestrator's words: "raise
the product-page budget by 2 KB, and fix the defect where category pages load the product page's
script". The reply reached the spec writer through the orchestrator's work order.

**Evidence.** The escalations of TASK-228 (PR 239, head `27be31fd`) and TASK-194 (PR 221, head
`8b2edade`), recorded in `docs/tasks/TASK-228.md` and `docs/tasks/TASK-194.md` on their branches.

**Original.** A1 sets ≤131,072 B Brotli script transfer for every measured URL and says the budget
"is not raised a second time"; A21 clause 3 and A-founder-2026-10-15b clause 3 restate it. On main
(`d6b5fa8f`) no product URL is in `pnpm budget:client-js`'s measured set (`DEFAULT_URLS`,
`scripts/client-js-budget.ts`) or in `tests/fixtures/seo/lighthouse-urls.json`; spec 009 AC-14
(T-14) and AC-26 name the product page's budget and its Lighthouse URLs.

**Measured.** The product page ships 130,950 B on main: 122 B free. Three founder-asked changes add
to it: +540 B in the product island (the printed-card grapheme counter that counts as checkout
counts, and every price following the chosen size; TASK-228, TASK-229, TASK-230), +504 B for the
approved motions (A23 clause 11, A24 clause 9; TASK-194), and +26 B of module-id growth. That is
about 132,020 B. The island itself stays inside its own 2,048 B (A21 clause 5). Separately, the
country category (`/en/poland/flowers/roses`) loads the product page's island, because one route
file, `src/app/[locale]/[segment]/[child]/[grandchild]/page.tsx`, serves both page types.

**Corrected.**
1. **Product pages** (spec 009's PDP URL, `plan/02` §4): ≤**133,120 B** Brotli script transfer
   (131,072 + 2,048). **Every other page type keeps 131,072 B**, including the listings, the hubs,
   the homes, the guide and the checkout (spec 010 AC-38, spec 013 AC-2).
2. **No further raise.** A1's "not raised a second time" now reads "not raised again beyond this
   one product-page step". Growth past 133,120 B escalates with the numbers.
3. **Where it is enforced.** `pnpm budget:client-js` (A13, AC-25) gains
   `/en/poland/product/amber-hour` and `/en-gb/poland/product/amber-hour` with a per-route budget
   of 133,120 B, and baselines for them under the 5 KB regression guard; every other route keeps
   `CLIENT_JS_BUDGET_BYTES`. This is spec 009 T-14's build-output measurement. `lighthouserc.json`
   gains a per-URL script assertion of 133,120 B that matches product URLs only, beside the global
   131,072 B; it binds as soon as spec 009 AC-26's product URLs join the Lighthouse set, and until
   then the build-output number is the gate.
4. **The defect is fixed with it.** No page except the product page loads the product island, so
   the country category and the country occasion keep 131,072 B (spec 009 §14
   A-founder-2026-10-18a, AC-37).
5. **Unchanged.** The island's 2,048 B and its no-network rule (A21 clause 5, spec 009 AC-14),
   A-founder-2026-10-15b's deferral rule, LCP < 2,000 ms, CLS < 0.05, the image and font budgets.

**SEO.** Script weight feeds CWV. 2,048 B is about 1.5 % of the product page's script, and the LCP
assertion does not move. Every Phase 0 product page is `noindex,follow` (spec 009 §14
A-founder-2026-10-15a clause 5). The listings, which carry the crawl, lose bytes when the defect
is fixed. **i18n.** No key changes. **Compliance.** No data flow, price display or consent change;
the RoPA is unchanged.

- **AC-56** On the build of the shipping PR's head, `pnpm budget:client-js` measures
  `/en/poland/product/amber-hour` and `/en-gb/poland/product/amber-hour` against 133,120 B and every
  other measured URL against 131,072 B, and passes. `lighthouserc.json` asserts
  `resource-summary:script:size` ≤ 133,120 only on URLs matching the product path and ≤ 131,072 on
  every other URL.

| ID | Layer | Given / When / Then | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-61 | unit (build output + config) | The script's budget per route and `lighthouserc.json` parsed (beside `tests/unit/lighthouse-budgets.test.ts`). Red with a product route fixture at 133,121 B, red with a listing route given 133,120 B, and red with the product assertion's URL pattern widened to match `/poland/flowers/roses` | AC-56 |

**Measured (E-4).** First-row price foot at 390 × 844, local production build (`.env.example`, FX
off, load about 5). CI's Linux text runs wider, so CI's build is the gate of record.

| Page at 390 × 844 | As built (44 px H1) | H1 at 32 px | Artboard |
|---|---|---|---|
| shop root `/en-gb/poland/flowers` | 865.7 | 808.8 | 808 |
| country category `/en-gb/poland/flowers/roses` | 809.7 | 752.8 | 808 |
| country category `/de/…/rosen` | 833.5 | 776.6 | n/a |
| country occasion (H1 not in 18b) | 838.3 | 838.3 | 786 |

On the occasion the note costs 50 px (gap included), so with the note after the grid the first row
ends near 788. The toolbar matches the artboards within 4 px; the whole difference is in the hero.

**Corrected.**
1. **The phone band.** "The phone" is below `md`, A24 clause 4's split (the same as A23 clause
   2). From `md` up, A-founder-2026-10-18b stands unchanged.
2. **The H1 below `md`.** On the shop root (`/{locale}/{country}/{shopCategory}`) and the country
   category (`/{locale}/{country}/{shopCategory}/{category}`), in every locale, the H1 takes
   `--text-3xl-fluid` below `md` and `--text-title-fluid` from `md` up. Clause 9 defines
   `--text-3xl-fluid` as `clamp(32px, min(3.6vw, 5.8vh), 46px)`; its preferred term stays under
   32 px at every width below 889 px, so below `md` it resolves to its 32 px minimum, the
   artboard's size.

   | Step on these two H1s | Band | 360 × 640 | 390 × 844 | 767 × 1024 | 768 × 1024 | 1280 × 800 | 1512 × 945 |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | `--text-3xl-fluid` | below `md` | 32 | 32 | 32 | | | |
   | `--text-title-fluid` | from `md` up | | | | 44 | 53.8 | 58 |

   - No token value changes (AC-42, T-44 unchanged), and AC-43's "each fluid step resolves to its
     unchanged minimum" holds. The H1 keeps its line height and tracking; only its step changes.
   - Every other page in AC-41's set keeps its step, the country occasion's H1 included.
3. **The country occasion's note below `md`.** On page 1 of the country occasion
   (`/{locale}/{country}/{occasions}/{occasion}`), below `md`, N1 renders after the grid's last
   card, and before the pager where one renders, as `country-occasion-mobile` draws it. It does not
   render in the hero below `md`. From `md` up its place in the hero is unchanged. Page 2 and later
   render no hero and no note, as today (spec 008 §14 A15 (a)).
   - At every width exactly one note is displayed and exactly one is in the accessibility tree. It
     keeps `role="note"`, its accessible name `shop.note.label` and its text, N1's resolved value.
   - The mechanism is the implementer's, recorded in the brief: one element placed by layout, or one
     render per band with the other `display: none`. In each band the DOM order equals the visual
     order. A displayed copy is never `aria-hidden`, and a hidden copy is never only visually
     hidden.
   - The shop root and the country category keep their note where it is (they fit at 32 px), and
     the hubs render none.
4. **What still binds.**
   - Rebound AC-34 and T-49 are not relaxed. At 390 × 844, on the three URLs, every first-row price
     ends at or above 844, and the occasion's date, `items[0]`'s price and the rest of its first row
     lie inside the first viewport.
   - The state line stays directly under the H1 below `md` (A24 clause 1, rebound AC-34). It is the
     phone's first-screen statement that ordering is not open (spec 007 §14 A-founder-2026-10-14c,
     AC-33). E-4's other lever, dropping the state line, is not taken.
   - If CI's build still puts a first-row price past 844 after this change, the task escalates with
     CI's numbers. No bound is relaxed and no other element moves without a ruling.
5. **Design.** The phone artboards already draw both (the 32 px H1, the note after the grid), so no
   artboard changes. A24 clause 1's `docs/design/README.md` row for the state line stands.

**SEO.** The H1's text, element and DOM place are unchanged; only its computed size below `md`
changes. N1 stays in the server HTML at every width; if it is built as two renders, the one
sentence appears twice in the HTML with one copy `display: none`, a non-heading sentence with no
link or keyword target, and every Phase 0 listing is `noindex,follow`. URLs (plan/02 §4),
canonical, robots, hreflang, sitemaps and JSON-LD are unchanged; rendering stays ISR (plan/01 §3).
The phone first screen gets shorter, which helps LCP, and nothing lays out after load, so CLS is
unchanged. **i18n.** No key changes; N1 keeps its value and review state. The step is a token, so
every locale's H1 takes it alike (`/de/…/rosen` measured 776.6 at 32 px). Logical properties only;
`/ar-XB` mirrors unchanged. **Compliance.** Price shown = price charged is unchanged. Every phone
card keeps `catalog.price.inclusive` beside its price (rebound AC-34, spec 008 AC-6), so the
all-in statement stays on every first-screen price, and the state line keeps the not-open statement
on the first screen. The 32 px H1 is large text (3∶1) and keeps AA contrast and 200 % resize
(§5.3, the px minimum scales with zoom). No data flow changes; the RoPA is unchanged.

**AC-43 amended.** It gains a bullet: "on `/en-gb/poland/flowers` and
`/en-gb/poland/flowers/roses`, the H1's computed `font-size` is 32 px, within 0.5 px".
AC-41 is unchanged (its viewports are the laptop band; 18b's font-size bullet stands).

**T-43 amended.** 18b's 390 × 844 case for `/en-gb/poland/flowers` and
`/en-gb/poland/flowers/roses` expects 32 ± 0.5 px (was 44) and covers AC-43. It gains a band-edge
pair for the same two URLs: 32 ± 0.5 px at 767 × 1024 and 44 ± 0.5 px at 768 × 1024. Red with the
two H1s on `--text-title-fluid` below `md` (44 ≠ 32 at 390), and red with `--text-3xl-fluid` kept
from `md` up (46 ≠ 53.8 at 1280, where `--text-3xl-fluid` reaches its 46 px maximum; 32 ≠ 44 at 768).

**Rebound AC-34 and T-49:** text unchanged. T-49's 390 × 844 shop root and occasion cases are the
fold checks this amendment answers.

- **AC-57** On page 1 of `/en-gb/poland/occasions/mothers-day`, its `de` counterpart and
  a country occasion with more than 12 products, so that a pager renders (today
  `/en-gb/poland/occasions/birthday`, 27 products per spec 008 §14 A15 (a)), in the built site:
  - at 390 × 844 and 360 × 640, exactly one element displaying N1's resolved text is displayed; its
    top edge is at or below the bottom edge of the grid's last card; where a pager renders, the
    note precedes the pager in DOM order and its box ends above the pager's top edge; no element
    between the H1 and the grid displays N1's text;
  - at 1280 × 800 and 1440 × 900, exactly one element displaying N1's text is displayed; it
    precedes the grid in DOM order and its box ends above the count line's top edge (its place in
    the hero, unchanged);
  - at every one of those sizes the accessibility tree holds exactly one `note` named by
    `shop.note.label`, and its text is N1's resolved value.

| ID | Layer | Given / When / Then | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-62 | e2e (built site, CI) + a11y | AC-57's three URLs at 390 × 844, 360 × 640, 1280 × 800 and 1440 × 900: count of displayed N1 elements, their boxes against the grid's last card, the pager and the count line, DOM order against the pager, and the accessibility snapshot's `note` count and name; axe clean. Red with the note put back in the hero below `md` (its box above the grid), red with a second copy displayed below `md` (two notes), and red with the displayed phone copy `aria-hidden` (no `note` in the tree) | AC-57 |

Open for the founder: none. The mechanism of clause 3 is the implementer's, inside its bullets.

**A-founder-2026-10-19a: TASK-194's motion module may merge before it waits for the LCP paint;
TASK-244 brings it under the rule (A-founder-2026-10-15b clauses 1, 2 and 4; AC-55, T-60
amended; §9 AC-24, T-26 unchanged and binding).**
Status: approved (founder, 2026-10-19). The founder's reply, 2026-10-19 morning, in chat,
verbatim: "A ship, approve batch 5, C A, all A". Its "A ship" answers the orchestrator's question,
in the orchestrator's words: "Animations load timing. The spec says the animation code loads only
after the main image shows; that piece waits for the speed-test setup. Pages load in 1.8 s today
(limit 2.0 s). My pick: ship now, the delay comes with the speed fix." Its "all A" is
A-founder-2026-10-19b's and A-founder-2026-10-19c's. "approve batch 5" and "C A" answer other
questions and are not recorded here. The reply reached the spec writer through the orchestrator's
work order.

**Evidence.**
- PR 221 (TASK-194, head `93ed3dd0`), description, round 1: "Review item 1 (AC-55: defer the
  module until after the LCP paint, spec 004 §14 A-founder-2026-10-15b clause 4)", left open for
  the founder with two options, "defer the module behind PR 253's `AfterPaint`, or amend clause 4
  for this PR". Commit "docs: escalate AC-55 for the motion module to TASK-244 (TASK-194)".
- `docs/tasks/TASK-194.md`, the orchestrator's carry-forward of 2026-10-18, and
  `docs/tasks/TASK-244.md`, "Re-sequenced by the orchestrator, 2026-10-18": PR 253 declares the
  deferred set with a reserved entry for the motions.
- "1.8 s" is the orchestrator's figure. It is not a measurement this amendment records.

**Original.** Clause 4: "TASK-194 (motions, PR 221) and the language popup's task build to this
rule before they merge, and every later island does too." Clause 2 puts the motions in the
deferred set; AC-55's first bullet requires the set to include them; T-60's unit case lists them.

**Corrected.**
1. **The deviation.** PR 221 may merge with its motion module (the one client module of
   TASK-194's brief, "JavaScript budget") loading as it does on head `93ed3dd0`, before the LCP
   element has painted. That is the only exception to clause 1 this amendment makes.
2. **Its bounds.**
   - **Only that module.** The language popup, the Menu enhancements and every later island
     follow clause 1 as written. A second TASK-194 module is not covered.
   - **AC-24 still binds every PR, PR 221 included.** The `lighthouse` job asserts LCP
     < 2,000 ms on every audited URL, on A12's Brotli origin and A18's warm runs. The assertion,
     the URL set, `numberOfRuns`, the throttling and the aggregation do not change (clause 3).
     This amendment waives clause 4's timing for one module, never the LCP budget. PR 221 does
     not merge on a red LCP.
   - **While the deviation is open,** a PR whose LCP is red does not merge. If that run shows the
     motion chunk starting before the LCP render time, the motion module's deferral is the next
     merge: TASK-244, or a new task row if TASK-244 cannot land first.
   - **Everything else holds:** A1's 131,072 B and A-founder-2026-10-18a's 133,120 B script
     budgets (PR 221 reports 126,915 B and 127,949 B), CLS < 0.05, no animation on the LCP
     element or its ancestors (T-48), no loader in front of server HTML, the JavaScript-off
     behaviour, and clause 3 in full.
3. **Who closes it: TASK-244 (PR 253).** When PR 253 lands after PR 221, it replaces the reserved
   motions entry in its declared set (clause 2) with TASK-194's motion module, behind the same
   mechanism as the other entries, and its AC-55 evidence covers the module. If PR 253 merges
   first, the deviation never opens: PR 221 rebases onto it and adds its module to the set before
   it merges, as the 2026-10-18 carry-forward says.
4. **Clause 4 now reads:** "The language popup's task builds to this rule before it merges, and
   every later island does too. TASK-194 (motions, PR 221) may merge before its module follows
   the rule (A-founder-2026-10-19a); TASK-244 brings that module under it. A merged island that
   breaks it is a new task row, not a reopened one."
5. **Bookkeeping (the orchestrator's).** TASK-194's 2026-10-18 carry-forward moves to TASK-244's
   brief as a binding clause. TASK-194's brief and PR 221 cite this amendment against review
   item 1.

**SEO.** LCP is a ranking input. Until TASK-244 lands, the motion chunk can join the race
A-founder-2026-10-15b measured (LCP 2,474 to 2,920 ms in six of nine runs once async chunks
finished before the paint), so the margin rests on AC-24 alone, and no PR merges red. Nothing is
indexed before TASK-096's flip. URLs, canonicals,
robots, hreflang, sitemaps and schema are unchanged; no content moves client-side. **i18n.** No
key changes. **Compliance.** No data flow, consent or price-display change. The consent banner
stays outside the deferred set (clause 2). Reduced motion (AC-6 as A24 reads it) is unchanged.
The RoPA is unchanged.

**AC-55 amended.** Its first bullet ends: "and the set includes the language popup, the Menu
enhancements and the motions; once TASK-194's motion module is on `main`, the motions entry
imports that module, and a reserved entry with no module does not pass".

**T-60 amended.** The unit case asserts that the motions entry resolves to TASK-194's module once
that module is on `main`; red with the entry left reserved. The e2e case gains "red with the
motion module imported eagerly (its chunk starts before the LCP)".

**AC-24 and T-26:** text unchanged. They are the guard this amendment names.

**A-founder-2026-10-19b: The listing card says its price once, with "Includes VAT and delivery";

country-page bullet; A24 clauses 2 (a) and 3; A24 clause 10 P5; spec 008 AC-6, A2, A15 (c),
A16 (a), T-6, T-7 via spec 008 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19b; no new AC or T id).**
Status: approved (founder, 2026-10-19). The founder's reply, 2026-10-19 morning, in chat,
verbatim: "A ship, approve batch 5, C A, all A". Its "all A" answers TASK-252's eight judgments.
Item 7's row in `docs/design/README.md` ("Awaiting founder", PR 267) says option A "**Reverses
the founder's ruling of 2026-10-03** (cards show the other currencies)", so this reply is the
founder's word on that reversal, which the row asked for. "A ship" is A-founder-2026-10-19a's;
items 1 to 4 and 6 are A-founder-2026-10-19c's; "approve batch 5" and "C A" answer other
questions and are not recorded here. The reply reached the spec writer through the orchestrator's
work order.

**Evidence.** `docs/design/README.md`, "Awaiting founder", row 7; `wireframes/qa-judgments-2026-10-18-desktop.dc.html`
and `-mobile.dc.html`, item 7 (note and option A drawing); QA-1 N10 and N11 and QA-2 Q18-07 and
Q1-19 (`.claude/state/qa-2026-10-18/`): every card showed "all in", a three-currency line and the
example label.

**Original.**
- A21 clause 6 (founder, 2026-10-03: "also do this too.. like showing prices this way too in usd
  eur and gpt at the bottom too") puts the equivalents line under every destination-specific
  price; (c) names "product cards on country-scoped listing pages (and any other placement of the
  same card), the product page's price and its summary". A23 clause 5 and spec 008 A15 (c) give
  the country lead's price tag `catalog.price.allIn` and the equivalents line.
- Spec 008 AC-6 asks for `catalog.price.inclusive` beside the card's price. The build renders
  `catalog.price.allIn` ("all in" in `en`; `src/modules/ui/shop/ProductCard.tsx`). A24 clauses
  2 (a) and 3 call `catalog.price.inclusive` "all in", which is `allIn`'s value; `inclusive` is
  "Includes VAT and delivery".
- Spec 008 A2 puts the example label on every card that shows an `ai` asset; A16 (a) keeps that
  from `md` up and shows it once per grid below `md`.
- On the destination-less hubs each card prints `shop.card.noPrice`, while `shop.hub.noMoney`
  already says why once (spec 008 AC-7's one sentence).

**Corrected.**
1. **One price line.** Every country-scoped listing card, and the country occasion's lead tag
   (spec 008 A15 (c), A16 (b)), shows the all-in price through `formatMoney` with
   `catalog.price.inclusive` beside it: "Includes VAT and delivery" in `en`, the authored formula
   in `de` and `pl`. No card or tag renders `catalog.price.allIn`. A23 clause 5's
   "`catalog.price.allIn`, and the equivalents line where A21 clause 6 (c) shows one" reads
   "`catalog.price.inclusive`". A24 clause 2 (a)'s and clause 3's `("all in")` read
   `("Includes VAT and delivery")`; clause 3's reason for dropping the strip's notice on the
   phone holds, more plainly.
2. **No equivalents on a card.** A21 clause 6 (c) reads: "Only on the product page: its price
   and its summary, in 'The price' section (spec 009 §14 A12 move 2)." Listing cards, the country
   lead tag and every other placement of the card render no equivalents line, and the card's
   view model carries no equivalents, so there is nothing to render one from. Clause 6 (a), (b),
   (d) and (e) bind the product page unchanged; the spec 008 §13 Q9 repaint island repaints the
   product page's line only. The checkout still shows none (spec 010 AC-18). This reverses the
   founder's 2026-10-03 ruling for cards only.
3. **The example label once per grid (replaces spec 008 A2 and A16 (a)'s first two bullets;
   spec 006 AC-17 unchanged).**
   - **All-example grid.** Where every card in a grid that shows a photograph shows an `ai`
     asset, and at least one does, the label (`media.provenance.aiExample`) is displayed once for
     the grid, at every width, and no grid card displays its own (the country occasion's lead figure from `md` up is the exception below).
   - **Mixed grid.** Where the grid also holds a non-`ai` photograph, from `md` up each card that
     shows an `ai` asset displays its label (A2), so the buyer can tell which picture is an
     example; below `md` A16 (a)'s once per grid stands.
   - **Place.** Directly above the grid and inside the first viewport: from `md` up on the count
     line's row, as item 7A draws it; below `md` where A16 (a) puts it, between the count line and
     the first row.
   - **Mechanism (A16 (a)'s, at every width).** Every card that shows an `ai` asset keeps its
     label in the server HTML. Where the grid label is displayed, the per-card labels are hidden
     by CSS (`display: none`). The grid label is one element per grid. So spec 006 AC-17's
     pre-hydration assertion and its no-suppression-prop rule hold unchanged.
   - **The country occasion's lead from `md` up** sits outside the grid's box, so it keeps its own
     label when its asset is `ai`.
   - **Scope.** Every listing grid, the destination-less hubs included. The home's Popular
     choices row is not a listing grid; A-founder-2026-10-14g stands for it.
4. **The hubs' per-card line goes.** A destination-less hub card is the photograph, the name and
   the label rule of clause 3; it prints no `shop.card.noPrice`. `shop.hub.noMoney` stays, once
   per page (spec 008 AC-7 unchanged). A24 clause 10's P5 row is withdrawn.
5. **Design.** The designer redraws the card in `system/components.dc.html` and the listing
   artboards (`country-shop-*`, `country-category-*`, `country-occasion-*`, `category-hub-*`,
   `occasion-hub-*`) to item 7A, and marks README row 7 answered. `/plan-tasks` makes one
   implementer row. It is a money display: the row keeps the breaker (A21 clause 6 (e)).
6. **Unchanged.** Spec 009 AC-21 and A12 (the product page's price element, its inclusive text and
   its one equivalents line); spec 008 AC-7; rebound AC-34 and T-49 (a shorter card only helps the
   first row fit); AC-53.

**SEO.** Schema price = visible price is unchanged: `ItemList` and every `Offer` read the card's
one price, and the equivalents never reached JSON-LD. URLs (`plan/02` §4), canonicals, robots,
hreflang and sitemaps are unchanged; rendering stays ISR (`plan/01` §3). The per-card labels stay
in the HTML, hidden, as A16 (a) already ships below `md`; every Phase 0 listing is
`noindex,follow`. Shorter cards, no layout after load: CLS and LCP unchanged or better.
**i18n.** No new key. `catalog.price.allIn` and `shop.card.noPrice` lose their last use and are
deleted from every catalogue and `messages/*.meta.json`, so `i18n:check`'s unused-key rule stays
green; the unreviewed share can only fall. `catalog.price.inclusive` is spec 003's price-display
legal text, authored per locale, never machine-only. The equivalents key stays for the product
page. Logical properties only; `/ar-XB` mirrors the count line's row. **Compliance.** Price shown
= price charged, VAT and delivery included, and the legal formula now sits beside every card
price (`plan/07` §4), where "all in" did not state it. The equivalents were an approximate extra,
not required information; the product page keeps them. The example label stays visible on the
first screen of every grid (spec 006 AC-17), per card from `md` up wherever a grid mixes example
and real photographs. The ranking disclosure is unchanged (spec 008 §8). No data flow changes;
the RoPA is unchanged.

**A21's test bullet "Spec 008 T-6 and spec 009 T-21: equivalents" amended.** Its first case
reads: "A fixture PDP renders exactly the three non-charged currencies of the set, each equal to
the mid-rate conversion at the charged price's `fxAsOf`, and a fixture listing card renders no
equivalents line. Red if the buffer is applied, if the charged currency appears in the line, or
if a card renders the line." Its other cases bind the product page unchanged.

(`.claude/state/qa-2026-10-18/`).

**Original.**
- AC-17 says the banner "blocks no content" and measures nothing. At 1330 × 860 it covered the
  home sentence's "Poland / her birthday / Continue"; at 390 × 844 it took about 45 % of the
  screen above the AC-48 bar.
- AC-20's settings panel lists every register entry inline: four phone screens before Save.
- §13 Q16 is open. AC-47 asks only for `Esc` and focus return; on the home, Menu › Send flowers
  scrolls to the sentence and leaves the menu open over it.
- AC-17 and spec 003 A16 each close on `Esc`; one press closed the popup and the banner together.
- The header is sticky and the AC-48 bar fixed at every height. At 200 % zoom on a 1280 screen
  (640 × 400) they keep 35 % of the viewport, and the home shows a photograph with no H1.

**Corrected.**
1. **Where the banner sits (item 1A).** From `md` up it is a card at the inline-end bottom
   corner, 420 px wide, 32 px from the inline-end and block-end edges. Below `md` it is a top
   sheet in the language popup's slot (spec 003 A16 clause 2): top edge at y 0, at most 260 CSS px
   tall, its three answers in one row of 44 px buttons. It shows only after the popup has closed
   (spec 003 AC-28 (f)). While it shows below `md` it covers the header's Menu, as the popup does,
   and the page under it still scrolls. AC-17's other clauses and AC-20's equal prominence are
   unchanged.
2. **The settings fold (item 2A).** "Choose" opens three group rows (Essential with a locked
   switch, Analytics, Marketing), each with its name, purpose and switch. Under each row a native
   disclosure, closed when the panel opens, lists that group's `src/config/cookies.ts` entries
   with purpose and lifetime. Save is pinned at the panel's foot. From `md` up the panel grows
   from the corner card, 420 px wide and at most the viewport height minus 64 px. Below `md` it
   is the top sheet, at most 780 CSS px tall at 390 × 844. Either way it scrolls inside itself.
   The disclosure label and the locked switch's word are TASK-251's consent batch: they ship only
   with founder-approved text, because `en-gb`'s unreviewed share has no headroom (spec 003 A17),
   so the build row waits for that approval.
3. **The open phone Menu (item 3A; answers §13 Q16).** With JavaScript on, below `md`:
   - while the Menu is open a scrim dims the page below the header, and a tap on it closes the
     Menu;
   - focus stays inside the open Menu and its summary; `Esc` closes it and returns focus to the
     summary (unchanged);
   - activating Send flowers closes the Menu;
   - while open, the summary shows a close mark and its accessible name is a close label: a
     reviewed "Close" key reused, as A-founder-2026-10-18a reused `nav.categories.label`, or a new
     `nav.menu.close` only with founder-approved text.

   **Send flowers on the home, at every width:** it scrolls the sentence letter into view below
   the header and moves focus to the letter's first choice ("my mum"). Elsewhere it stays a link
   to the home's `#send`. With JavaScript off the `<details>` Menu and the `#send` link work as
   today.
4. **One `Esc`, one layer (item 4A).** Overlays form a stack, and one `Esc` press acts on the top
   layer only. The banner mounts only after the language popup's close and ignores the key press
   that closed it. With both pending, the first `Esc` closes the popup (spec 003 A16: `fo_locale`
   is set), the banner then shows, and a second `Esc` closes the banner without recording a
   decision (AC-17).
5. **Short screens (item 6A).** When the viewport is under 500 CSS px tall
   (`(max-height: 500px)`, at any width), the header is not sticky, the AC-48 bar is not drawn,
   and the home cover is capped at the viewport height minus the header, so the H1 is on the
   first screen. At 500 px tall and above, nothing changes (390 × 844 included). AC-48's twin
   rule is why the bar can go: every action in it has a twin in the flow. The rule is CSS only,
   and the header's box stays identical before and after hydration (AC-7).
6. **Design.** The designer draws 1A, 2A, 3A and 6A into `chrome-desktop`, `chrome-mobile` and
   `system/components.dc.html`'s consent sheet, and marks README rows 1 to 6 and 8 answered.
   AC-27's consent baselines (banner shown, settings open) are re-taken by the build task.

**SEO.** Chrome only. No URL, link, canonical, robots, hreflang, sitemap or schema change; the
Menu's links stay in the server HTML, so AC-14's crawl is unchanged. The banner no longer covers
the home's H1 or letter. The focus and scrim behaviour lives in the Menu's existing island, which
is in A-founder-2026-10-15b's deferred set; a tap before it loads is honoured by the native
`<details>`. The consent banner stays outside that set. The 500 px rule does not fire on
Lighthouse's viewports, and nothing lays out after load, so CLS and LCP are unchanged.
**i18n.** No unreviewed key ships in `en-gb`: the new words (clauses 2 and 3) wait for the
founder's copy batch. Corners and insets are logical (`inline-end`, `block-end`), so `/ar-XB`
mirrors the corner card. German labels wrap inside the 420 px card; nothing truncates.
**Compliance.** Consent: Accept, Reject and Choose keep equal prominence (AC-20); nothing is
pre-enabled; `Esc` still records nothing (§8); the banner stops blocking content (AC-17); every
cookie's purpose and lifetime stays one tap away inside the choice (AC-20). Accessibility (WCAG
2.2 AA): focus is kept inside the open Menu and `Esc` always leaves it, so there is no keyboard
trap (2.1.2); one `Esc` per layer keeps both overlays dismissible (1.4.13); the short-screen rule
serves reflow at 200 % (1.4.4, 1.4.10). No data flow changes; `fo_consent`'s lifetimes are
unchanged; the RoPA is unchanged.

**§13 Q16:** resolved by clause 3.

**AC-17 amended.** It gains: "On `/en` with no `fo_consent` and a valid `fo_locale`, at 1440 ×
900, 1330 × 860, 1280 × 800 and 390 × 844, the banner's box intersects neither the H1's, the
sentence letter's nor the AC-48 bar's. From `md` up its inline-end and block-end edges are 32 ± 1
px from the viewport's and its width is 420 ± 1 px; below `md` its top edge is at y 0 and its
height is ≤ 260 CSS px. With the language popup and the banner both pending, one `Esc` closes the
popup only; the banner then shows, and a second `Esc` closes it with no `fo_consent` set."

**AC-20 amended.** Its last clause reads: "the settings panel lists every register entry in each
category with its purpose and lifetime, inside that category's disclosure, closed when the panel
opens; at 390 × 844 the open panel is ≤ 780 CSS px tall and its Save control lies inside the
viewport without scrolling; from `md` up the panel is at most the viewport height minus 64 px and
its Save control lies inside it."

**AC-47 amended.** It gains, with JavaScript enabled at 390 × 844 and 360 × 640: "while the Menu
is open a scrim covers the page below the header, and a tap on it closes the Menu; Tab and
Shift+Tab keep focus inside the Menu and its summary; activating Send flowers closes the Menu, and
on the home the sentence letter's top lies below the header and focus is on its first choice;
elsewhere Send flowers' `href` is the home's `#send`; the open summary's accessible name is the
close label." And: "at 640 × 400 and 844 × 390 the header's computed `position` is neither
`sticky` nor `fixed`, and on `/en` the H1's box lies inside the first viewport; at 390 × 844 and
1440 × 900 the header is sticky as before."

**AC-48 amended.** It gains: "at 640 × 400 and 844 × 390, no bar is displayed on a page that
displays one at 390 × 844."

**T-19 amended.** It gains the banner's boxes at AC-17's four sizes and the two-`Esc` sequence.
Red with the banner placed over the sentence letter at 1330 × 860, and red with one `Esc` closing
both overlays.

**T-22 amended.** It gains the closed disclosures, the panel heights and Save inside the viewport,
and still finds every register entry in the DOM. Red with the disclosures open on load (the phone
panel over 780 px).

**T-52 amended.** It gains the scrim tap, focus containment, Send flowers closing the Menu and its
focus target on the home, the close label, and the short-screen header. Red with the Menu left
open after Send flowers, and red with the header sticky at 640 × 400.

**T-53 amended.** It gains no bar at 640 × 400 and 844 × 390, and the bar at 390 × 844. Red with
the `max-height` rule removed.

Open for the founder: the wording of the new consent and Menu labels goes to the founder with
TASK-251's copy batch (clauses 2 and 3).

**A-founder-2026-10-19d: No cookie banner while the site sets only essential cookies; the footer's
"Cookie settings" opens the settings panel; the banner returns in a compact form when analytics or
ads are switched on (founder ruling 2026-10-19, item 2; §2 L92 "Banner"; A-founder-2026-10-19c
clauses 1 and 4 and its AC-17, T-19 gains; AC-6, AC-9, AC-13, AC-17, AC-19, AC-20, AC-21, AC-22,
AC-27, AC-39, AC-48, AC-55 and T-8, T-11, T-15, T-19, T-21, T-22, T-23, T-24, T-29, T-41, T-53,
T-60 amended; spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19d owns the language popup; spec 041 §14
A-founder-2026-10-19d is the cookie notice's pointer; no new AC or T id).**
Status: approved in substance by the founder's ruling of 2026-10-19 (below). The founder confirms
this text when they approve the docs PR that carries it. The spec writer's readings are marked
*Reading* and can be overruled in one word.

**Ruling, verbatim.** The founder, 2026-10-19, in chat: "the cookie banner is way too big on the
site. also the popup that appears.. on the site of.. language.. that also annoying and big so what
should we do? what do u suggest?" The orchestrator proposed two things. Item 2, in the
orchestrator's words: "Cookie banner: not shown at launch while the site sets only essential
cookies (GA4 id blank, nothing in analytics or marketing active). Visitors are informed by the
cookie policy page and the footer "Cookie settings" link, which still opens the settings panel.
When analytics or ads are switched on, the banner returns in the compact all-A form, with the phone
strip capped at about 140 CSS px (one short line plus the three answers in one row) instead of
260 px; its one short line of copy ships only with founder-approved text." The founder answered,
verbatim: "yes yes" (yes to item 1, the language popup, which spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19d
records, and yes to item 2). The reply reached the spec writer through the orchestrator's work
order.

**Trigger and evidence.**
- The founder's message above, after the site went live `noindex` (decisions of 2026-10-18).
- QA-2 N-03 (`.claude/state/qa-2026-10-18/`), as A-founder-2026-10-19c records it: at 390 × 844
  the banner took about 45 % of the screen; at 1330 × 860 it covered the home sentence.
- What the site stores today, from the code: `src/config/cookies.ts` has ten rows; every one is
  `essential` except `_ga` and `_ga_*` (`analytics`, `status: "declared"`); no row is `marketing`.
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` is blank in `.env.example` and unset in CI and on previews (A15;
  decisions log 2026-09-16: "Until then `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` stays unset"). No ad tag is
  wired anywhere. So on today's data the banner asks a question about storage the site never does.

**Original.**
- §2 L92 "Banner" and AC-17: with no `fo_consent`, the banner appears after hydration on every
  localised page. AC-9: the footer's "Cookie settings" re-opens the banner after a decision.
- A-founder-2026-10-19c clause 1: the phone banner is a top sheet of at most 260 CSS px, holding the
  headline, A14's two body paragraphs and the three answers. Clause 4: with the popup and the
  banner both pending, one `Esc` closes the popup only, a second closes the banner.
- AC-6, AC-19, AC-20, AC-22, AC-27 and AC-48, and their tests, drive the banner as shown on first
  visit; AC-39, AC-55 and T-41, T-60 drive the language popup as opened on first visit.

**Corrected.**
1. **One reader decides whether anything needs consent.** `consentSources(env)` is exported from
   the `src/modules/analytics` barrel (specs 004 and 023). It returns two things:
   `ga4MeasurementId` (the value of `ga4MeasurementId(env)`: a well-formed `G-…` id, or `undefined`
   when the variable is blank or malformed, exactly as today) and `active`, the non-essential
   categories that have an **active source**, in register order:
   - **`analytics` is active** when the reader's `ga4MeasurementId` is defined, or when any
     `src/config/cookies.ts` row in the `analytics` category has `status: "set"`.
   - **`marketing` is active** when any register row in the `marketing` category has
     `status: "set"`. No marketing source exists today: the register has no marketing row and no ad
     tag is wired. A spec that adds an ad tag adds its env reader to this function in the PR that
     adds the tag's loader.
   - **Read in one place.** The locale layout calls `consentSources(process.env)` once per document
     render and takes both the `measurementId` it hands to `AnalyticsScripts` and the banner's
     `prompt` (true when `active` is non-empty) from that one result. No other file under `src/`
     decides whether the banner mounts, and no file under `src/` other than `src/lib/env.schema.ts`
     (which defines it) and this reader contains a call to `ga4MeasurementId(` outside a comment
     (the mentions in comments in `src/app/robots.ts` and `src/modules/seo/environment.ts` are not
     calls). `next.config.ts` keeps its own call for the CSP.
   - **Today's value:** `active` is `[]` and `ga4MeasurementId` is `undefined`: the id is blank and
     both GA4 rows are `declared`. If the founder sets the id in production, `active` is
     `["analytics"]` and the banner shows there, which is the correct answer for that deploy.
2. **While `active` is empty, no banner.**
   - No consent sheet mounts on any page, whatever `fo_consent` holds, and the consent island's
     chunk is not requested on page load.
   - The consent loader keeps one delegated listener for `data-fo-consent-reopen` (the footer's
     "Cookie settings" and the cookie notice's "Change cookie settings", spec 041). The first
     activation imports the island and opens the **settings panel** directly: A-founder-2026-10-19c
     clause 2's panel (Essential locked, Analytics, Marketing, each register entry inside its
     group's closed disclosure, Save pinned), in clause 2's place (at the corner card's position from
     `md` up, as the top sheet below `md`). Focus moves into the panel, because the visitor asked for
     it. `Esc` closes it, records nothing and returns focus to the control. With JavaScript off the
     control does nothing, as today (spec 041 §5 table).
   - The Consent Mode v2 default-denied bootstrap stays inline on every document, unchanged (AC-18).
     No third-party request is made (AC-18, AC-21).
   - **`fo_consent`** stays `essential` in the register. It is neither read nor written on page
     load. Save in the panel writes it with AC-19's lifetimes (12 months when any category is on,
     6 when none) and sends the `POST /api/consent` record. A forged or stale value is deleted when
     the island loads on activation (AC-19's rewrite rule, at that moment rather than on load).
   - The language popup gate: spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19d serves `<html>` without
     `data-fo-locale-gate` wherever no language can be offered, so a banner never waits for a popup
     that cannot open.
3. **When `active` is non-empty, the banner returns, by data.**
   - The island mounts after hydration and asks as AC-17 says, in clause 4's compact form once that
     form is built. The footer control re-opens it after a decision, and Choose opens the panel
     (AC-9, unchanged in that state).
   - The flip is data: setting `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` and rebuilding (runbook
     `docs/runbooks/analytics-consent.md` §2), or a register row turning `set` in the PR that ships
     its writer. No file under `src/app/` or `src/modules/` changes at the flip.
   - **Before the first category is switched on** (procedural; TASK-NNN writes these into the
     runbook's §1; none is a code gate): (a) clause 4's compact form is on `main`; (b)
     `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` is bumped in the PR that updates the cookie notice for that category,
     so a decision saved while nothing was active is asked again (the runbook's existing lever,
     §4a); (c) RoPA row 4, the DPA, the privacy notice and the cookie notice, as the runbook already
     lists; (d) A15's budget decision; (e) **the founder's lawyer has ruled, in writing filed under
     `docs/compliance/`, on two points:** whether the GA4 tag may load before a decision under
     Consent Mode v2 default-denied and send cookieless pings ("advanced" mode, as §2 L94, AC-21 and
     `plan/04` §11 specify today), or must wait for a grant ("basic" mode); and whether clause 4's
     layering (the controller sentence in the panel, a link to the cookie notice in the first
     layer) is enough. If the ruling is "basic", the change to AC-18, AC-21 and §2 L94 is a new
     amendment before the id is set. This amendment changes neither behaviour now.
4. **The compact form (replaces clause 1 of A-founder-2026-10-19c for the banner; its clause 2
   panel stands).**
   - **Content.** One short line of text and the three answers, Accept all, Reject all and Choose,
     in one row with equal prominence (AC-20, A-founder-2026-10-14i). A14's headline and two body
     paragraphs leave the first layer. Which existing `consent.*` keys the panel keeps and which
     are deleted is the copy batch's call; `i18n:check`'s unused-key rule holds.
   - **The line is founder-approved copy.** No key for it ships before the founder approves its
     text (spec 003 A17; `en-gb` has no headroom). It joins TASK-251's consent batch. *Reading:*
     the line carries a link to the cookie notice (spec 041), where the controller sentence and
     every cookie are, and spec 041 AC-29's controller sentence moves from the first layer into the
     settings panel (spec 041 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19d). The founder can overrule this in one word;
     the lawyer rules on it before any category is switched on (clause 3 (e)).
   - **Below `md`:** top edge at y 0 (the language popup's slot, the "all-A" placement), height at
     most **140 CSS px** at 390 × 844 and 360 × 640 in `en` and `en-gb`, the three answers in one
     row, each at least 44 px tall. The page under it still scrolls.
   - **From `md` up:** clause 1's corner card stands (inline-end bottom corner, 420 px wide, 32 px
     insets) with the same one line and one row.
   - **Choose** opens clause 2's panel, unchanged (≤ 780 CSS px at 390 × 844).
   - **Who builds it.** A-founder-2026-10-19c's build row, which takes this clause in place of
     clause 1's 260 px sheet and already waits for TASK-251's copy approval. If that row does not
     exist, the orchestrator makes one. It merges before any category is switched on (clause 3 (a)).
5. **One `Esc`, one layer (A-founder-2026-10-19c clause 4, restated).** The stack rule binds only
   when a language offer (spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19d clause 1) and the consent banner are
   both pending. On today's data neither ever is. With both pending, the first `Esc` closes the
   language overlay (`fo_locale` is set to the page's locale), the banner then shows, and a second
   `Esc` closes it with no `fo_consent` set. When the language overlay becomes spec 003's slim bar,
   the bar task restates this clause for it; its default is that on a phone the bar and the compact
   banner are never on screen together and the banner waits for the bar's close.
6. **Unchanged.** AC-18; equal prominence (AC-20); nothing pre-enabled; `Esc` records nothing;
   no cookie wall; withdrawal from the footer on every page; the register and its lifetimes;
   `POST /api/consent`, `ConsentDecisionSchema`, `logConsentSink`; §2 L94's GA4 loading rule
   (pending clause 3 (e)); clauses 2, 3, 5 and 6 of A-founder-2026-10-19c; A-founder-2026-10-15b
   (the consent banner stays outside the deferred set); A-founder-2026-10-19a.
7. **Design.** `/design` on this amendment draws, before `/plan-tasks`: the first-visit chrome with
   neither overlay at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900; the settings panel opened from the footer with no
   banner behind it, at both sizes; and the compact banner (clause 4) at 390 × 844, 360 × 640 and
   1440 × 900, replacing 1A's 260 px sheet in `chrome-mobile`, `chrome-desktop` and
   `system/components.dc.html`'s consent sheet.
8. **The consent-active build (test fixture).** CI builds the site a second time with
   `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID=G-FIXTURE00` and runs a Playwright project, `consent-active`,
   against it on `ci:full`. Every request to `www.googletagmanager.com` and
   `*.google-analytics.com` is aborted by route interception, and the project asserts each was
   aborted, never answered. No Lighthouse run and no script budget on that build (A15: the tag
   breaks the budget by design). The project first asserts the GA4 tag element is in the served
   HTML, so it cannot run silently against the committed configuration. This is where the
   banner-shown halves of the amended ACs below run.
9. **Chunk checks carry their own positive control.** Every assertion below that a chunk was *not*
   requested names the chunk by the file the build output records for it (the
   `react-loadable-manifest.json` entry that `budget:client-js` counts), and first asserts that file
   exists and is non-empty, so a wrong name cannot pass. Where the same run can then load the chunk
   (the consent island, after activation), it asserts the same matcher now finds it.
10. **Owner.** **TASK-NNN** (the orchestrator assigns the id): the reader (clause 1), the layout
   wiring, the banner-off path and the footer activation (clause 2), the restated tests below, the
   `consent-active` project (clause 8), the runbook steps (clause 3), with spec 003's popup gate and
   spec 041's two test restatements in the same feature PR. It keeps the breaker: consent and
   compliance, a CI workflow and i18n behaviour every visitor meets (DoD §4). The compact form is
   clause 4's row.

**SEO.** No interstitial on a first visit: neither the consent banner nor the language popup
covers content, which removes the risk Google's intrusive-interstitials guidance describes. Less
client JavaScript on a first view: the consent island (2,363 B Brotli, runbook §4a) and its panel
chunk (967 B) are fetched only on activation, and the popup's island (up to 3 KB, spec 003 A14) not
at all on today's data. The consent sheet was once the page's LCP element (A14); it can no longer
compete. The server HTML stays one cache entry per URL with no `Vary` and no `Set-Cookie`; the only
document change is the absent `data-fo-locale-gate` attribute, identical for every visitor.
Nothing indexable moves: URLs (`plan/02` §4), canonicals, robots, hreflang, sitemaps and schema are
unchanged; rendering stays as `plan/01` §3 sets it. AC-24, AC-55's rule and the first-paint visual
baselines hold.

**i18n.** No new key in this amendment. The compact line's key ships only with the founder's
approved text in TASK-251's batch (spec 003 A17). The panel's strings are unchanged. Logical
properties only, so `/ar-XB` mirrors the corner card. **For the next copy batch (no text change
here):** `consent.essentialLocked` carries two U+2014 characters in `en`, one on each side of "your
language, your basket and the security of your order", and the `de` and `pl` echoes carry the same.
It is consent copy the panel now shows first when a visitor opens "Cookie settings", so the batch
replaces both with commas or a colon (the founder's no-em-dash condition).

**Compliance.**
- **ePrivacy, today.** Art. 5(3) of Directive 2002/58/EC requires consent for storing or reading
  information on a visitor's device, except where it is strictly necessary to provide the service
  the visitor asked for. The UK equivalent is PECR reg. 6(4)(b); Germany's is TDDDG §25(2) no. 2.
  On today's data the site stores only `fo_locale` and `fo_consent` (a language choice and the
  record of a consent choice, both first-party, both written only on the visitor's act) and the
  declared essential rows, and it loads no third-party tag, so no consent is required and no banner
  is owed.
- **ePrivacy, once the GA4 id is set.** Under the design §2 L94, AC-21 and `plan/04` §11 chose, the
  GA4 tag then loads on every page **before** any decision and, under Consent Mode v2
  default-denied, sends cookieless pings; no analytics cookie is set until a grant. EDPB Guidelines
  2/2023 on Art. 5(3) treat that kind of access to the visitor's device as within Art. 5(3), so it
  is not covered by the exemption above. This amendment does not change that design and does not
  claim it is exempt: the banner returns by data the moment a source is active (clauses 1 and 3),
  and clause 3 (e) makes the lawyer's ruling on advanced versus basic mode a step before the id is
  set.
- **Transparency.** The cookie notice (spec 041, TASK-183) still lists every register entry,
  essential ones included, with purpose and lifetime (spec 041 AC-13 unchanged in that half), and
  the footer's "Cookie settings" opens the same list on every page.
- **Consent quality when the banner returns.** Equal prominence, no pre-ticked category, `Esc`
  records nothing, and a decision taken while nothing was active is asked again (clause 3 (b)).
- **Records.** RoPA unchanged: no new flow; consent records (row 3) only become rarer. GA4 (row 4)
  stays "declared but inactive". The cookie register is unchanged.
- **Geo-blocking and redirects.** Regulation (EU) 2018/302 and ADR-0006 are unaffected: nothing
  redirects or blocks.
- **Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA).** No overlay obscures content or focus on a first visit (2.4.11);
  the panel opened on request takes focus and `Esc` always leaves it (2.1.2).
- **Carry-forwards, not gates.** (1) The founder's lawyer reviews the "no banner while only
  essential cookies" position and the cookie notice after go-live; the result goes into TASK-NNN's
  brief and `docs/compliance/`. The same review checks the production site's real cookies against
  the register (an edge cookie set by Cloudflare would be essential but is not in the register
  today). (2) The ruling of clause 3 (e) comes before any GA4 id is set; it is a runbook step, not a
  code gate.

**AC-6 amended.** Its e2e reads: "with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, opening the settings panel
from the footer (committed build) and the banner's appearance (`consent-active` build, clause 8)
produce no animated property change." The language overlay's half moves to spec 003's bar task.

**AC-9 amended.** Its control clause reads: "With `consentSources().active` empty, activating
'Cookie settings' opens the settings panel, with or without a recorded decision, with focus inside
it, and `Esc` closes it, returns focus to the control and writes no cookie. With a category active,
it re-opens the consent banner after a decision has been recorded, and the banner's Choose opens the
panel." The company-block clauses are unchanged.

**AC-13 amended.** It reads: "The consent layer token is above the language popup's layer token in
the built CSS, and spec 003 AC-28 as amended by spec 003 §14 A-founder-2026-10-19d passes."

**AC-17 amended (replaces A-founder-2026-10-19c's gain).** AC-17 now reads:
- (a) With `consentSources().active` empty (the committed configuration), on `/en`, `/en-gb`, `/de`
  and `/pl` at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844, with no `fo_consent`: after hydration, network idle and a
  further 5 s (past the popup gate's 4 s fail-open timer), no consent sheet or panel element is in
  the DOM, no request has been made for the consent island's chunk (named per clause 9), no
  `fo_consent` exists, and an `Esc` press writes no cookie. The same run then activates "Cookie
  settings", and the same matcher finds the consent island's chunk requested.
- (b) In the `consent-active` build, on the same pages and sizes, with no `fo_consent`: `<html>`
  carries no `data-fo-locale-gate`; the banner appears after hydration, reserves no layout space
  (CLS delta 0), does not steal focus, is reachable and operable by keyboard, closes on `Esc`
  without recording a decision and returns on the next page view; its box intersects neither the
  H1's, the sentence letter's nor the AC-48 bar's.
- (c) Once clause 4's form is built, in the `consent-active` build: below `md` its top edge is at
  y 0 and its height is ≤ 140 CSS px at 390 × 844 and 360 × 640 in `en` and `en-gb`, and the three
  answers' top edges lie within 1 px of each other; from `md` up its inline-end and block-end edges
  are 32 ± 1 px from the viewport's and its width is 420 ± 1 px.
- (d) With the language overlay and the banner both pending (clause 5), one `Esc` closes the
  language overlay only and a second closes the banner with no `fo_consent` set.

**AC-19 amended.** It reads: "(a) Committed build, from the footer's panel: analytics on and
marketing off, then Save, sets `fo_consent` with `a: true`, `m: false` and `Max-Age` ≈ 12 months;
both off, then Save, sets it with ≈ 6 months and issues no grant; each sends a `POST /api/consent`
body that parses under `ConsentDecisionSchema`; a forged `fo_consent` is deleted when the island
loads on activation and rewritten by the Save. (b) `consent-active` build: 'Accept all' sets
`fo_consent` with ≈ 12 months and issues a `gtag('consent','update',…)` granting analytics; 'Reject
all' sets it with ≈ 6 months and issues no grant; Choose, analytics on, marketing off saves exactly
that; each body parses under `ConsentDecisionSchema`."

**AC-20 amended.** It reads: "In the `consent-active` build, Accept all, Reject all and Choose have
equal computed prominence (same rendered width class, font size and weight, both text and
background pairs at 4.5:1 or better). On the committed build, the panel opened from the footer
pre-enables no non-essential category and lists every register entry as A-founder-2026-10-19c's
amended last clause says (closed disclosures; ≤ 780 CSS px and Save inside the viewport at
390 × 844; at most the viewport height minus 64 px from `md` up)."

**AC-21 amended.** It gains: "`consentSources()` returns `active: []` and no id for the committed
register with the id blank; `active: ["analytics"]` and the id with `G-FIXTURE00`; `active: []` and
no id with `GTM-ABC123` and with whitespace; `active: ["analytics"]` and no id for a fixture register
whose `_ga` row is `status: "set"` with the id blank; and `active: ["marketing"]` for a fixture
register with one marketing row `set`. The locale layout hands `AnalyticsScripts` the reader's id
and the consent banner `prompt: true` exactly when `active` is non-empty. No file under `src/` other
than `src/lib/env.schema.ts` and the reader contains a call to `ga4MeasurementId(` outside a
comment."

Open for the founder: none blocking. The compact line's text goes to the founder with TASK-251's
