  3. otherwise none: the page is **country-less**.
  Never the IP address, `cf-ipcountry`, `Accept-Language` or a geolocation API (ADR-0006).

#### 2. No price until the visitor chooses a country (option B, the founder's "b", 2026-10-24; spec 005 §13 Q10 and A-founder-2026-10-14g kept)

- **The rule, in the founder's words:** "He has to select a country. We have to show him his country prices." A country-less page shows products, never prices; one choice of a country, on the page or in the chooser, shows that country's exact prices.
- **What a country-less page shows.** Each core product card (item 8) shows its photograph, name and, in the price's reserved box, `shop.price.choose` ("Choose a country to see the price"); its action line reads `shop.cta.chooseCountry` ("Choose where to send"). No amount, no currency, no equivalents, no delivery slots, no add-to-basket. The reviewed `shop.hub.noMoney` ("We show no price on this page, because the price depends on where the flowers are going. Choose a destination and every price you see there includes VAT and delivery.") appears once per country-less page, beside the picker or above the first grid.
- **Country-less pages:** the locale home, every category hub and occasion hub, the occasions index, the destinations hub, guides' non-shop sections, info and legal pages, and the 404 page.
- **No price in structured data or meta** on a country-less page: no `Offer`, `AggregateOffer`, `ItemList` price, meta description or Open Graph amount (spec 008 AC-18 unchanged).
- **Rejected alternative (option A):** "from" prices across countries before a choice; rejected by the founder's "b" on 2026-10-24 (the research found no relay that shows one price across countries, and the spread reaches 52 %).
- **The law (checked 2026-10-24).** No price is indicated before a choice, so the price-indication duties attach where a price first shows, which is always a country page with that country's exact all-in price: the Price Indication Directive 98/6/EC Art. 2(a) and Art. 4(1) (final price including VAT, unambiguous, easily identifiable, clearly legible); UCPD 2005/29/EC Art. 7(4)(c) (in an invitation to purchase, the price inclusive of taxes and delivery charges); CRD 2011/83/EU Art. 6(1)(e) (total price before the contract); in the UK the Price Marking Order 2004, the CCRs 2013 and DMCCA 2024 ss. 226 and 227. A country-less card is not an invitation to purchase: UCPD Art. 2(i) defines one as a communication that indicates the product's characteristics **and price**, and the card states no price and offers no purchase.

#### 3. Choosing a country: the chooser, the remembered country, the URL rule, the edge cases

- **Every link from a country-less page to a priced page goes through the chooser** (founder's rule; AC-76). A priced page is a product page, a shop root, a country category, a country occasion page or search results. Product cards, the menu's shop entries out of context, "See all" controls, price-band buttons and the search form all lead to the chooser. **Links between country-less pages stay plain links** (the menu's category and occasion hub entries, the occasions index, the destinations hub, guides, info pages), so the hubs keep their inbound links and their crawl depth (spec 043 AC-6; `plan/02` §11). **A link that names its country** (a hub's destination rows, the Destinations menu panel, a guide's shop entry) is the visitor's choice made by clicking it, and goes straight to that country's page.
- **The chooser route** `/{locale}/{chooseSegment}/...` (segments in `src/config/locales.data.ts`: `en`, `en-gb` `choose-country`; `de` `land-waehlen`; `pl` `wybierz-kraj`): the one new dynamic route. SSR, uncached (`Cache-Control: private, no-store`), `noindex, nofollow` in meta and `X-Robots-Tag`, disallowed in `robots.txt`, in no sitemap; every link to it carries `rel="nofollow"`. It is **a normal page, never a modal or an interstitial**: its own document with a header, an H1 and `<main>`, reached only by the visitor's click, so it overlays no content on landing (Google Search Central's intrusive-interstitial guidance) and works with JavaScript off.
  - **`next`, the page the visitor wanted, is a path inside the chooser's own path**, never a query string and never a full URL: `/{locale}/{chooseSegment}/{next}`. Accepted shapes, each country-less and each mapped to "that country's version": empty (the home: the country's shop root); a category hub key path (the country category of the same key, else the shop root); an occasion hub key path (the country occasion of the same key, else the shop root); `product/{slug}` (that product's page in the country, else the not-available page below); `price/{band}` (the shop root with `?price={band}`); `search` (spec 045's results for the country, with the form's `q`). **Validation:** a same-site relative path whose segments are all lowercase ASCII words, digits and hyphens from those shapes, at most 256 characters; anything else (a scheme, `//`, a backslash, an encoded slash, another locale's segment, an unknown shape) is dropped and the target becomes the chosen country's shop root. `next` carries no personal data; the search query travels only in the GET form field `q`, which spec 045 never logs.
  - **The chooser page** (`/{locale}/{chooseSegment}/{next}`): H1 `home.picker.label` ("Where should the flowers go?"), then one GET form whose buttons are the shop countries (`name="to"`, the first `PICKER_CHIPS` by `sortWeight`, then by name in the locale's collation), a searchable field (`<input type="search" name="to" list="fo-countries">`, `home.picker.search`, with a `<datalist>` of every registry country, live or not) and the submit `home.picker.submit` ("Show prices"); under them, `shop.choose.remember` ("We will remember this country on this device. Change it any time under Sending to."). With a remembered country, a first button `shop.choose.continue` ("Continue to {country}") leads the form. **The chooser never forwards on its own**, even with a remembered country, so Back never bounces the visitor forward again.
  - **The choice** (the form's GET with `to`, or the path form `/{locale}/{chooseSegment}/{iso2}/{next}` that the island and the selector use): `to` is an ISO code or a country name in any launch locale (endonyms and exonyms from `country_translation`, normalised as spec 045's `normaliseForSearch` does). A shop country: the response sets `fo_dest={iso2}` (item 3's cookie) and answers **303** to that country's version of `next`. No match: the chooser page lists the closest names and the full grouped list. A registry country outside the shop set: the not-live page below.
  - **History: the chooser step is replaced.** With JavaScript on, the deferred island on the chooser page turns the form's submit into `location.replace()` of the same GET, so the chooser leaves the history and Back returns to the page the visitor came from. With JavaScript off, Back shows the chooser page again (it never forwards), with the remembered country's "Continue" button first.
  - It reads no IP and no geo header (source scan); the same request with any `cf-ipcountry` or `Accept-Language` gives the same answer.
- **The remembered country** `fo_dest`: set by the chooser's choice response (`Set-Cookie`; 180 days; `SameSite=Lax`; `Secure`; first party; not `HttpOnly`, because the island reads it), or by the island under the URL rule below. **Never set by any cached response** and never set without the visitor's own action. A row in `src/config/cookies.ts` with `consent.cookies.dest.purpose`; its basis is in §8 below.
- **With a remembered country, the home and hubs show that country's exact prices after load** (founder's rule; AC-80). On a country-less page, when `fo_dest` names a shop country, the deferred island fetches one cached document, `/api/prices/{locale}/{iso2}` (route handler, ISR 3,600 s, tag `catalog:{iso2}`, `public` cache, no cookie read, `Disallow` in `robots.txt`). It holds, for each core product, the server-formatted exact card price, equivalents line and delivery slots (item 10) in that country, and a link map from each `next` key used on country-less pages (core products, hub keys, price bands, home) to that country's URL (absent where the country has no such page). The island fills each card's price box, removes its "Choose where to send" line, sets its slots, rewrites every chooser href on the page (cards, menu shop entries, "See all", bands) to the country's URL from the map, or to the path form `/{locale}/{chooseSegment}/{iso2}/{next}` where the map has none, and shows `shop.chosen.line` ("Sending to {country}") with the reviewed `product.destination.change` ("change destination") linking to the chooser. The server HTML is unchanged and the same for everyone; every swap happens inside boxes reserved in the HTML (layout shift at most 0.01). With JavaScript off the page stays as served and the chooser's "Continue" button is one click.
- **The URL wins over the cookie** (founder's rule; AC-81). A country in the URL (from Google, an advert or a shared link) shows that country's exact prices in the server HTML, whatever `fo_dest` says. After paint, when `fo_dest` exists and names another shop country, the island rewrites `fo_dest` to the URL's country, so the remembered country follows the shop the visitor is looking at. When no `fo_dest` exists, the URL creates none (item 3's cookie is created only on a choice in the chooser).
- **Changing country from the header selector re-prices the page** (founder's rule; AC-88). The selector's panel is the chooser's form, posted (GET) to the chooser with `next` set to the current page's country-less key, which the server derives from the route (static per route). The answer is the same page in the new country (a product page, the same category or occasion, the shop root), or the not-available page when the product is not sold there. **The basket keeps its lines** across a change of country: each line keeps its own country and price (spec 010 allows several countries in one basket).
- **A product not offered in the chosen country** (a shared link, a changed country): the chooser renders, with status 200 and `noindex`, `product.unavailable.line` ("{product} is not available for delivery to {country}. These are the closest we send there."), the **3 closest core products** sold there (`closestMatches()`), as cards at that country's exact prices, and `product.unavailable.back` ("See {product} for {country}") back to the previous country's page. Never a dead end and never a 404 for this case.
  - `closestMatches(sku, iso2)` (pure, `src/modules/catalog`): candidates are the core products saleable in `iso2`, never the product itself; ranked first by the same colour family **and** the same price band, then the same colour family, then the same band, then spec 045's "Our order", then SKU; at most 3, fewer only when fewer exist. The original's band is its band in the country it came from and a candidate's band is its band in `iso2` (both by `priceBands()`, spec 008's amendment). Deterministic; a suggestion the buyer picks, never a swap.
- **A destination that is not live** (founder's rule; AC-85): the chooser renders, with status 200 and `noindex`, `destination.notLive.line` ("We don't deliver to {country} yet."), the opt-in waitlist (below) and the chooser's form again to choose another country. No price and no product card on this page. Where the country has a published guide, the page links it.
- **The waitlist** (opt-in email):
  - A POST form (server action): an email field and one **unticked** checkbox whose label is the consent text `waitlist.consent` ("Email me once when you start delivering to {country}. You can unsubscribe at any time."), with a link to the privacy notice. Nothing is pre-ticked; no other field.
  - **Double opt-in (Q-24j):** a confirmation email (Resend) with a one-time token link (the token identifies the row, it is not personal data); unconfirmed rows are deleted after 7 days. The confirmation and the one launch email carry an unsubscribe link.
  - **Storage:** new table `waitlist_signup` (id, email, country_iso2, locale, consent_text_hash, created_at, confirmed_at, notified_at, unsubscribed_at), migration `NNNN_waitlist_signup` with its `.down.sql` (§5.1 below). Retention: delete 30 days after `notified_at` or 24 months after `confirmed_at`, whichever first, by a pg-boss sweep.
  - **No PII in URLs, logs or analytics**: the email travels only in the POST body; logs carry the row id and country; the confirmation link carries the token only.
  - **Abuse:** the same rate limit as spec 045 §2.8 per IP (abuse protection only; never a result).
  - **The admin** sees confirmed counts per country (no addresses on screen) to decide where to recruit florists next; exporting addresses for the launch email is a permissioned admin action logged in the audit trail (spec 012).
  - **RoPA:** a new row (purpose: notify on launch; lawful basis: consent, GDPR Art. 6(1)(a); UK GDPR the same; processor Resend; retention as above).
- **No basket, search or checkout without a country** (founder's rule; AC-87). No country-less page holds an add-to-basket control or a form posting to the basket or checkout; their only purchase entry is "Choose where to send", which leads to the chooser. The header search on a country-less page submits to the chooser with `next=search` and its `q`. The basket and checkout accept only lines that carry a country (spec 010, unchanged).

#### 4. The home, rebuilt around products (supersedes §2 "Home"'s order; amends A9, A21 clause 4, A23, A24's home clauses; spec 043 AC-8 kept)

Every product on the home is a country-less core card (items 1 and 2), filled with exact prices after load when a country is remembered (item 3).

1. **Header** (item 5).
2. **A short first screen.** The H1, one promise line, the country picker, spec 043 AC-8's state line while nothing is open, and `shop.hub.noMoney`. **The picker** is the chooser's form inline: `home.picker.label`, one button per shop country up to `PICKER_CHIPS = 6` (by the registry's `sortWeight`, set in the admin, then by name in the locale's collation), the searchable field with its `<datalist>`, `home.picker.submit` and `shop.choose.remember`; it submits to the chooser with an empty `next` (the shop root). Under the submit button and its remember line, inside the hero card, item 11's trust row (option 2, AC-115). Native HTML, no script needed. **The sentence picker (A21 clause 4) leaves the home (Q-24c):** its country step is the picker; its "who" and "occasion" steps are the occasion tabs and the menu's "For whom"; A-founder-2026-10-21d's listbox lapses with it. The hero photograph takes at most 45 % of the first viewport's height at 1440 × 900 (beside the H1 and picker from `lg`); below `md` it is not above the first product.
3. **Our selection: the first 8 of the core range** under the reviewed `home.trending.heading`, in **"Our order"** (spec 045 §2.5; until spec 045's signal exists every band is 0, so the rank order of the core list decides). Beside the heading, the "How we sort" disclosure (spec 043 trim item 2, holding the listing disclosure text spec 045 selects), closed. Below the grid, `shop.seeAll` to the chooser with an empty `next`. Desktop: 4 columns, 2 rows. Phone: 2 columns, 4 rows.
4. **Shop by occasion.** Under the reviewed `home.occasions.eyebrow`, up to six tabs from `HOME_OCCASION_TABS` (config of occasion keys: birthday, sympathy, thank_you, anniversary, get_well, new_baby; a tab renders only where at least 2 core products carry the occasion). Each panel: up to 4 core cards in "Our order", then `shop.seeAll` linking to the occasion hub (a country-less page, so a plain link).
   - **Mechanism:** no script. Every panel is in the server HTML. The tabs are one native radio group (`<input type="radio" name="home-occasion">` and `<label>`s drawn as chips), the first checked; CSS shows the checked one's panel (`:has(:checked)`) and sets the others to `display: none`. Arrow keys move through the group natively. The group is named by the heading, each panel (`role="group"`) by its occasion.
5. **Shop by price.** Three buttons with word labels and no amount (`shop.price.bandWord.low`, `.mid`, `.high`: "Lower prices", "Middle prices", "Higher prices"), each a chooser link with `next=price/{band}`; the bands themselves are computed in the chosen country by `priceBands()` (spec 008's amendment). No cards in this section.
6. **Coming up.** The next 3 dated occasions observed in any shop country, by their earliest date, that at least 2 core products carry. Each: the date (`formatDate`) and the shop countries that keep it on that date (`Intl.ListFormat`, at most two names then `home.comingUp.more` "and {count} more"), its name linking to the occasion hub (which has every country's date), and up to 3 cards.
7. **Then, shortened:** the destinations block (spec 043 §5.3 C, from data, each row a direct link to that country), how it works in three lines, the FAQ, the footer. (The trust bar is not here: item 11 option 2 puts its row inside the hero card, under the picker, item 4.2.)
- **Sections 3, 4 and 6 each end in their product list, followed only by their "See all" control.**
- **Phone round links** (below `md`, directly under the header, home only): one sideways row of six round photographs, 72 px, each a link with its label: Birthday, Sympathy, Roses, Plants, Thank you (their hubs, plain links) and "All flowers" (`nav.mega.allFlowers`, the chooser with an empty `next`). Images 144 px variants, at most 6,144 B each, `fetchpriority="low"`, never the LCP element.
- **Distinct products (the dedupe rule).** Section 3 takes its 8 first. Each later section, walking its own order, skips a product already displayed above it while enough others remain to fill its slots; otherwise it repeats. With today's data every product of `coreRange()` is displayed at least once.
- **What leaves the home:** the 5-card `TrendingRow` scroller, `OccasionTiles` (its hub links move to the menu and the round links, so every hub keeps an out-of-context inbound link: spec 043 AC-6), `OccasionDates`' Poland-only dates (item 13), the long how-it-works, `HomeProvenanceNote` (the example label is once per grid).

#### 5. The mega menu and the header (supersedes §5.3's header row, A7's link row, A24 clause 3's phone row; amends A20, AC-47; spec 043 AC-2, AC-3 via its amendment; spec 008 A14's link set)

- **The desktop row (from `xl`, 1,280 px):** logo · **Flowers · Occasions · Sympathy · Plants & gifts · By price · Destinations** · "Sending to" selector · search (spec 045 §2.1, once published) · account (spec 018, once published) · Basket with its count slot (spec 010 A-founder-2026-10-22 AC-70, once published). **The "Send flowers" pill leaves the row (Q-24d).** Between `md` and `xl`: logo, selector, search, account and basket on the 64 px row; the six items on a second row that does not scroll. The notice bar above is unchanged.
- **The selector** "Sending to": its `<summary>` reads `nav.sendingTo` ("Sending to {country}") in context (the URL's country, server HTML) and `nav.sendingToChoose` ("Sending to: choose a country") elsewhere; its panel is the chooser's form with `next` set to the current page's key (item 3). After paint, on a country-less page with `fo_dest` set, the island replaces the summary text with the remembered country's name; the summary's box keeps a fixed inline size (ellipsis), so nothing shifts.
- **Panels out of context**, from `megaMenuModel(locale, iso2 | null)` (pure, `src/modules/catalog`, built only from the existence functions, the taxonomy and the registry, so an entry whose page does not exist is not drawn, A20):

| Top item | Columns (each entry only where its page answers 200) | Featured |
|---|---|---|
| Flowers | **By type**: category hubs of kind `productType` except `funeral` and `plant`. **By flower**: category hubs of kind `flowerType`. **By colour**: one chooser link per colour with at least 2 core products, `next` the colour's category hub key where one exists, else the shop root (spec 008 AC-90). "All flowers" (the chooser, empty `next`) | the first core product in "Our order" |
| Occasions | **Every day**: evergreen occasion hubs except `sympathy`. **For whom**: `RECIPIENT_GROUPS` (config mapping a recipient to occasion hubs: mum to `mothers_day`, grandparents to `grandparents_day`, partner to `romance`, new parents to `new_baby`, a colleague to `thank_you`), each linking its hub. **Coming up**: the next 5 dated occasions observed in any shop country that have a hub, each with its earliest date. "All occasions" to the occasions index | the first core product of the next dated occasion |
| Sympathy | the `sympathy` occasion hub, the `funeral` category hub, the `all_saints` occasion hub, any published spec 041 or 044 page tagged `sympathy` | the first core product carrying `sympathy` |
| Plants & gifts | the `plant` and `orchids` category hubs, the `gift_set` hub while it exists; "Extras you can add" (`nav.mega.extras`) as plain text naming the add-ons from data (no page, so no link) | the first core product of type `plant` |
| By price | the three word-label chooser links of item 4.5 | none |
| Destinations | **Ordering open**: shop countries' corridor pages. **Opening soon**: other published destinations' corridor pages. "All destinations" to the destinations hub. All from the registry, grouped by region past 12 entries; each names its country, so each is a direct link | none |

- **Hub entries are plain links** to the country-less hubs (item 3); **shop entries** (colour, price, "All flowers", featured cards) are chooser links.
- **Featured card:** photograph (384 px variant), name, the country-less price box of item 2, linking to the chooser with `next=product/{slug}`.
- **In context** (a URL naming country X with a shop, spec 043 §5.3 A): each hub entry becomes X's country category or country occasion page of the same key; an entry with no such page in X is not drawn; colour and price entries go straight to X's shop root with the facet (GET forms, spec 008 AC-15); featured products are X's (core or non-core) at X's exact price (spec 043 AC-50).
- **Mechanism.**
  - Each top item is a native `<details name="fo-mega">`; the `<summary>` is the label with an `aria-hidden` chevron, the focus ring and a 44 px target; the panel is absolutely positioned under the header row inside the header frame (no layout shift). `name` gives one open at a time where supported.
  - Click, tap, Enter and Space open and close it with **no script**. A closed panel's links are not in the tab order.
  - **Hover needs a few bytes of script**: CSS cannot open a `<details>` on hover across browsers, and WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.13 requires Esc to dismiss content shown on hover or focus. The deferred island opens after 150 ms of hover intent (fine pointer only), keeps it open while the pointer is over the summary or panel, closes 300 ms after it leaves, closes on Esc with focus back on the summary, on focus leaving the panel and on a click outside.
  - Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` no transition.
  - Panel images: `loading="lazy"`, at most 384 px wide and 25,600 B; a closed `<details>` renders nothing inside, so none is fetched before its panel opens.
  - About 60 entries, all in the server HTML of every page, the same for every visitor.
- **The phone (below `md`):** the 64 px row holds the logo, the search link (spec 045), account and Basket where published (spec 010 Q26's icon with count), and the Menu. The Menu sheet's first line is the "Sending to" selector; then the six items as nested `<details>` accordions with the same entries as the desktop panels (no images); then the language switcher, the currency and the help line, as today.

#### 6. Hubs and occasion pages show real product cards

Owned by spec 008 (its amendment of this date: AC-88, AC-89). Every category hub and occasion hub stays crawlable and indexable under its existing rules and shows, in this order: its H1 and lede, `shop.hub.noMoney`, the picker (the chooser's form with `next` set to the hub's key), then its core products as country-less cards (photograph, name, `shop.price.choose`, `shop.cta.chooseCountry`, a chooser link with `next=product/{slug}`), filled with exact prices after load when a country is remembered (item 3); never names only. Then the occasion hub's dates table, the hub's copy, and its destination rows linking every live country's version directly. TASK-315's clickability (A-founder-2026-10-21a) is folded in and generalised: a hub card leads through the chooser, not to a fixed country.

#### 7. Equivalents on every price (reverses A-founder-2026-10-19b clause 2; amends A21 clause 6 (c), (d))

- **The founder's new words** (2026-10-24, verbatim): "every product pricing will be shown in the local pricing, as well as USD and GBP... also euros... it's not being shown on the product pages or like our selection". This **reverses A-founder-2026-10-19b clause 2** ("No equivalents on a card", founder "all A", 2026-10-19); the later words bind.
- **(a) Unchanged:** one prominent price: the charged price in its currency, VAT and delivery included, equal to the quote and schema `price` on the product page.
- **(b) Everywhere a price is:** product page price and summary, every country listing, the in-context menu's featured cards, search results in a country, the not-available page's matches, and every card the island fills after load. Directly under it, smaller, at 4.5:1: the other currencies of {GBP, EUR, USD, PLN}, never the displayed one, at the mid rate without buffer (A21 clause 6 (b)), via `catalog.price.equivalents` ("about {amounts} at the rate of {date}"). **Order:** the locale's display currency first when it is not the displayed one, then GBP, EUR, USD, PLN. The card view model regains a nullable `equivalents` field. A country-less card has no price and so no equivalents.
- **(c) Never in:** JSON-LD, `ItemList`, `Offer`, a sitemap, `<meta>` or Open Graph, `priceTable`, a sort key, a band, a quote, the checkout (spec 010 AC-18).
- **(d) Hidden only when truly stale:** a display bound `EQUIVALENTS_MAX_AGE_DAYS = 14` calendar days from the rate's `as_of`, applied by a new `displayRateFor()`; `fxRateFor()`, `MAX_FX_AGE_HOURS` (two working days) and the stale-FX price fallback are unchanged and still govern every charged amount (spec 005 AC-35). A price carrying `fxReasonKey` shows no line. Until TASK-071's daily `fx.refresh`, the date moves with each deploy's build-time ECB fetch (Q-24f).

#### 8. Cards (spec 008 AC-6 via its amendment)

- **Country-less:** photograph, name, `shop.price.choose` in the reserved price box, `shop.cta.chooseCountry`, one chooser link (item 2).
- **In a country:** "from" with the smallest size's exact all-in price there (`fromPriceProjection`, `catalog.price.from`), `catalog.price.inclusive`, the equivalents line, the delivery line (item 10) in a reserved box, and on a local special `shop.card.localSpecial` ("Only in {country}"). Price sorts and price bands use the same displayed price.
- "Example arrangement" once per grid, in every grid including the home's sections (A-founder-2026-10-19b clause 3, extended), with the reviewed text **unchanged** ("Example arrangement · our florist hand-makes each one"; founder "trust").
- 24 per page on country listings; no quick add (`plan/04` §6), no rating, star or strike-through (spec 008 AC-6's list stands).

#### 9. Click targets

- **First visit** (no remembered country), from the home's first screen at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844: a product page with its price is **two activations** away (the first card, then a country in the chooser), or one country button in the picker then one card. Checkout is **three** (plus the product page's primary action). On a phone, payment takes **7 taps or fewer** from the home with the open fixture: card, country, primary action, step 1 Continue, step 2 Continue, Pay, plus one spare. Typing and the provider's own authentication do not count.
- **With a remembered country:** one activation fewer for each (the island's direct links).
- If spec 010's steps cannot meet these, the AC fails and the founder decides.

#### 10. The delivery countdown (Idea A, approved 2026-10-24)

- **Text:** `delivery.countdown.tomorrow` ("Order within {duration} for delivery tomorrow"), hours and minutes, or minutes under an hour, by `formatDuration()` in `src/modules/i18n/format.ts` (`Intl.DurationFormat` `style: "short"` where present; else `Intl.NumberFormat` unit parts joined by `Intl.ListFormat` `type: "unit"`). No em dash, no "hurry", no red.
- **Facts from the server.** At render, for each priced product in its country (the URL's; or the remembered one, delivered by the prices document), the server computes from spec 009's cutoff rules (`deliveryWindow()`, `src/modules/geo/delivery/calendar.ts`, with the product's lead time, holidays and closed days) the **next two deliverable slots**, each a delivery date in the country's time zone and its cutoff instant, and writes them as `data-fo-slots` on the card and the product page's price block, the same for every visitor. A country-less card carries none.
- **Why the last step is in the browser:** pages are cached up to an hour and served later, so "3 h 20 m" or "tomorrow" written at render would be wrong when read. The line is `hidden` in the server HTML; the deferred island compares the facts with the visitor's clock.
- **Shown only when true:** the first slot whose cutoff is still ahead has a date that is the country's tomorrow (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` with its `timeZone`) **and** the visitor's local tomorrow, with at least a minute left. Re-checked once a minute (one 60 s timer per page); hidden the minute the cutoff passes.
- **Never shown:** while the country takes no orders (A19, AC-34: no cutoff text while nothing is open); without cutoff data; when the next slot is today or later than tomorrow; with JavaScript off.
- **Honesty:** never a fake or reset timer; availability is rechecked at placement (spec 010 L66), and a slot that filled up reopens step 1 with the next date: the stated residual.
- **Layout:** the box is reserved, so showing it shifts nothing; it is not a live region.

#### 11. The trust bar (Idea C): option 2, three points by the buy button (founder "2", 2026-10-24)

- **The pick, binding.** The founder saw the three drawn options (PR 365, `docs/design/proposals/trust-bar-options.dc.html`) and picked option 2, verbatim (relayed by the orchestrator): **"2"**. Option 2 is: on the product page, **directly under the primary action** ("Continue", spec 010's live action; in the not-open state, directly under the state line in the same place), three short points, each an icon, a bold name and one sentence; on the home, **the same three as one short row inside the hero card**, under the picker's submit button (`home.picker.submit`) and its remember line (item 4.2; the place the board draws under "Continue"). Options 1 (a row under the header) and 3 (a band above the footer) are not built. The board stays a proposal; the designer draws option 2 into `wireframes/product-*` and `wireframes/home-*`, which are the build source (CLAUDE.md, `docs/design/`). AC-114 to AC-116.
- **The three items, in this order** (copy keys; "new" keys go through the founder's copy batch, AC-112):

| # | Item | Product page: name / sentence | Home row (name only) | Shows when (`TRUST_CLAIMS` predicate) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The fresh-flower promise | `nav.utility.guarantee` ("Fresh-flower promise", reviewed, spec 041) / `trust.promise.line` (new, copy approved by the founder 2026-10-24 "trust copy ok": "Not fresh on arrival? Send us a photo within 72 hours and we'll replace them or refund you in full.", a shorter wording of spec 041 A.3; no number of days, spec 041 AC-16, spec 009 A11) | `nav.utility.guarantee` | always; the **name links to the guarantee page** where it exists (TASK-287's `infoPageExists`, spec 041), and is plain text, not styled as a link, where it does not (A20 clause 1: nothing looks clickable and does nothing) |
| 2 | "Photo at the door", the delivery photo | `home.proof.photo.title` ("We photograph it at the door", reviewed) / `home.proof.photo.body` ("We email you the picture of what was handed over", reviewed 2026-10-18, spec 016 B4) | `trust.photo.short` (new, copy approved 2026-10-24 "trust copy ok": "Photo at the door") | while no order can close without a delivery photo: spec 002 §14 A6.11 ruling D6 (no `delivered ▶ closed` arrow without a photo), spec 011 (the florist's photo step), spec 016 B4 (the photo emailed to the buyer). The predicate `deliveryPhotoRequired()` is computed from the order transition table, never a constant |
| 3 | "Secure payment" | `trust.payment.name` (new, copy approved 2026-10-24 "trust copy ok": "Secure payment") / `footer.payment.processor` ("Card payments are processed by Stripe. We never store card numbers.", reviewed, spec 013) | `trust.payment.name` | **only once real payments are live**: spec 013's `payments.stripe` flag is on **and** the environment's Stripe mode is live (`livemode`, the key spec 013 AC-40 reads); off, in test mode, or with only the backup processor (spec 014, Mollie, which would make the word "Stripe" untrue) it renders nothing. Turning the flag off purges the tags of every page carrying the bar, so a cached page never keeps the claim past the switch |

- **Rules (binding):**
  1. Every claim is true on the day it shows and is rendered from a predicate over data: `TRUST_CLAIMS` (config: a key and a predicate each), evaluated at render (build or revalidation); a false predicate renders nothing and leaves no gap (the remaining items close up; the row or list is absent when none holds).
  2. No review score, star or count until real reviews exist: at least 25 verified reviews from the provider (`plan/04` §3), read at build or revalidation. Option 2's review place (a first line above the three points on the product page; the end of the home row) is **not built and not reserved** until spec 022 makes reviews real.
  3. The delivery-photo claim renders only under item 2's predicate. It names the photo of the delivery the buyer receives, never shows a photograph of a past delivery (spec 027) and never a count.
  4. No florist, partner, relay, network or acceptance language in the bar (founder ruling 2026-10-14). Elsewhere "our florist" stays (founder "trust").
  5. No speed, freshness or refund claim a spec does not already guarantee (spec 041's promise is the only freshness and refund claim, without a number of days); no card or wallet logos (other companies' trademarks, §5.3 `TrustMarks`); no "same day", no count of florists, orders or deliveries.
  6. Server HTML, the same for everyone, no client fetch, no script, no layout shift; icons are inline SVG from `src/modules/ui`, `aria-hidden`, no image request.
  7. Markup: one `<ul>`; each item an `<li>` with the icon, the name in bold and (product page) the sentence; no new heading level in the product page's outline; text at 4.5:1; the link a 24 × 24 CSS px target at least; logical properties only, mirrored under `/ar-XB`.
- **Where it does not go:** the header, the notice bar, the footer, listings, hubs, cards, the chooser and checkout. The product page's lower "What we promise" block (spec 009 A11) is unchanged by this item.
- **Spec 009's prohibition is withdrawn for this claim:** spec 009 §2, §8, AC-22 and T-22 forbade a delivery-photograph claim on the product page (gated on spec 027, ruling 2026-09-15); spec 002 ruling D6, spec 011 and spec 016 have since made the photo part of every order, so spec 009 §14 A-founder-2026-10-24a withdraws that prohibition for item 2 (the claim, not a photograph), and AC-22 and T-22 no longer block option 2. For the same reason this spec's own "delivery-photo claim" bans (§2 trust strip, §8 "Reviews and trust", AC-15) no longer cover item 2's three keys: AC-15's DOM and grep checks exempt exactly `home.proof.photo.title`, `home.proof.photo.body` and `trust.photo.short` while `deliveryPhotoRequired()` is true, and keep every other shape (reviews, ratings, stars, partner names and photos, counts, a photograph of a past delivery).

#### 12. Coverage in the admin and at go-live (pointer to spec 012b)

- **A product × country coverage matrix** at `/admin/coverage` (spec 012 permissions: `catalogue.read`): rows are the core range first, in rank order, then every other active product; columns are the shop countries and every country with at least one active florist; each cell shows the number of active florists linked who can make the product there. A core cell under `CORE_MIN_FLORISTS = 2` carries a warning (text and icon, never colour alone); a core cell at 0 in a shop country also says the product is hidden from country-less pages (item 1). Read-only, server-rendered, no personal data. It reads the same `coreRange()` the pages read, so the admin and the shop cannot disagree.
- **The go-live checklist** (spec 012b `goLivePreflight`) gains one item, **"Core range in {country}"**: the florist count per core product in the candidate country. The item fails while any core product has 0 florists there (going live would hide it from every country-less page) and warns where any has fewer than 2. The founder may still flip past a failure with spec 012b's typed code, which records the override in the audit trail (Q-24i).
- **A florist joins a country by committing to make every core product** at their own price; their links to core products are the coverage the matrix counts (spec 012b pointer).
- **The waitlist counts** per country (item 3) sit on the same page.

#### 13. Clean-up: no Poland in code

Each becomes "the chosen country" or "every shop country", computed (AC-73):

| Where (on `main`) | Today | Becomes |
|---|---|---|
| `src/modules/ui/home/OccasionDates.tsx` | `DATED_DESTINATION = "PL"`, "Coming up in Poland" | item 4.6 over the shop set; the file's Poland constant deleted |
| `src/modules/ui/home/TrendingRow.tsx` | picks and links from `DEMO_DESTINATION_ISO2`, a Poland eyebrow | replaced by item 4.3 |
| `src/config/site-links.ts` L95 | `DEMO_DESTINATION_ISO2 = "PL"`, "Our selection" to Poland's shop root | the constant deleted; the header link goes with the mega menu |
| `src/app/[locale]/page.tsx` L142 | the home's shop link from `DEMO_DESTINATION_ISO2` | the chooser route |
| `src/modules/catalog/listing.ts`, `product.ts` | "Poland first" ordering of registries | registry `sortWeight` from data, then name |
| `src/modules/ui/shop/CategoryChipRow.tsx` | "Also for Poland" copy | "Also for {country}" with the URL's country, or removed where none |
| `src/modules/ui/home/DestinationsGrid.tsx` | a Poland special case | every destination by the same rule |
| spec 045's default search destination | `DEMO_DESTINATION_ISO2` | the URL's country, else the chooser with `next=search` (item 3) |

A source scan (AC-73) over `src/` (excluding `src/config/catalogue/*.data.ts`, `src/config/countries*.ts` registry data, `messages/`, generated files and tests) finds no ISO country code literal used as a destination and no country name literal.

#### Data model (§5.1)

- **One migration** `NNNN_commerce_structure` (next free number at implementation, coordinated with TASK-293, TASK-294 and the merged-into migration) with its `.down.sql`:
  - **`waitlist_signup`** (item 3): a unique index on (lower(email), country_iso2) where `unsubscribed_at` is null; `country_iso2` a foreign key to `country`. Rollback drops it.
  - **`country.sort_weight int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`**, if absent (the picker's and menus' order). Rollback drops it.
  - **`product.core_rank smallint NULL`** (null: not core), `CHECK (core_rank BETWEEN 1 AND 20)`, unique where not null. Rollback drops it. Until spec 012b's cutover both columns live in the seed (`src/config/catalogue/*.data.ts`); `pnpm seed:check` and the admin refuse a core list outside `CORE_RANGE_SIZE`; the founder edits it on spec 012b's product page (`catalogue.write`, audited).
- **No money column.** All money is integer minor units with an ISO currency; equivalents are computed, never stored; band thresholds are computed integers.
- **One cookie:** `fo_dest` (register row, purpose key, cookie policy line).
- **Pure functions:** `shopCountries`, `coreRange`, `isCore`, `closestMatches`, `priceBands` (spec 008's amendment), `megaMenuModel`, `displayRateFor`, `parseChooserNext`, `countryVersionOf`, `deliveryPhotoRequired` (item 11, over the order transition table); config `HOME_OCCASION_TABS`, `RECIPIENT_GROUPS`, `PICKER_CHIPS`, `CORE_RANGE_SIZE`, `CORE_MIN_FLORISTS`, `TRUST_CLAIMS`, `EQUIVALENTS_MAX_AGE_DAYS`.

#### Rendering (§5.4; `plan/01` §3)

- Home, hubs, listings and the header stay ISR with today's tags and `revalidate`; the header stays TASK-288's `@header` slot, static per route. A go-live or a change to the core list re-renders every country-less page through spec 012b's publish and the `catalog:{iso2}` tags.
- New: the chooser route (SSR, uncached, `noindex`), the prices document (route handler, ISR, public cache), the waitlist action (POST).
- No `headers()`, `cookies()` or `searchParams` read in a prebuilt route; no `Vary`, no `Set-Cookie` on any cached response (only the chooser's choice response sets the cookie).
- Client script: the hover and Esc handler, the selector label, the chooser's history replace, the price and link fill, the URL rule's cookie update and the countdown, all in the existing deferred island set after the LCP paint, at most 2,560 B Brotli together; no new island on any route.

#### SEO (§6)

- **Server HTML for everyone, bots included:** products, the menu and its entries, every tab's panel, the dates, the picker. What changes after paint (a remembered country's prices and links, the countdown, the selector label) follows the visitor's own earlier choice; a crawler, like any first visit, gets the full country-less page. Nothing reads the user agent. This is not cloaking (AC-91).
- **Hubs stay crawlable and indexable** under `indexability()` as today, with their canonicals, hreflang and sitemaps (spec 043 AC-6's equality re-runs). They now show core products without prices and link every live country's version through their destination rows, so authority still flows to the country pages (`plan/02` §11).
- **Internal links:** country-less pages link every hub with plain links (closing TASK-288's escalation: 20 category hubs had lost their header links), and link product pages only through the `nofollow`, robots-disallowed chooser. Product pages are reached by crawlers from the country listings, which the hubs' destination rows and the Destinations panel link directly: home to hub (1), hub to country page (2), country page to product (3), so crawl depth stays at 3 or less (spec 008 AC-21). In context the menu links the country's own pages.
- **No parameterised `<a href>`:** chooser links carry `next` in the path; facet and price entries in context are GET forms (spec 008 AC-15 kept).
- **Not intrusive:** the chooser is a page the visitor opens by clicking, never an overlay on a landing page.
- **Pagination:** 24 per page; pages past the new last page become 404s; they were `noindex` and in no sitemap.
- **Chooser, not-available and not-live pages:** `noindex`, robots-disallowed, linked only with `rel="nofollow"`.

#### Core Web Vitals: risks and budgets

| Risk | Budget or rule | Checked by |
|---|---|---|
| Many product photos on the home | first-viewport images eager, the rest `loading="lazy"`, fixed 4:5 boxes; initial image transfer at 390 × 844 at most 307,200 B | AC-94 |
| LCP on the phone with no hero photo above the first product | one `priority` image per viewport class (hero from `md`, first card below), preloads with non-overlapping `media`; Lighthouse LCP under 2,000 ms, CLS under 0.05 on `/en`, `/en-gb` and one hub | AC-94 |
| Menu HTML on every page | header plus Menu markup at most 6,144 B Brotli | AC-103 |
| Script | deferred island growth at most 2,560 B Brotli; every route inside A1's ceilings as TASK-376 restores them | AC-103 |
| The fill after load | one request after paint, only with `fo_dest` set; document at most 8,192 B Brotli; CLS from the swap at most 0.01 | AC-80 |
| Panel and round images | panel images only after opening; round images low priority, 6,144 B each | AC-102, AC-104 |
| INP | native controls; island handlers set attributes and return | T-103, T-107 |

The known Lighthouse LCP flake on `/en` and `/en-gb` cannot be pleaded by this amendment's home PR, which touches those pages: it shows its own CI medians.

#### i18n and copy (§7; spec 003 §14 A17)

- **Copy headroom is zero.** Each new key that renders on an indexable page ships with the founder's own `record-approval` on the PR branch before merge (A17 clause 5); the chooser, not-available, not-live and waitlist keys render only on `noindex` pages. Each PR prints `i18n:check`'s `en-gb` share before and after. `en-gb` inherits; `de` and `pl` get drafts by the existing policy.
- **New keys (`en`):**

| Key | Text | Item |
|---|---|---|
| `home.picker.label` | Where should the flowers go? | 3, 4 |
| `home.picker.search` | Search all countries | 3 |
| `home.picker.submit` | Show prices | 3 |
| `shop.choose.continue` | Continue to {country} | 3 |
| `shop.choose.remember` | We will remember this country on this device. Change it any time under Sending to. | 3 |
| `shop.price.choose` | Choose a country to see the price | 2 |
| `shop.cta.chooseCountry` | Choose where to send | 2 |
| `nav.sendingTo` | Sending to {country} | 5 |
| `nav.sendingToChoose` | Sending to: choose a country | 5 |
| `shop.chosen.line` | Sending to {country} | 3 |
| `product.unavailable.line` | {product} is not available for delivery to {country}. These are the closest we send there. | 3 |
| `product.unavailable.back` | See {product} for {country} | 3 |
| `destination.notLive.line` | We don't deliver to {country} yet. | 3 |
| `waitlist.consent` | Email me once when you start delivering to {country}. You can unsubscribe at any time. | 3 |
| `waitlist.submit` | Tell me | 3 |
| `waitlist.done` | Thank you. Please confirm with the link we just emailed you. | 3 |
| `email.waitlist.confirm.*`, `email.waitlist.launch.*` | confirmation and launch emails (`reviewed: false` allowed under `email`) | 3 |
| `consent.cookies.dest.purpose` | Remembers the country you chose to send flowers to, so we can show that country's prices. | 3 |
| `shop.seeAll` | `{count, plural, one {See the one bouquet} other {See all #}}` | 4 |
| `shop.price.bandWord.low` / `.mid` / `.high` | Lower prices / Middle prices / Higher prices | 4, 5 |
| `shop.price.band.under` / `.between` / `.over` | Under {amount} / {from} to {to} / {amount} and over (country pages only) | spec 008 |
| `home.comingUp.more` | and {count} more | 4 |
| `shop.card.localSpecial` | Only in {country} | 8 |
| `nav.mega.flowers`, `.plantsGifts`, `.byPrice`, `.byType`, `.byFlower`, `.byColour`, `.everyDay`, `.forWhom`, `.comingUp`, `.allFlowers`, `.allOccasions`, `.extras`, `.open`, `.soon`, `.allDestinations` | Flowers · Plants & gifts · By price · By type · By flower · By colour · Every day · For whom · Coming up · All flowers · All occasions · Extras you can add · Ordering open · Opening soon · All destinations | 5 |
| `nav.mega.recipient.mum`, `.grandparents`, `.partner`, `.newParents`, `.colleague` | For mum · For grandparents · For your partner · For new parents · For a colleague | 5 |
| `delivery.countdown.tomorrow` | Order within {duration} for delivery tomorrow | 10 |
| `trust.promise.line` | Not fresh on arrival? Send us a photo within 72 hours and we'll replace them or refund you in full. | 11 |
| `trust.photo.short` | Photo at the door | 11 |
| `trust.payment.name` | Secure payment | 11 |

- **Reused, reviewed:** `shop.hub.noMoney` (now on every country-less page, once), `product.destination.label`, `product.destination.change`, `home.trending.heading`, `home.occasions.eyebrow`, `shop.toolbar.howWeSort`, the listing disclosure keys, `catalog.price.from`, `catalog.price.inclusive`, `catalog.price.equivalents`, `media.provenance.aiExample` (text unchanged), item 11's `nav.utility.guarantee`, `home.proof.photo.title`, `home.proof.photo.body` and `footer.payment.processor` (texts unchanged), the existing header labels for Occasions, Sympathy and Destinations, every occasion, category, colour and country label.
- **Changed:** `CategoryChipRow`'s "Also for Poland" key takes `{country}`.
- **Deleted keys** (no renderer left): the home's sentence-picker keys, the `OccasionTiles` and long how-it-works keys, `nav.send` and `home.trending.basis` if unused; each PR lists what it deletes.
- **No concatenation:** one ICU message per line; country names from the registry's translations; `formatMoney`, `formatDate`, `formatDuration`, `Intl.ListFormat`, `Intl.Collator` for country order. Logical properties only; `/ar-XB` mirrors the menu, picker, chooser, tabs, round row and cards.
- **Locale and country are independent:** the language switcher keeps the country in the URL and `fo_dest`; a change of country keeps the locale.

#### Compliance (§8)

- **Price display (CLAUDE.md, `plan/07` §4):** item 2: no price before a country; once a country is known, every price is that country's exact price with VAT and delivery included; equivalents labelled approximate and dated. Price shown = price charged holds without exception.
- **Geo-blocking (EU 2018/302 Art. 3, ADR-0006):** no IP read; every country's pages are reachable from everywhere by URL; the only redirect is the chooser's answer to the visitor's own choice, which Art. 3(2) allows with the customer's explicit consent, given here by the click; the visitor can always change country.
- **The remembered-country cookie (ePrivacy Directive 2002/58/EC Art. 5(3), second subparagraph; UK PECR reg. 6(4)(b)):** `fo_dest` is exempt from consent as storage strictly necessary to provide a service the visitor explicitly requested: seeing prices for the country they chose. The Article 29 Working Party's Opinion 04/2012 on the cookie consent exemption (WP194, adopted 7 June 2012) places user-interface preference cookies of this kind (its example: a language preference) inside the exemption when set on the user's explicit action, and asks for a clear indication when the preference is kept beyond the session; `shop.choose.remember` is that indication, shown at the point of choice (WP194 read through secondary summaries on 2026-10-24; its passage was not fetched, Q-24l). It is set only on the visitor's choice (the URL rule only updates an existing cookie), stores only an ISO code, is first party, and is listed in the cookie register and policy beside `fo_currency`. A-founder-2026-10-19d's "no banner while only essential cookies" holds.
- **Waitlist (GDPR Art. 6(1)(a), 7, 13, 17; UK GDPR; ePrivacy Art. 13 for the launch email):** unticked consent with its text hashed into the row; double opt-in; one launch email then deletion; unsubscribe in every email; RoPA row; DPA with Resend already on file (`docs/compliance/`); no PII in URLs, logs or analytics.
- **Ranking transparency (`plan/07` §2.1):** the home's "Our selection" carries "How we sort" with the listing text.
- **Countdown (UCPD Annex I point 7; DMCCA 2024 Sch. 20):** a real delivery cutoff, never a sale deadline, never reset; hidden when facts are missing or passed.
- **Trust claims (UCPD Art. 6; DMCCA s. 226):** item 11's predicates: each of option 2's three claims shows only while it is true (the promise as spec 041 states it, with no number of days; the delivery photo while ruling D6 makes it part of every order; secure payment only with live Stripe payments, since the sentence names Stripe and card data stays in Stripe's form, spec 013 §8); no review, star or count; no trademark logos.
- **Voice:** no partner or acceptance language; "our florist" kept where it is (founder "trust", 2026-10-24); no negative framing (2026-10-14). The not-available and not-live pages say plainly what is true and offer alternatives.
- **Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA, EAA):** native disclosures, radios, links and forms; visible labels equal accessible names; 1.4.13 by the island; 2.4.11 (an open panel never covers the focused element; `scroll-padding-top` covers the sticky header); 2.5.8 (44 px targets); 2.1.1 with JavaScript on and off; 1.4.3 (equivalents and countdown at 4.5:1); 3.2.2 (no change of context on input: the picker and selector submit only on a button); the countdown is not a live region; axe zero serious or critical.

#### Acceptance criteria

"Today's data": the committed registries (no country open; demo shops stand in) with the default core list (Q-24a). "The open fixture": spec 007 AC-33's (one country open). "The two-open fixture": two countries open, with florist mappings where one non-core product is sold in only one of them and one core product loses its last florist in one of them. "The crawl set": AC-14's. "Country-less pages": item 2's list. JavaScript off unless an AC says "JavaScript on". The founder's eleven rules of 2026-10-24 are AC-75 to AC-91.

- **AC-72 (shop set and core range).** `shopCountries()` equals the demo shop countries with today's data, the one open country with the open fixture, and both with the two-open fixture. `coreRange()` returns the curated list in rank order with today's data, and with the two-open fixture returns it minus exactly the core product that lost its florist; no non-core product is ever in it. `pnpm seed:check` fails, naming the count, on a core list of 7 and of 13. Neither function, nor anything it calls, reads a header, cookie or IP (source scan).
- **AC-73 (no country in code).** The source scan of item 13 finds no destination ISO literal and no country-name literal; `DEMO_DESTINATION_ISO2` and `DATED_DESTINATION` exist nowhere; with a fixture registry that adds a new shop country with prices and florists, the build lists its shop pages, the picker, the chooser and the Destinations panel show it, and `coreRange()` is re-evaluated against it, with no file under `src/` changed.
- **AC-74 (country-less pages show only core products).** With the two-open fixture, every product card on `/en`, every category hub, every occasion hub, every out-of-context featured card and the chooser is a core product in `coreRange()`; no non-core product appears on any country-less page; the local special appears on its country's listings with `shop.card.localSpecial`.
- **AC-75 (no price before a country; rules 1, 4 and 7).** In the served HTML of every country-less page in `en` and `en-gb`, with today's data and with the two-open fixture: every product card's price box holds the resolved `shop.price.choose` and its action line the resolved `shop.cta.chooseCountry`; neither the header nor `<main>` contains `formatMoney` output, a currency symbol or ISO currency code beside digits, `catalog.price.inclusive` or an equivalents line; `shop.hub.noMoney` occurs exactly once; no JSON-LD, `ItemList`, `<meta>`, Open Graph tag or sitemap carries an amount; A-founder-2026-10-14g's AC-53 passes on the served HTML.
- **AC-76 (every priced link goes through the chooser; rule 1).** Over every country-less page in the crawl set, JavaScript off: every `<a href>` or form action whose target is a priced page (item 3's list) is a chooser path `/{locale}/{chooseSegment}/{next}` with a valid `next` and `rel="nofollow"`, except links that name their country (destination rows, the Destinations panel, a guide's shop entry), which point at that country's page; every link to a country-less page (hubs, occasions index, destinations hub, info pages) is a plain link; no chooser href carries a query string.
- **AC-77 (the chooser page; rule 1).** For each accepted `next` shape, `GET /{locale}/{chooseSegment}/{next}` answers 200 with its own document (header, H1 `home.picker.label`, `<main>`), no element with `role="dialog"` or `aria-modal`, the shop-country buttons in `sortWeight` then collation order, the field with a `<datalist>` of every registry country, `shop.choose.remember`, `noindex, nofollow` in meta and `X-Robots-Tag`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, disallowed in `robots.txt` and in no sitemap; it works with JavaScript off; with `fo_dest` set it answers 200 (never 3xx) with `shop.choose.continue` first. `parseChooserNext()` accepts every item 3 shape and rejects `https://x.test`, `//x.test`, `\x`, `%2F%2Fx`, another locale's segment, an unknown shape and a 257-character path, each falling back to the shop root; no `next` or chooser URL in a recorded run contains an email, a name or a phone.
- **AC-78 (the choice; rule 1).** For each shop country and each `next` shape, choosing by button, by typing "Polska", "Poland" or "pl", and by the path form answers **303** to `countryVersionOf(next, country)` (the country page of the same key, the shop root where none exists, the product page, the not-available page for an unsold product, the shop root with `?price=` for a band, spec 045's results with `q` for search) with exactly one `Set-Cookie: fo_dest` (180 days, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure`, `Path=/`); a non-shop registry country answers the not-live page; an unknown name lists matches.
- **AC-79 (history; rule 1).** JavaScript on: from an occasion hub, activating a card then a country lands on the product page in that country, and `history.back()` returns to the occasion hub, not the chooser. JavaScript off: Back from the product page shows the chooser page with status 200 and no redirect.
- **AC-80 (a remembered country fills exact prices after load; rule 2).** JavaScript on, with `fo_dest` naming a shop country, on `/en` and on an occasion hub: the served HTML is byte-identical to a visit without the cookie; after load each core card's price, equivalents and slots equal that country's shop-root card for the same product; every chooser href on the page points at that country's URL (or the path form where the country has no such page); `shop.chosen.line` with the country and `product.destination.change` appear; layout shift from the swap is at most 0.01; `/api/prices/{locale}/{iso2}` is at most 8,192 B Brotli, publicly cacheable and reads no cookie. With JavaScript off nothing changes.
- **AC-81 (the URL wins; rule 3).** With `fo_dest=de` and a visit to Poland's product page, the server HTML shows Poland's exact prices and the selector reads Poland; after load (JavaScript on) `fo_dest` is `pl`. With no `fo_dest`, the same visit leaves `document.cookie` without `fo_dest`. No cached response carries `Set-Cookie`.
- **AC-82 (country-less hubs; rule 4).** Every category hub and occasion hub in `en` and `en-gb` keeps the robots directive, canonical, hreflang cluster and sitemap membership it had on `main` (`indexability()` unchanged); holds the picker with `next` equal to its own key, its core products as AC-75 cards linking through the chooser, no add-to-basket and no price; and links every live country's version of itself directly through its destination rows, each answering 200.
- **AC-83 (not available in the chosen country; rule 5).** Choosing, through the chooser or the selector, a country that does not sell the product renders with 200 and `noindex` `product.unavailable.line`, exactly 3 cards that `closestMatches()` returns (fewer only when fewer core products are sold there), each sold in that country at its exact price, and `product.unavailable.back` to the previous country's page; no 404 is returned on this path.
- **AC-84 (closest matches).** `closestMatches()` over a fixture returns the pinned order (same colour family and same band first, then same colour, then same band, then "Our order", then SKU), only core products saleable in the country, never the product itself, at most 3, and the same list on every run.
- **AC-85 (a country that is not live; rule 6).** Choosing a registry country outside the shop set renders with 200 and `noindex` `destination.notLive.line` with that country, the waitlist form and the chooser's form again; the page holds no product card and no amount; choosing a shop country from it answers AC-78's 303.
- **AC-86 (waitlist).** The checkbox is unticked; submitting without it is refused with a labelled error; a valid submit stores one unconfirmed row and sends one confirmation email whose link carries a token and no email; confirming sets `confirmed_at`; unconfirmed rows are swept after 7 days; no URL, log line or analytics event contains the email (scan of a recorded run); `/admin/coverage` shows confirmed counts per country and no address; the migration applies, rolls back and applies on a throwaway Postgres 16; the rate limit refuses the eleventh submit from one IP in a minute.
- **AC-87 (no basket, search or checkout without a country; rule 7).** No country-less page holds a control or form whose action is the basket, the checkout or an add-to-basket endpoint; the header search form on a country-less page posts (GET) to the chooser with `next=search` and keeps `q`; choosing a country from there lands on spec 045's results for that country with the same `q` once spec 045 is published; a basket or checkout request with a line lacking a country is refused (spec 010 unchanged, re-run).
- **AC-88 (the selector re-prices; rule 8).** On a product page, a country category, a country occasion page and a shop root in country X, choosing country Y in the header selector lands on the same page in Y with Y's exact prices, or the shop root where Y has no such page, or AC-83's page for an unsold product; a basket holding a line for X still holds it, at X's price, after the change, and a Y line can be added beside it.
- **AC-89 (no guessing; rule 9).** A source scan finds no read of `cf-ipcountry`, `x-forwarded-for` for geography, `Accept-Language` or a geolocation API outside the existing locale-hint code (ADR-0006); every chooser, prices-document and country-page response is identical across those headers; switching language on a Poland page keeps Poland, and choosing Germany on `/pl` keeps `/pl`.
- **AC-90 (the cookie's basis; rule 10).** `src/config/cookies.ts` has a `fo_dest` row (essential, purpose `consent.cookies.dest.purpose`, 180 days, first party) and the cookie policy lists it with its basis (ePrivacy Art. 5(3); PECR reg. 6(4)(b)); across a recorded crawl, `Set-Cookie: fo_dest` appears only on the chooser's choice responses, and `document.cookie` gains `fo_dest` only after a choice or, when it already existed, by the URL rule; `shop.choose.remember` is visible on every chooser page and the home picker; no consent banner appears while only essential cookies are set.
- **AC-91 (the same HTML for everyone, bots included; rule 11).** Every cached route this amendment touches, and the chooser page, answers with no `Vary` and no `Set-Cookie` on cached routes, and its body is byte-identical with Googlebot's and a desktop Chrome user agent, and with and without `fo_locale`, `fo_currency`, `fo_consent`, `fo_dest`, `Accept-Language` and `cf-ipcountry` (the chooser differs only by the remembered country's "Continue" button); no code reads the user agent; the build lists the same routes as prebuilt as `main`.
- **AC-92 (the home picker).** On `/en` and `/en-gb` at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844 the picker is inside the first viewport with its label, one button per shop country up to `PICKER_CHIPS` in `sortWeight` then collation order, the search field with its `<datalist>` and `shop.choose.remember`; submitting a country by keyboard lands on that country's shop root.
- **AC-93 (the selector).** At 1440 the header holds the selector after the six items; in context its summary reads `nav.sendingTo` with the URL's country (server HTML); out of context `nav.sendingToChoose`; its panel is the chooser's form with the page's `next`. JavaScript on with `fo_dest` set, a country-less page's summary shows the remembered country after paint with no layout shift.
- **AC-94 (the first screen).** First visit, no cookies, at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844 on `/en` and `/en-gb`: the first product card's photograph top is at most the viewport height minus 160 px; the card is a link; the H1, the picker and spec 043 AC-8's line are in the first viewport; no fixed or sticky overlay intersects the first card; exactly one `priority` image per viewport class with non-overlapping preload `media`; initial image transfer at 390 is at most 307,200 B; CI Lighthouse LCP under 2,000 ms and CLS under 0.05.
- **AC-95 (the whole core range on the home).** With today's data on `/en` and `/en-gb`, default state, after scrolling to the end at both sizes: every product of `coreRange()` is displayed at least once as an AC-75 card whose chooser link answers 200, the number of distinct products displayed equals `coreRange()`'s size, and no other product appears.
- **AC-96 (the home's sections).** `<main>` on `/en` holds, in DOM order: the first screen; "Our selection" (8 cards in "Our order", "How we sort" closed, a "See all" chooser link); "Shop by occasion" (a tab per qualifying `HOME_OCCASION_TABS` key, up to 4 cards and a plain hub link per panel); "Shop by price" (three word-label chooser links with `next=price/{band}` and no amount); "Coming up" (3 occasions in date order with dates, country names, hub links and up to 3 cards each). In sections 3, 4 and 6 the last element before "See all" is the product list. The sentence picker is absent.
- **AC-97 (the tabs).** JavaScript off: the first tab is checked and only its panel's cards display; the other panels are in the HTML with `display: none`; click, or arrow keys on the focused group, display only the chosen panel with no request; accessible names as item 4.4; no `<script>` belongs to the section.
- **AC-98 (dedupe).** Unit: a product shown in "Our selection" is skipped by a later section while it can fill its slots, repeated when it cannot; the same on every run.
- **AC-99 (the desktop menu).** At 1440 on every page of the crawl set: six `<details name="fo-mega">` with the resolved labels in order, closed in the server HTML; each opens by click, Enter and Space, then Tab reaches its entries; closed entries are not focusable; Flowers, Occasions, Sympathy and Plants & gifts each have one featured card with photograph, name, the country-less price box and a chooser link (in context: the country's exact price and a direct link); the selector, search, account and basket follow in that order where published; no "Send flowers" pill.
- **AC-100 (the menu model).** `megaMenuModel("en", null)` with today's data equals a pinned table built from the committed data; removing an entry's page in a fixture removes the entry; every href answers 200 over the crawl set; hub entries are plain links and shop entries are chooser links; no entry is an `<a href>` with a query string; "For whom" lists `RECIPIENT_GROUPS`' entries whose hub exists; "Coming up" lists the next 5 dated occasions by earliest date across shop countries.
- **AC-101 (hover and dismissal, JavaScript on).** At 1440 with a fine pointer: 200 ms hover opens a panel and closes others; moving into the panel keeps it open; leaving closes it within 500 ms; Esc closes it and focuses its summary; Tab past the last entry or a click outside closes it; reduced motion has no transition; axe zero serious or critical with a panel open; the focused element is never covered.
- **AC-102 (no shift, no early images).** Toggling each panel records no layout shift outside the header; no panel image is requested on load; opening Flowers requests exactly its featured image; each panel image is at most 384 px wide and 25,600 B.
- **AC-103 (bytes).** Header plus Menu outer HTML, Brotli, at most 6,144 B on `/en` and on a country listing; `budget:client-js` reports every route at most `main` plus 2,560 B and inside A1's ceilings.
- **AC-104 (the phone).** At 390 × 844: the row holds logo, search, account, basket (where published) and Menu; the opened Menu starts with the selector, then six `<details>` whose entries equal the desktop panels' on that page, with no `<img>`; on `/en` the round row follows the header with item 4's hrefs, each image at most 6,144 B, `fetchpriority="low"`, none the LCP; with JavaScript off the Menu and accordions work.
- **AC-105 (equivalents everywhere a price is).** With rates inside the display bound, on a product page, a country listing card, an in-context featured card, a not-available card and a card filled after load, in `en` and `en-gb`: one line under the price holds exactly the three other currencies of {GBP, EUR, USD, PLN}, each the mid-rate conversion at the named date, half-up, in item 7's order; the displayed currency is absent from it; the price's font size is larger; the line meets 4.5:1; no JSON-LD, `<meta>`, `ItemList`, sitemap, quote or checkout contains an equivalent; no country-less card in the served HTML has one.
- **AC-106 (the display bound).** `displayRateFor()` returns a rate through the end of day `as_of + 14` and `null` after; inside 48 hours its amounts equal `fxRateFor()`'s; `fxRateFor()`, `MAX_FX_AGE_HOURS` and the stale fallback are unchanged (spec 005 AC-35's tests unedited and green); `fxReasonKey` hides the line.
- **AC-107 (home grids).** In each home section the example label follows A-founder-2026-10-19b clause 3 with its reviewed text; every `ai` card keeps its label in the HTML; each section's `ItemList` equals its displayed cards by count, order, name and URL, and carries no price.
- **AC-108 (click targets).** With the open fixture on CI's build, first visit: from `/en` at 1440 and 390, the first card then one country loads a product page with its price; one activation of its primary action loads checkout step 1 with a date selected; at 390 with payment mocked, a scripted purchase from `/en` completes in at most 7 activations, typing excluded. With `fo_dest` set and JavaScript on, each count is one lower. With today's data the same path reaches the product page and the demo checkout.
- **AC-109 (countdown, server facts).** With the open fixture and a fixed clock, each priced card on a country page and the product page's price block carry `data-fo-slots` equal to `deliveryWindow()`'s next two slots for that product and country, and the prices document carries the same for each core product; the line is present and `hidden`; the HTML is byte-identical across cookies and headers; no country-less card in the served HTML carries `data-fo-slots`. With today's data no element carries `data-fo-slots` and no page holds cutoff or order-by text (AC-34 re-run).
- **AC-110 (countdown, shown only when true; JavaScript on).** With browser clock and time zone controlled: at 10:00 in the country with a 14:00 cutoff for tomorrow the line reads the resolved key with `formatDuration`'s "4 hr" (`en`); a minute after the cutoff it is hidden; with the next slot the day after tomorrow, hidden; with a time zone where the country's tomorrow is not the visitor's, hidden; JavaScript off, hidden; showing it shifts nothing; no `aria-live`; one timer of 60 s or more per page.
- **AC-111 (trust bar honesty).** The pick is recorded (option 2, founder "2", item 11); options 1 and 3 render nowhere (no trust row under the header, no trust band above the footer). No review claim with 24 verified reviews; with 25 the review place stays unbuilt until spec 022 amends this item (rule 2); the delivery-photo claim only under `deliveryPhotoRequired()` (AC-116); no trust key in any locale contains florist, partner, relay, network or accept; every claim is in the server HTML.
- **AC-112 (copy).** Against `main`, `messages/en.json` gains exactly the i18n table's keys the PR renders and loses exactly the keys it lists; each new or changed key outside the `email` namespace is `reviewed: true` by the founder at the PR's head; `i18n:check` reports `en-gb` inside spec 003 AC-24's line, before and after printed.
- **AC-113 (coverage and go-live).** `/admin/coverage` lists the core range first in rank order, then every other active product, against every shop country and florist country; each cell equals the count of active linked florists; every core cell under 2 carries the warning text, and a core cell at 0 in a shop country says the product is hidden from country-less pages; the core rows equal `coreRange()` plus the hidden ones; a user without `catalogue.read` gets 403. `goLivePreflight` for a candidate country lists each core product's florist count there, fails with any 0, warns with any 1, passes otherwise; a typed-code flip past a failure records the override in the audit trail.
- **AC-114 (trust points on the product page; option 2).** On a product page in `en` and `en-gb`, with the open fixture (live picker) and with today's data (not-open state), JavaScript off: one `<ul>` follows the primary action ("Continue") as its next sibling in the buy column (in the not-open state, the state line), and holds, in order, the items whose predicate is true, each an `aria-hidden` inline SVG, the bold name and the sentence of item 11's table, resolved from those keys; it adds no heading element; at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844 its top edge is within 24 px below the action's bottom edge and no other element sits between them; nothing of it appears in JSON-LD, `<meta>` or Open Graph; no trust list appears in the header, notice bar or footer of any page in the crawl set; CI Lighthouse CLS stays under 0.05 and the LCP element is unchanged from `main`.
- **AC-115 (trust row on the home; option 2).** On `/en` and `/en-gb` at 1440 × 900 and 390 × 844, JavaScript off: one `<ul>` inside the hero card follows the picker's submit button and `shop.choose.remember`, holding, in order, the names only (`nav.utility.guarantee`, `trust.photo.short`, `trust.payment.name`) of the items whose predicate is true, with no sentence; it is inside the first viewport; AC-94's first-card bound and AC-92 still pass; no other trust list exists on the page.
- **AC-116 (trust honesty conditions).** (a) With `infoPageExists` true for the guarantee page, the promise name on both pages is an `<a>` to that page's URL answering 200; with it false, the name is plain text with no `<a>`, no `role="link"` and no link styling. (b) With the transition table as committed (no `delivered ▶ closed` arrow without a photo, spec 002 D6) `deliveryPhotoRequired()` is true and the photo item renders; with a fixture table that adds a closing arrow without a photo it is false and the item is absent with no gap (the list holds two items). (c) The payment item renders only with `payments.stripe` on **and** live Stripe mode; with the flag off, with the flag on in test mode, and with only the backup processor configured, it is absent from both pages with no gap; turning the flag off purges the tags of every page carrying the bar, after which a fresh fetch of the product page and the home has no "Secure payment". (d) The served HTML is byte-identical across `fo_dest`, `fo_locale`, `fo_currency`, `Accept-Language`, `cf-ipcountry` and user agent (AC-91), and the bar adds no script chunk, no network request and no image request.

#### Test cases

| ID | Layer | Given / When / Then | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-78 | unit | Registry, mapping and core-list fixtures (today, open, two-open); the 7 and 13 lists through `seed:check`; the source scan. Red with a demo mapping counted once a country is live, red with `coreRange` ignoring one country's coverage, red with the core list derived from mappings instead of read from `core_rank` | AC-72 |
| T-79 | unit + build | The literal scan; the new-country fixture build and its diff of `src/` (empty). Red with "PL" restored in `OccasionDates.tsx` | AC-73 |
| T-80 | e2e (built site, fixture) | SKUs on every country-less page and the chooser against `coreRange()`; the special's absence and its label on its country listing. Red with the hub reading all products | AC-74 |
